# Introducing Chatnext

Chatnext is the revolutionary AI-powered assistant designed exclusively for Frappe and ERPNext users. With a deep understanding of the Frappe framework and ERPNext modules, Chatnext enhances your user experience, streamlines your workflow, and helps you unlock the full potential of your ERP system.

As a copilot for ERPNext, Chatnext's primary goal is to assist you in managing your business more efficiently by answering your queries, providing relevant information, and offering insights on various aspects of Frappe and ERPNext. Say goodbye to hours spent on searching through documentation or waiting for support responses – Chatnext is your one-stop solution.

## Key Features

- Instant Query Resolution: Have a question related to Frappe or ERPNext? Just ask Chatnext. It intelligently understands and responds to your queries in real-time, providing you with accurate and relevant information.
- In-Depth Knowledge: Chatnext is equipped with a comprehensive understanding of various ERPNext modules, including accounting, HR, sales, purchase, inventory, CRM, and more. It's like having a dedicated expert by your side, 24/7.
- Contextual Assistance: Chatnext doesn't just provide generic responses. It takes into account your specific use case, offering customized solutions that cater to your unique business requirements.
- Proactive Assistance: Chatnext's advanced AI capabilities enable it to anticipate potential challenges and offer proactive solutions, helping you to tackle issues before they even arise.
- Continuous Improvement: Chatnext learns from every interaction, evolving its knowledge base and refining its responses over time. This ensures that you always receive the most up-to-date and relevant information.
- Easy Integration: Chatnext seamlessly integrates with your existing Frappe and ERPNext setup, allowing you to access its powerful features without any hassle or disruption to your workflow.

## Future Roadmap

As Chatnext continues to grow and evolve, our vision is to transform it into a full-fledged copilot for ERPNext, further enhancing its capabilities and offering even more value to users. Some of the planned features include:

- Workflow Automation: Chatnext will be able to automate various routine tasks, saving you time and minimizing human error.
- Advanced Analytics: Harness the power of AI-driven analytics to gain deeper insights into your business data and make better-informed decisions.
- Voice Command Support: Experience hands-free operation of your ERP system by interacting with Chatnext through voice commands.
- Third-Party Integrations: Chatnext will support integration with other popular business applications, providing a unified and seamless experience across platforms.

Don't miss out on the opportunity to revolutionize your ERPNext experience. Download Chatnext today and discover the future of intelligent business management!

Hybrowlabs Technologies

# Code Style Guide for ERPNext and Frappe Project
## User-Facing Messages Must Be Translated
All user-facing strings/text must be wrapped in the __() function in JavaScript and _() function in Python so that they are translated for the user.
## Breaking Functions/Methods Longer Than 10 Lines
Long functions are hard to read and debug. A function should do one thing and only one thing. If a function exceeds 10 lines, break it into smaller parts or consider using a class.
### Signs Your Function is Doing Too Much:

More than one level of indentation (indicates too many logic branches)
Whitespace between logical groups of code (suggests multiple responsibilities)

## Code Indentation

Use tabs, not spaces.
Ensure indentation is consistent in both JavaScript and Python.
Multi-line strings or expressions must be consistently indented.

## SQL Queries
### Preferred SQL Formatting
frappe.db.sql(
    """SELECT
        item_name, description, default_warehouse
    FROM
        tabItem
    WHERE
        disabled = 0"""
)

For query builder (frappe.qb), use Black's formatting:
result = (
    frappe.qb.from_(doctype)
    .select(doctype.name)
    .where(doctype.autoname.like(Concat(prefix, ".%")) &amp; doctype.name != name)
).run()

### SQL Injection Prevention
Do not use .format() for string replacement:
# ❌ WRONG:
frappe.db.sql("SELECT age FROM tabUser WHERE name='{}'".format(user))

# ✅ CORRECT:
frappe.db.sql("SELECT age FROM tabUser WHERE name=%s", user)

## Simple Structures

Avoid complex one-liners with multiple AND or OR conditions.
Break them into multiple lines for readability.

## Function Sequence
The calling function should be at the top, and called functions should be below:
def fa():
    fb()
    fc()

def fb():
    pass

def fc():
    pass

## Business Logic Must Be API-Friendly (Implemented on Server Side)

Any business logic (calculations, value settings) must be implemented server-side (Python) and client-side (JS).
This ensures that when documents are posted via REST API, the logic is executed correctly.

## Commit Messages
Commit messages must follow [Conventional Commit](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/en/v1.0.0-beta.2/) standards.
## Code Comments

Code should be self-explanatory, but add comments explaining why a method is implemented in a particular way.
Avoid excessive comments, but document non-trivial logic.

## Avoid Deprecated APIs
Use the latest recommended methods. Avoid:

$c_obj()
cur_frm
get_query
add_fetch

## Tabs, Not Spaces
ERPNext and Frappe projects use tabs, not spaces. No exceptions.

## General Rules
no print statements where unnecessary, no console.log in JS where unnecessary
make sure there are no spelling errors in the variable and doctype names

# Documentation Update Instructions for AI Assistant

## ⚠️ CRITICAL UPDATE PRINCIPLES (MUST FOLLOW)

### 1. **ALWAYS PRESERVE EXISTING CONTENT**
- **NEVER delete or rewrite entire documentation files**
- **ALWAYS read the existing documentation first**
- **ONLY remove content that is factually wrong or misleading**
- **PRESERVE all useful examples, use cases, and explanations**

### 2. **MINIMAL CHANGE APPROACH**
- **Make the smallest possible changes to fix issues**
- **Add missing information without removing existing content**
- **Update only the specific sections that need correction**
- **Keep the original structure and formatting intact**

### 3. **VERIFICATION BEFORE CHANGES**
Before making ANY documentation changes:
1. ✅ Read the complete existing documentation
2. ✅ Check the actual implementation in Python files
3. ✅ Verify configuration in funnel_node.json
4. ✅ Identify ONLY what is wrong or missing
5. ✅ Plan minimal changes needed

### 4. **DOCUMENTATION UPDATE WORKFLOW**
```
1. READ existing documentation →
2. COMPARE with implementation →
3. IDENTIFY issues (wrong info OR missing info) →
4. APPLY minimal fixes →
5. PRESERVE everything else
```

## 🎯 GOLDEN RULE
**"If it's not broken or missing, don't change it."**

When updating documentation:
- ✅ **DO**: Fix factual errors, add missing required fields
- ❌ **DON'T**: Rewrite, restructure, or remove valid content
- 🔧 **USE**: Edit tool for targeted fixes (not Write tool for rewrites)
- 📊 **RESULT**: Minimal diff, maximum preservation

## Purpose
This file contains instructions for updating NextAI Funnel node documentation. **Follow these instructions exactly to ensure documentation quality.**

## Quick Reference
- **Total Nodes**: 85 documented nodes
- **Source of Truth**: `apps/nextai/nextai/fixtures/funnel_node.json`
- **Documentation Location**: `apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/`

## Implementation File Locations

**Python implementations by node type:**
- **Triggers**: `apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/doctype/funnel_task/triggers/[node_name].py`
- **Actions**: `apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/doctype/funnel_task/actions/[node_name].py`
- **Awaiting Actions**: `apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/doctype/funnel_task/awaiting_actions/[node_name].py`

**Note**: Some trigger nodes have different naming (e.g., `cron_node` → `on_cron_trigger.py`)

## How to Check for Updates

**Use the helper script:**
```bash
# Check all nodes
python3 apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/check_documentation_updates.py

# Check specific node
python3 apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/check_documentation_updates.py [node_name]
```

**Manual check:**
Compare timestamps between `funnel_node.json` "modified" field and the first line `` in documentation

## Documentation Structure

### Individual Node Documentation (`/nodes/[node_name].md`)
Each node documentation file should contain:

1. **HTML Comment** (First line): `` for easy comparison
2. **Title** (H1): Human-readable node label
3. **Overview**: Brief description of what the node does
4. **Category and Type**:
   - Category: From funnel_node.json
   - Type: Trigger/Action/Awaiting Action
   - Element Type: From funnel_node.json
5. **Configuration Fields**: Extract from `form_data` JSON in funnel_node.json
6. **Variables Usage**:
   - Input variables the node accepts
   - Output variables the node creates (from `created_variables` field)
7. **Common Use Cases**: 3-4 practical examples
8. **Best Practices** (optional): Tips for using the node effectively
9. **Technical Notes** (optional): Implementation details
10. **Last Updated**: Formatted timestamp

### Date Format
Always format dates as: `**Last Updated:** [Month Day, Year at HH:MM:SS AM/PM]`
Example: `**Last Updated:** January 23, 2025 at 05:14:27 PM`

**Important**: Include seconds in the timestamp to ensure exact matching with funnel_node.json timestamps. This prevents false positives when checking for needed updates.

## What to UPDATE vs What to PRESERVE

### ❌ DO NOT REMOVE (Unless Factually Wrong):
- Working examples and use cases
- Best practices and tips
- Technical notes that are still valid
- Related nodes sections
- Common patterns and workflows
- Troubleshooting information
- Performance considerations
- Security notes

### ✅ DO UPDATE/ADD:
- Wrong field names or types
- Missing required fields
- Incorrect configuration details
- Missing branching/output handles documentation
- Wrong technical implementation details
- New features or capabilities
- Updated timestamp

### 📝 Example of GOOD Update:
```markdown
BEFORE: "This node uses JavaScript expressions..."
AFTER:  "This node uses Python expressions..."
(Only the wrong word changed, rest preserved)
```

### 📝 Example of BAD Update:
```markdown
BEFORE: [200 lines of documentation with 1 wrong field]
AFTER:  [Completely rewritten 150 lines]
(Entire documentation replaced for one small fix)
```

## Step-by-Step Update Process

### 1. Identify Changed Nodes
```bash
# Compare timestamps between funnel_node.json and documentation
# Look for nodes where funnel_node.json modified > doc Last Updated
```

### 1.5. Understand Before Documenting

**Check the implementation:**
1. Read the Python implementation file (see locations above)
2. Check `funnel_node.json` for node configuration
3. Identify required fields from `node_data["field_name"]` in code
4. Note variables created/modified in the implementation
5. Check for error handling and validations

**NEVER document without understanding the actual implementation**

### 2. Extract and Update Information

**From funnel_node.json, check:**
- `form_data`: Extract configuration fields
- `created_variables`: Variables the node outputs
- `element_type`: Trigger/Action/Awaiting Action
- `handles`: Check for branching (multiple source handles)
- `modified`: Timestamp for updates

**Update only what's needed:**
- ❌ Wrong field names or types
- ➕ Missing required fields
- ➕ Missing output handles documentation
- ✏️ Incorrect technical details


## Important Notes

1. **Variable Names**: Use `{{variable}}` syntax in documentation
2. **Field Types**: Map FormIO types correctly (textfield, textarea, checkbox, select, number)
3. **Always use Edit/MultiEdit tools** for targeted changes, never Write tool for updates



## Minimal Change Principle - IMPORTANT

### Before Making ANY Change:
1. **Ask yourself**: Is this change absolutely necessary?
2. **Can I fix the issue** with a smaller change?
3. **Am I preserving** all valid existing content?
4. **Would adding be better** than replacing?

### Change Priority Order:
1. **Fix wrong information** (highest priority - safety/correctness)
2. **Add missing required fields** (functionality critical)
3. **Add missing optional fields** (only if important for usage)
4. **Update formatting** (lowest priority - only if broken)

### Documentation Change Checklist:
```
□ Read entire existing documentation
□ Identified specific wrong/missing items
□ Planned minimal changes needed
□ Preserved all valid content
□ Only updated what was necessary
□ Verified changes against implementation
□ Kept original structure intact
□ Maintained consistent formatting
```

## Final Checklist Before Saving

✅ Did I read the existing documentation completely?
✅ Did I identify only what's wrong or missing?
✅ Did I make minimal changes?
✅ Did I preserve all valid content?
✅ Did I use Edit/MultiEdit tools (not Write)?
✅ Did I verify against implementation?

---

## Appendix: Common Field Examples

**Only use these as reference, not as templates:**

```markdown
# Example required field:
### Field Name
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Key**: `field_key`
- **Description**: What this field does
- **Default**: Default value if any

# Example branching node:
## Output Handles
This node has two output handles for branching:
- **Yes Handle** (bottom): Continues if condition passes
- **No Handle** (right): Continues if condition fails
```

## Common Workflow Patterns

### Customer Support Workflow
1. WhatsApp/Email Trigger → AI Intent Detection
2. Route based on intent → Collect information
3. Create support ticket → Send confirmation
4. Notify agent → Track resolution

### Lead Generation Workflow
1. Public trigger or form → Collect contact info
2. Validate and enrich data → Score lead
3. Route to CRM → Assign to sales
4. Send welcome email → Schedule follow-up

### Document Processing Workflow
1. Document event trigger → Extract data
2. Validate information → Transform data
3. Update related documents → Generate reports
4. Send notifications → Archive

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/README.md
# Funnel Workflow Builder Documentation

## Overview

Funnel is a comprehensive workflow builder system for NextAI that enables visual creation and automation of complex business processes. It provides a node-based interface for building workflows with various triggers, actions, and conditional logic.

## Table of Contents

1. [Architecture Overview](#architecture-overview)
2. [Core Components](#core-components)
3. [Node Types](#node-types)
4. [Workflow Execution](#workflow-execution)
5. [Node Categories](#node-categories)
6. [API Reference](#api-reference)

## Architecture Overview

The Funnel system consists of several key components:

- **Funnel Workflow**: The main container for a workflow instance with variables and execution status
- **Funnel Task**: Individual task execution units that process nodes
- **Funnel Node**: Node definitions with configurations and FormIO schemas
- **Funnel Definition**: Workflow definitions and configurations
- **Funnel Published**: Published versions of workflows ready for execution

### System Flow

```
Trigger → Create Workflow → Create Tasks → Execute Actions → Handle Results
```

## Core Components

### 1. Funnel Workflow

The workflow container that manages:
- **Variables**: JSON storage for workflow-wide variables
- **Status**: running, finished, stopped, or failed
- **Priority**: Execution priority (0-2, with 0 being highest)
- **Funnel**: Link to published funnel configuration

### 2. Funnel Task

Individual execution units that:
- Process specific nodes in the workflow
- Track execution status (pending, executing, completed, failed, cancelled)
- Maintain parent-child relationships for nested tasks
- Store execution results and error information

### 3. Node Execution Flow

1. Task retrieves node configuration from `Funnel Node`
2. Executes node logic based on type (trigger, action, awaiting_action)
3. Updates workflow variables
4. Creates child tasks for connected nodes
5. Updates task status

## Node Types

### 1. Triggers
Starting points for workflows that respond to events:

- **Manual Trigger** (`manual_trigger`): User-initiated workflow execution
- **Document Event Trigger** (`document_event_trigger`): Responds to Frappe document events
- **Cron Trigger** (`cron_node`): Time-based scheduled execution
- **Email Trigger** (`email_trigger`): Triggered by incoming emails
- **WhatsApp Trigger** (`whatsapp_trigger`): Responds to WhatsApp messages
- **Personal WhatsApp Trigger** (`personal_whatsapp_trigger`): Personal WhatsApp account integration
- **ChatNext Assistant Trigger** (`chatnext_assistant_trigger`): ChatNext AI assistant events
- **Submit Assistant Trigger** (`on_submit_assistant_trigger`): Form submission events

### 2. Actions
Immediate execution nodes that perform operations:

- **Document Operations**
  - `create_doctype`: Create new documents
  - `update_doctype`: Update existing documents
  - `copy_doc_fields`: Copy fields between documents
  - `add_tags_to_doc`: Add tags to documents
  - `add_comment`: Add comments to documents
  - `assign_user`: Assign users to documents

- **Communication**
  - `send_mail`: Send email messages
  - `send_bulk_mail`: Send bulk emails
  - `reply_to_mail`: Reply to email threads
  - `send_notification`: Send system notifications

- **WhatsApp Integration**
  - `send_message_whatsapp`: Send text messages
  - `send_template_whatsapp`: Send template messages
  - `send_image_message_whatsapp`: Send images
  - `send_video_message_whatsapp`: Send videos
  - `send_audio_message_whatsapp`: Send audio
  - `send_file_message_whatsapp`: Send files

- **AI Integration**
  - `chatgpt_message`: Single ChatGPT prompt
  - `chatgpt_multi_prompt`: Multiple ChatGPT prompts
  - `text_intent_detection`: Detect text intent

- **Utility Actions**
  - `exec_python`: Execute Python code
  - `frappe_exec_dotted_path`: Execute Frappe functions
  - `call_api`: Make HTTP API calls
  - `file_to_ocr`: OCR file processing
  - `create_print_file`: Generate print files
  - `split_array`: Split array variables
  - `var_filter`: Filter variables

- **Meeting Integration**
  - `create_zoom_meet_link`: Create Zoom meetings
  - `create_google_meet_link`: Create Google Meet links

### 3. Awaiting Actions
Actions that execute then wait for events:

- **User Input**
  - `chatnext_assistant_text_question`: Wait for text input
  - `chatnext_assistant_question_single_choice`: Single choice selection
  - `chatnext_assistant_form_question`: Form submission
  - `chatnext_assistant_multi_actions`: Multiple action choices
  - `chatnext_assistant_document_approval`: Document approval

- **WhatsApp Questions**
  - `whatsapp_question_ai_qa_agent`: AI-powered Q&A
  - `whatsapp_question_boolean`: Yes/No questions
  - `whatsapp_question_email`: Email collection
  - `whatsapp_question_mobile`: Mobile number collection
  - `whatsapp_question_single_choice`: Choice selection
  - `whatsapp_question_image`: Image collection
  - `whatsapp_question_video`: Video collection
  - `whatsapp_question_audio`: Audio collection
  - `whatsapp_question_file`: File collection

- **Email Awaiting**
  - `on_email_reply`: Wait for email reply
  - `on_email_seen`: Wait for email read receipt

- **Time-Based**
  - `wait_node`: Simple wait
  - `wait_till_date`: Wait until specific date
  - `wait_for_nodes_to_complete`: Wait for parallel nodes

## Workflow Execution

### Execution Process

1. **Trigger Activation**: Workflow starts from a trigger node
2. **Task Creation**: System creates tasks for connected action nodes
3. **Task Execution**: Tasks execute in order based on priority
4. **Variable Management**: Variables updated and passed between nodes
5. **Error Handling**: Errors caught and error flow executed if defined
6. **Completion**: Workflow marked as finished when all tasks complete

### Task States

- `PENDING`: Task created but not started
- `EXECUTING`: Currently being processed
- `COMPLETED`: Successfully finished
- `FAILED`: Encountered error
- `CANCELLED`: Manually or programmatically cancelled

### Error Handling

Nodes can define error handlers using the `ON_ERROR_HANDLE_NAME` connection. When a node fails:
1. Error captured in variables
2. Error flow tasks created
3. Original error re-raised for logging

## Node Categories

### 1. ChatNext Assistant Nodes
Specialized nodes for ChatNext AI assistant interactions:
- Message display
- Question collection
- Document approval workflows
- UI actions
- Modal management

### 2. Personal WhatsApp Nodes
Personal WhatsApp account automation:
- Group management
- Session selection
- Media sending
- Poll creation
- Member management

### 3. Control Flow Nodes
- `stop_current_workflow`: Stop workflow execution
- `trigger_funnel`: Trigger another funnel
- `on_funnel_trigger`: Respond to funnel triggers
- `section_label`: Visual organization
- `text_label`: Documentation labels

## API Reference

### Key Functions

#### `execute_task(task)`
Main task execution function that:
- Validates task status
- Retrieves node configuration
- Executes node logic
- Updates workflow variables
- Creates child tasks
- Handles errors

#### `create_new_task(workflow, target_node_id, source_node_id, parent_task, task_info)`
Creates new tasks in the workflow execution queue.

#### `update_task_status(task, status, info)`
Updates task execution status and stores results.

#### `queue_execute_pending_tasks(at_front=False)`
Queues pending tasks for execution with rate limiting.

### Variable System

Variables are stored as JSON and support:
- Document references (`doc`)
- Event information (`event`)
- User context (`trigger_user_id`, `execute_current_workflow_as_user`)
- Custom data from node outputs (`variable_path_for_output`)
- Error information (`error`)

### FormIO Integration

Nodes use FormIO JSON schemas for configuration UIs, stored in `funnel_node.json`. These schemas define:
- Input fields
- Validation rules
- Default values
- UI components

## Best Practices

1. **Error Handling**: Always define error flows for critical paths
2. **Variable Naming**: Use descriptive variable names for clarity
3. **Priority Management**: Use appropriate priorities (0=high, 2=low)
4. **Testing**: Test workflows in isolation before production
5. **Documentation**: Use text_label nodes for workflow documentation
6. **Security**: Use proper user context for node execution

## Advanced Features

### Custom Nodes
Create custom nodes by:
1. Setting `is_custom: true` in Funnel Node
2. Implementing Python function at `python_function_dotted_path`
3. Defining FormIO schema for configuration

### Parallel Execution
Nodes can create multiple child tasks for parallel execution. Use `wait_for_nodes_to_complete` to synchronize.

### Dynamic Node Execution
Use `chatnext_dynamic_multi_actions` for runtime-determined action selection based on variables.

## Debugging

### Logging
- Task execution logs stored in `Funnel Task` documents
- Manual execution logs in `Funnel Manual Execution Logs`
- Error traces include full stack traces

### Monitoring
- Check workflow status in `Funnel Workflow`
- View task execution states
- Monitor execution delays in task info

## Security Considerations

1. **User Context**: Nodes can execute as specific users
2. **Permission Checks**: `can_execute` validation for workflows
3. **Funnel User**: Special system user for workflow execution
4. **Access Control**: Role-based permissions (Funnel Admin, System Manager)

# Funnel Action Nodes Documentation

## Overview

Action nodes are immediate execution nodes that perform operations without waiting for external events. They execute synchronously and pass control to the next connected nodes upon completion.

## Action Categories

### 1. Document Operations

#### `create_doctype`
**Purpose**: Create new Frappe documents

**Location**: `actions/create_doctype.py`

**Configuration**:
- `docTypeJson`: JSON string with document data
- Supports variable substitution

**Function**: `create_doctype(action_node, variables)`

**Returns**:
```python
{
    "result": new_doc.as_dict()
}
```

**Example Configuration**:
```json
{
    "docTypeJson": "{\"doctype\": \"Task\", \"subject\": \"{{task_subject}}\", \"description\": \"{{task_description}}\"}"
}
```

---

#### `update_doctype`
**Purpose**: Update existing Frappe documents

**Location**: `actions/update_doctype.py`

**Configuration**:
- `doctype`: Document type
- `docname`: Document name (supports variables)
- `updates`: JSON with field updates

**Features**:
- Partial updates
- Variable substitution
- Permission checks

---

#### `copy_doc_fields`
**Purpose**: Copy fields between documents

**Location**: `actions/copy_doc_fields.py`

**Configuration**:
- `source_doctype`: Source document type
- `source_name`: Source document name
- `target_doctype`: Target document type
- `target_name`: Target document name
- `field_mappings`: Field mapping configuration

---

#### `data_to_doc`
**Purpose**: Transform data into document

**Location**: `actions/data_to_doc.py`

**Features**:
- Data transformation
- Field mapping
- Validation
- Bulk creation support

---

#### `add_tags_to_doc`
**Purpose**: Add tags to documents

**Location**: `actions/add_tags_to_doc.py`

**Configuration**:
- `doctype`: Document type
- `docname`: Document name
- `tags`: Comma-separated tags or array

---

#### `add_comment`
**Purpose**: Add comments to documents

**Location**: `actions/add_comment.py`

**Configuration**:
- `doctype`: Document type
- `docname`: Document name
- `comment_type`: Comment, Like, Info, etc.
- `content`: Comment content

---

#### `assign_user`
**Purpose**: Assign users to documents

**Location**: `actions/assign_user.py`

## Action Return Format

All actions follow a standard return format:

```python
{
    "result": ,  # Main result data
    "source_handle_id": ,  # Optional: specific output handle
    "skip_task_creation": False,  # Skip creating child tasks
    "skip_task_update": False,  # Skip updating task status
    "mark_as_cancelled": False,  # Mark task as cancelled
    "task_info": {},  # Additional task information
}
```

## Error Handling

Actions can define error handlers:

1. **Try-Catch**: Internal error handling
2. **Error Output**: Connect error handle for error flow
3. **Validation**: Input validation before execution

Example:
```python
def action(action_node, variables):
    try:
        # Action logic
        return {"result": success_data}
    except Exception as e:
        # Log error
        raise  # Re-raise for error flow
```

## Best Practices

1. **Variable Usage**: Always use `format_with_variables()` for dynamic content
2. **Validation**: Validate inputs before processing
3. **Idempotency**: Make actions idempotent where possible
4. **Logging**: Log important operations
5. **Performance**: Consider async for heavy operations
6. **Security**: Sanitize user inputs
7. **Error Messages**: Provide clear error messages

## Performance Considerations

1. **Batch Operations**: Use bulk operations when possible
2. **Caching**: Cache frequently used data
3. **Async Processing**: Use queues for time-consuming tasks
4. **Database Queries**: Optimize queries and use indexes
5. **API Calls**: Implement rate limiting and retries

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/awaiting_actions.md
# Funnel Awaiting Action Nodes Documentation

## Overview

Awaiting Action nodes are special nodes that execute an action and then wait for an external event or user input before proceeding. They create a pause point in the workflow until specific conditions are met.

## Architecture

Awaiting actions follow a two-phase execution pattern:

1. **Initial Execution**: Perform the action (e.g., send a question)
2. **Wait Phase**: Pause workflow until response/event occurs
3. **Resume**: Continue workflow with received data

## Awaiting Action Categories

### 1. User Input Actions

#### `chatnext_assistant_text_question`
**Purpose**: Ask for text input from user

**Location**: `awaiting_actions/chatnext_assistant_text_question.py`

**Configuration**:
- `question`: Question text
- `placeholder`: Input placeholder
- `validation`: Input validation rules
- `required`: Is response required
- `timeout`: Response timeout

**Wait For**: User text input

**Returns**:
```python
{
    "result": {
        "answer": user_input,
        "answered_at": timestamp
    }
}
```

---

#### `chatnext_assistant_question_single_choice`
**Purpose**: Present single-choice question

**Location**: `awaiting_actions/chatnext_assistant_question_single_choice.py`

**Configuration**:
- `question`: Question text
- `options`: Choice options array
- `default`: Default selection
- `required`: Is selection required

**Options Format**:
```json
[
    {"label": "Option 1", "value": "opt1"},
    {"label": "Option 2", "value": "opt2"}
]
```

**Wait For**: User selection

---

#### `chatnext_assistant_form_question`
**Purpose**: Display form for data collection

**Location**: `awaiting_actions/chatnext_assistant_form_question.py`

**Configuration**:
- `form_schema`: FormIO schema
- `title`: Form title
- `description`: Form description
- `validation`: Form validation rules

**Features**:
- Complex form layouts
- Field validation
- File uploads
- Conditional fields

**Wait For**: Form submission

---

#### `chatnext_assistant_multi_actions`
**Purpose**: Present multiple action choices

**Location**: `awaiting_actions/chatnext_assistant_multi_actions.py`

**Configuration**:
- `title`: Action prompt
- `actions`: Available actions
- `allow_multiple`: Multiple selection
- `required`: Must select at least one

**Action Structure**:
```json
{
    "actions": [
        {
            "label": "Approve",
            "value": "approve",
            "style": "primary",
            "icon": "check"
        },
        {
            "label": "Reject",
            "value": "reject",
            "style": "danger",
            "icon": "times"
        }
    ]
}
```

**Wait For**: Action selection

---

#### `chatnext_assistant_document_approval`
**Purpose**: Document approval workflow

**Location**: `awaiting_actions/chatnext_assistant_document_approval.py`

**Configuration**:
- `doctype`: Document type
- `docname`: Document name
- `approval_fields`: Fields to display
- `approval_actions`: Available actions
- `comments_required`: Require comments

**Approval Actions**:
- Approve
- Reject
- Request Changes
- Delegate

**Wait For**: Approval decision

## Implementation Details

### Awaiting Action Lifecycle

1. **Initial Execution**:
```python
def action_name(action_node, variables):
    # Perform initial action
    send_message_or_prompt()

    # Store state for resumption
    save_awaiting_state()

    # Return with waiting flag
    return {
        "skip_task_creation": True,  # Don't create next tasks yet
        "skip_task_update": True,    # Keep task in waiting state
    }
```

2. **Event Handler**:
```python
def on_response_received(response_data):
    # Find waiting task
    task = find_waiting_task()

    # Process response
    result = process_response(response_data)

    # Update variables
    update_workflow_variables(result)

    # Create next tasks
    create_next_tasks()

    # Mark task complete
    complete_task()
```

### State Management

Awaiting actions maintain state through:

1. **Task State**: Task remains in "executing" state
2. **Workflow Variables**: Store waiting context
3. **External References**: Track external IDs (message IDs, session IDs)
4. **Timeout Handling**: Automatic timeout processing

### Response Handling

Responses are processed through:

1. **Webhooks**: External service callbacks
2. **Polling**: Periodic status checks
3. **Event Listeners**: System event monitoring
4. **Manual Triggers**: User actions

## Best Practices

### 1. Timeout Management
Always configure appropriate timeouts:
```python
timeout = node_data.get("timeout", 3600)  # Default 1 hour
schedule_timeout_handler(task_id, timeout)
```

### 2. State Persistence
Store sufficient state for resumption:
```python
awaiting_state = {
    "question_sent": True,
    "message_id": message_id,
    "start_time": now(),
    "expected_response": "text"
}
```

### 3. Error Recovery
Implement robust error handling:
```python
try:
    # Send question
except Exception as e:
    # Mark task failed
    # Trigger error flow
```

### 4. Response Validation
Validate responses before processing:
```python
def validate_response(response):
    if not response:
        return False
    if response_type != expected_type:
        return False
    return custom_validation(response)
```

### 5. User Experience
Provide clear feedback:
- Send confirmation of receipt
- Show waiting indicators
- Provide timeout warnings
- Allow cancellation

## Common Patterns

### 1. Retry on Invalid Input
```python
while not valid_response:
    send_question()
    response = wait_for_response()
    if validate(response):
        valid_response = response
    else:
        send_error_message()
```

### 2. Multi-Step Collection
Chain multiple awaiting actions:
```
Ask Name → Ask Email → Ask Phone → Confirm
```

### 3. Conditional Waiting
Wait based on conditions:
```python
if requires_approval:
    wait_for_approval()
else:
    proceed_immediately()
```

### 4. Parallel Waiting
Wait for multiple inputs:
```python
create_parallel_wait_tasks([
    "manager_approval",
    "finance_approval",
    "legal_approval"
])
wait_for_all_responses()
```

## Troubleshooting

### Issue: Response Not Received
**Checks**:
1. Webhook configured correctly
2. External service accessible
3. Response format valid
4. Task still in waiting state

### Issue: Timeout Not Working
**Checks**:
1. Scheduler running
2. Timeout value set
3. Timeout handler implemented
4. Task not already completed

### Issue: Wrong Response Processed
**Checks**:
1. Response validation logic
2. Message ID matching
3. Session management
4. Race condition handling

### Issue: State Lost
**Checks**:
1. State persistence implementation
2. Database transactions
3. Cache invalidation
4. Concurrent access handling

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/developer_guide.md
# Funnel Developer Guide

## Creating Custom Nodes

### Custom Node Structure

Custom nodes allow extending Funnel with specific business logic. Here's how to create one:

#### 1. Create the Node Definition

Add entry to `funnel_node.json`:

```json
{
    "name": "my_custom_node",
    "is_custom": 1,
    "python_function_dotted_path": "myapp.module.my_custom_node",
    "formio_json": {
        "components": [
            {
                "key": "api_key",
                "label": "API Key",
                "type": "textfield",
                "required": true
            }
        ]
    }
}
```

#### 2. Implement the Python Function

```python
# myapp/module.py

def my_custom_node(action_node, variables):
    """
    Custom node implementation

    Args:
        action_node: Node configuration object
        variables: Workflow variables dict

    Returns:
        dict: Result dictionary with standard keys
    """
    import json
    from nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task.utils.format_with_variables import format_with_variables

    # Get node configuration
    node_data = json.loads(action_node.data)

    # Get configuration with variable substitution
    api_key = format_with_variables(node_data.get("api_key"), variables)

    # Your custom logic here
    result = perform_custom_operation(api_key)

    # Return standard format
    return {
        "result": result,  # Will be stored in variables if output path configured
        "source_handle_id": None,  # Optional: specific output handle
        "skip_task_creation": False,  # Skip creating next tasks
        "task_info": {}  # Additional info to pass to next tasks
    }
```

### Custom Trigger Implementation

#### 1. Create Trigger Function

```python
# myapp/triggers/my_trigger.py

import frappe
from nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task.funnel_task import (
    create_new_task,
    queue_execute_pending_tasks,
    set_funnel_user
)
from nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task.utils.get_action_nodes import (
    get_action_nodes_from_trigger
)

@frappe.whitelist()
def my_custom_trigger(trigger_name, **kwargs):
    """Custom trigger implementation"""

    # Get trigger configuration
    trigger_doc = frappe.get_doc("Funnel Definition", trigger_name)

    # Get connected action nodes
    actions = get_action_nodes_from_trigger(trigger_doc.id)

    # Get published funnel
    published_funnel = frappe.get_doc("Funnel Published", trigger_doc.parent)

    # Set funnel user for execution
    set_funnel_user()

    # Prepare variables
    variables = {
        "event": "my_custom_trigger",
        "trigger_user_id": frappe.session.user,
        **kwargs
    }

    # Create workflow
    workflow = frappe.get_doc({
        "doctype": "Funnel Workflow",
        "funnel": trigger_doc.parent,
        "variables": json.dumps(variables, default=str),
        "priority": 1
    })
    workflow.insert(ignore_permissions=True)

    # Create initial tasks
    for action_node in actions:
        create_new_task(
            workflow.name,
            action_node.id,
            trigger_doc.id,
            "",
            task_info={"trigger": "my_custom"}
        )

    # Queue execution
    queue_execute_pending_tasks()

    return workflow
```

#### 2. Register Trigger Hook

```python
# In your app's hooks.py

doc_events = {
    "Your DocType": {
        "after_insert": "myapp.triggers.my_trigger.on_doc_event"
    }
}

# Or for scheduled triggers
scheduler_events = {
    "cron": {
        "0/5 * * * *": [
            "myapp.triggers.my_trigger.check_conditions"
        ]
    }
}
```

### Custom Awaiting Action

```python
# myapp/awaiting_actions/wait_for_approval.py

def wait_for_approval(action_node, variables):
    """Initial execution - send approval request"""

    node_data = json.loads(action_node.data)

    # Send approval request
    approval_request = create_approval_request(
        approver=node_data.get("approver"),
        document=variables.get("doc"),
        workflow_task=variables.get("task_name")
    )

    # Store state for resumption
    frappe.db.set_value(
        "Funnel Task",
        variables.get("task_name"),
        "awaiting_data",
        json.dumps({
            "approval_request": approval_request.name,
            "sent_at": frappe.utils.now()
        })
    )

    # Return waiting state
    return {
        "skip_task_creation": True,  # Don't create next tasks
        "skip_task_update": True,    # Keep task waiting
    }

def on_approval_received(approval_request_name, decision):
    """Resume handler - called when approval received"""

    # Find waiting task
    task = frappe.db.get_value(
        "Funnel Task",
        {
            "awaiting_data": ("like", f'%"{approval_request_name}"%'),
            "execution_status": "executing"
        },
        ["name", "workflow"],
        as_dict=1
    )

    if not task:
        return

    # Get workflow variables
    workflow = frappe.get_doc("Funnel Workflow", task.workflow)
    variables = json.loads(workflow.variables)

    # Update variables with approval decision
    variables["approval_decision"] = decision
    variables["approval_timestamp"] = frappe.utils.now()

    # Update workflow
    workflow.variables = json.dumps(variables, default=str)
    workflow.save()

    # Determine output handle based on decision
    source_handle = "approved" if decision == "approve" else "rejected"

    # Get next nodes for the specific output
    from nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task.utils.get_action_nodes import (
        get_next_action_nodes_with_source_handle_id
    )

    task_doc = frappe.get_doc("Funnel Task", task.name)
    next_nodes = get_next_action_nodes_with_source_handle_id(
        task_doc.target_node_id,
        source_handle
    )

    # Create next tasks
    for node in next_nodes:
        create_new_task(
            task.workflow,
            node.id,
            task_doc.target_node_id,
            task.name,
            task_info={"approval": decision}
        )

    # Mark task complete
    from nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task.utils.create_update_task import (
        update_task_status
    )
    from nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task import task_states

    update_task_status(
        task_doc,
        task_states.COMPLETED,
        {"result": {"decision": decision}}
    )

    # Queue next tasks
    queue_execute_pending_tasks()
```

## Working with Variables

### Variable Format

Variables are stored as JSON and support nested structures:

```python
variables = {
    "simple_value": "text",
    "number": 123,
    "array": [1, 2, 3],
    "object": {
        "nested": "value"
    },
    "doc": {
        "doctype": "Task",
        "name": "TASK-001",
        # Full document dict
    }
}
```

### Variable Substitution

Use the `format_with_variables` utility:

```python
from nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task.utils.format_with_variables import (
    format_with_variables
)

# Simple substitution
template = "Hello {{user_name}}"
result = format_with_variables(template, {"user_name": "John"})
# Result: "Hello John"

# Nested access
template = "Status: {{doc.status}}"
result = format_with_variables(template, {
    "doc": {"status": "Open"}
})
# Result: "Status: Open"

# Array access
template = "First item: {{items.0}}"
result = format_with_variables(template, {
    "items": ["apple", "banana"]
})
# Result: "First item: apple"
```

### Storing Output

Configure `variable_path_for_output` in node data:

```json
{
    "variable_path_for_output": "api_response"
}
```

Result will be stored at `variables["api_response"]`.

## Error Handling

### Node-Level Error Handling

```python
def my_action(action_node, variables):
    try:
        # Your logic
        return {"result": "success"}
    except ValidationError as e:
        # Handle specific error
        frappe.log_error(f"Validation failed: {e}")
        raise  # Re-raise for error flow
    except Exception as e:
        # Log and re-raise
        frappe.log_error(f"Unexpected error: {e}")
        raise
```

### Error Flow Configuration

Connect error output handle in workflow:

```javascript
// In workflow definition
{
    "edges": [
        {
            "source": "node1",
            "sourceHandle": "on_error",
            "target": "error_handler"
        }
    ]
}
```

### Global Error Handling

Implement workflow-level error handlers:

```python
def global_error_handler(workflow_name, error):
    """Handle workflow-level errors"""

    # Log error
    frappe.log_error(
        f"Workflow {workflow_name} failed: {error}",
        "Funnel Workflow Error"
    )

    # Update workflow status
    frappe.db.set_value(
        "Funnel Workflow",
        workflow_name,
        "status",
        "failed"
    )

    # Send notification
    notify_admin(workflow_name, error)
```

## Testing

### Unit Testing Nodes

```python
# test_my_node.py

import unittest
import frappe
from myapp.module import my_custom_node

class TestMyCustomNode(unittest.TestCase):

    def setUp(self):
        # Create mock action_node
        self.action_node = frappe._dict({
            "data": json.dumps({
                "config_field": "test_value"
            }),
            "id": "test_node_1",
            "type": "my_custom_node"
        })

        # Create test variables
        self.variables = {
            "test_var": "value"
        }

    def test_successful_execution(self):
        result = my_custom_node(self.action_node, self.variables)

        self.assertIn("result", result)
        self.assertFalse(result.get("skip_task_creation"))

    def test_error_handling(self):
        # Modify to cause error
        self.action_node.data = json.dumps({})

        with self.assertRaises(Exception):
            my_custom_node(self.action_node, self.variables)
```

### Integration Testing Workflows

```python
def test_workflow_execution():
    """Test complete workflow execution"""

    # Create test workflow
    workflow = create_test_workflow()

    # Trigger workflow
    trigger_workflow(workflow.name, test_data)

    # Execute pending tasks
    execute_all_pending_tasks()

    # Verify results
    workflow.reload()
    assert workflow.status == "finished"

    # Check variables
    variables = json.loads(workflow.variables)
    assert variables.get("result") == expected_value
```

## Performance Optimization

### 1. Batch Operations

```python
def process_bulk_items(action_node, variables):
    items = variables.get("items", [])

    # Bad: Individual operations
    # for item in items:
    #     frappe.db.set_value("Item", item, "status", "processed")

    # Good: Batch update
    frappe.db.sql("""
        UPDATE tabItem
        SET status = 'processed'
        WHERE name IN (%s)
    """ % ", ".join(["%s"] * len(items)), items)

    frappe.db.commit()
```

### 2. Async Processing

```python
def heavy_operation(action_node, variables):
    """Queue heavy operations"""

    # Queue the heavy task
    frappe.enqueue(
        "myapp.heavy_task",
        queue="long",
        task_id=variables.get("task_name"),
        data=variables.get("large_data")
    )

    # Return immediately
    return {
        "skip_task_creation": True,
        "skip_task_update": True
    }
```

### 3. Caching

```python
def cached_operation(action_node, variables):
    cache_key = f"funnel_cache_{variables.get('cache_key')}"

    # Try cache first
    cached_result = frappe.cache().get(cache_key)
    if cached_result:
        return {"result": cached_result}

    # Compute result
    result = expensive_computation()

    # Cache for 1 hour
    frappe.cache().set(cache_key, result, 3600)

    return {"result": result}
```

## Security Best Practices

### 1. Input Validation

```python
def secure_action(action_node, variables):
    # Validate inputs
    user_input = variables.get("user_input", "")

    # Sanitize
    if not isinstance(user_input, str):
        raise ValueError("Invalid input type")

    if len(user_input) > 1000:
        raise ValueError("Input too long")

    # Escape for SQL if needed
    safe_input = frappe.db.escape(user_input)

    # Process safely
    return process_input(safe_input)
```

### 2. Permission Checks

```python
def check_permissions(action_node, variables):
    doc_name = variables.get("doc_name")

    # Check read permission
    if not frappe.has_permission("DocType", "read", doc_name):
        raise frappe.PermissionError

    # Check specific permission
    doc = frappe.get_doc("DocType", doc_name)
    doc.check_permission("write")

    # Process with permissions verified
    return process_document(doc)
```

### 3. Rate Limiting

```python
def rate_limited_action(action_node, variables):
    user = variables.get("trigger_user_id")

    # Check rate limit
    cache_key = f"rate_limit_{user}_{action_node.type}"
    count = frappe.cache().get(cache_key) or 0

    if count >= 10:  # Max 10 per minute
        raise Exception("Rate limit exceeded")

    # Increment counter
    frappe.cache().set(cache_key, count + 1, 60)

    # Process request
    return process_request()
```

## Debugging

### Enable Debug Logging

```python
# In your node
import frappe

def debug_node(action_node, variables):
    # Log entry
    frappe.logger().debug(f"Entering node: {action_node.type}")
    frappe.logger().debug(f"Variables: {variables}")

    try:
        result = process()
        frappe.logger().debug(f"Result: {result}")
        return result
    except Exception as e:
        frappe.logger().error(f"Error in node: {e}")
        raise
```

### Task Inspection

```python
# Debug helper to inspect task state
def inspect_task(task_name):
    task = frappe.get_doc("Funnel Task", task_name)

    print(f"Task: {task.name}")
    print(f"Status: {task.execution_status}")
    print(f"Node: {task.target_node_id}")

    # Get workflow variables
    workflow = frappe.get_doc("Funnel Workflow", task.workflow)
    variables = json.loads(workflow.variables)
    print(f"Variables: {json.dumps(variables, indent=2)}")

    # Check execution info
    if task.execution_info:
        info = json.loads(task.execution_info)
        print(f"Execution Info: {json.dumps(info, indent=2)}")
```

### Workflow Visualization

```python
def visualize_workflow(workflow_name):
    """Generate workflow execution graph"""

    tasks = frappe.get_all(
        "Funnel Task",
        filters={"workflow": workflow_name},
        fields=["name", "target_node_id", "parent_task", "execution_status"]
    )

    # Build execution tree
    for task in tasks:
        indent = "  " * count_depth(task, tasks)
        status_icon = {
            "completed": "✓",
            "failed": "✗",
            "executing": "→",
            "pending": "○"
        }.get(task.execution_status, "?")

        print(f"{indent}{status_icon} {task.target_node_id}")
```

## Common Patterns

### 1. Approval Workflow

```python
# Multi-level approval pattern
def approval_workflow():
    return {
        "nodes": [
            {"id": "start", "type": "manual_trigger"},
            {"id": "level1", "type": "wait_for_approval", "data": {"approver": "manager"}},
            {"id": "level2", "type": "wait_for_approval", "data": {"approver": "director"}},
            {"id": "approved", "type": "update_status", "data": {"status": "Approved"}},
            {"id": "rejected", "type": "update_status", "data": {"status": "Rejected"}}
        ],
        "edges": [
            {"source": "start", "target": "level1"},
            {"source": "level1", "sourceHandle": "approved", "target": "level2"},
            {"source": "level1", "sourceHandle": "rejected", "target": "rejected"},
            {"source": "level2", "sourceHandle": "approved", "target": "approved"},
            {"source": "level2", "sourceHandle": "rejected", "target": "rejected"}
        ]
    }
```

### 2. Retry Pattern

```python
def retry_on_failure(action_node, variables):
    max_retries = 3
    retry_count = variables.get("retry_count", 0)

    try:
        # Attempt operation
        result = risky_operation()
        return {"result": result}
    except Exception as e:
        if retry_count < max_retries:
            # Update retry count
            variables["retry_count"] = retry_count + 1

            # Re-queue same task
            return {
                "source_handle_id": "retry",
                "task_info": {"retry_count": retry_count + 1}
            }
        else:
            # Max retries reached
            raise Exception(f"Failed after {max_retries} retries: {e}")
```

### 3. Parallel Processing

```python
def split_and_process(action_node, variables):
    items = variables.get("items", [])

    # Split into chunks
    chunk_size = 10
    chunks = [items[i:i+chunk_size] for i in range(0, len(items), chunk_size)]

    # Store chunks in variables
    for i, chunk in enumerate(chunks):
        variables[f"chunk_{i}"] = chunk

    # Return with parallel output
    return {
        "result": {"chunk_count": len(chunks)},
        "source_handle_id": "parallel"  # Connected to multiple process nodes
    }
```

## Node Statistics

- **Total Nodes**: 85
- **Triggers**: 11
- **Actions**: 48
- **Awaiting Actions**: 26

## Node Categories Summary

| Category | Count | Purpose |
|----------|-------|---------|
| **Triggers** | 11 | Workflow initiation points |
| **Document Operations** | 7 | CRUD operations on Frappe documents |
| **Communication** | 12 | Email and WhatsApp messaging |
| **AI Integration** | 5 | ChatGPT and intent detection |
| **User Input** | 19 | Collect user responses |
| **Utility** | 11 | Helper functions and integrations |
| **Control Flow** | 6 | Workflow control and synchronization |
| **Personal WhatsApp** | 9 | Personal account automation |
| **ChatNext Assistant** | 12 | Assistant UI and interactions |

## Common Node Patterns

### 1. Question-Answer Pattern
```
Trigger → Send Question (Awaiting) → Process Answer → Next Action
```
Examples: All `*_question` nodes

### 2. Approval Pattern
```
Trigger → Document Approval (Awaiting) → Approved/Rejected branches
```
Example: `chatnext_assistant_document_approval`

### 3. Notification Pattern
```
Trigger → Process → Send Notification → Complete
```
Examples: `send_mail`, `send_message_whatsapp`

### 4. Data Collection Pattern
```
Trigger → Multiple Questions (Awaiting) → Aggregate → Create Document
```
Examples: Form questions + `create_doctype`

### 5. Parallel Processing Pattern
```
Trigger → Split → Parallel Actions → Wait for Completion → Merge
```
Examples: `split_array` + `wait_for_nodes_to_complete`

## Node Connection Rules

### Output Handles

Standard output handles used by nodes:

| Handle Name | Purpose | Used By |
|------------|---------|---------|
| `default` | Standard success output | All nodes |
| `on_error` | Error flow | All nodes (optional) |
| `approved` | Approval granted | Approval nodes |
| `rejected` | Approval denied | Approval nodes |
| `yes` | Positive response | Boolean questions |
| `no` | Negative response | Boolean questions |
| `timeout` | Response timeout | Awaiting actions |
| `retry` | Retry flow | Actions with retry logic |
| `parallel` | Parallel execution | Split nodes |

### Connection Restrictions

1. **Triggers** can only be source nodes (no incoming connections)
2. **Awaiting Actions** pause execution until event occurs
3. **Control Flow** nodes can modify execution path
4. **Actions** execute immediately and continue

## Variable Naming Conventions

Standard variables set by node types:

| Variable | Set By | Description |
|----------|--------|-------------|
| `doc` | Document triggers/actions | Document data |
| `event` | All triggers | Event type |
| `trigger_user_id` | All triggers | User who triggered |
| `error` | Error handlers | Error information |
| `result` | Actions with output | Action result |
| `answer` | Question nodes | User response |
| `approval_decision` | Approval nodes | Approval status |
| `workflow_name` | System | Current workflow |
| `task_name` | System | Current task |

## Performance Characteristics

### Fast Nodes ( 1s)
- AI operations (`chatgpt_message`)
- OCR processing (`file_to_ocr`)
- API calls (`call_api`)
- Bulk operations (`send_bulk_mail`)

### Awaiting Nodes (Variable)
- User input (seconds to hours)
- Email replies (minutes to days)
- Scheduled waits (`wait_till_date`)

## Security Levels

| Level | Nodes | Restrictions |
|-------|-------|--------------|
| **High Risk** | `exec_python`, `frappe_exec_dotted_path` | Requires admin permission |
| **Medium Risk** | `call_api`, Document operations | Requires specific permissions |
| **Low Risk** | Messaging, UI actions | Standard user permissions |
| **Safe** | Labels, wait nodes | No special permissions |

## Version Compatibility

| Node | Minimum Version | Notes |
|------|----------------|-------|
| All core nodes | v1.0.0 | Base functionality |
| ChatNext nodes | v1.2.0 | Requires ChatNext module |
| Personal WhatsApp | v1.3.0 | Requires WhatsApp Web integration |
| AI nodes | v1.4.0 | Requires OpenAI configuration |

## Debugging Tips

1. **Check Node Existence**: Verify node is in `funnel_node.json`
2. **Validate Configuration**: Check FormIO schema matches
3. **Review Permissions**: Ensure user has node permissions
4. **Monitor Task State**: Check `Funnel Task` execution status
5. **Inspect Variables**: Review workflow variables for issues
6. **Check Logs**: Review error logs for detailed traces

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/add_comment.md
# Add Comment

## Overview
The Add Comment node allows you to automatically add comments to any document in your system. This is useful for logging workflow activities, adding status updates, providing context, or creating an audit trail of automated processes.

**Category**: Utilities
**Type**: Action Node
**Label**: Add Comment

## When to Use
Use the Add Comment node when you need to:
- Log workflow progress on a document
- Add automatic status updates
- Create audit trails for compliance
- Provide context about automated actions
- Add notes from AI or calculation results
- Document approval decisions or rejections
- Track when certain conditions were met

## Configuration Fields

### 1. Select Doctype
**Field Type**: Select (Dropdown)
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty
**Data Source**: All DocTypes in the system

**Description**: Choose the document type where you want to add the comment. This dropdown fetches all available DocTypes from your system.

**How It Works**:
- If left empty, the node will use the DocType from the workflow context (if available)
- When selected, it defines which type of document will receive the comment
- The DocType must exist and be accessible to the workflow user

**Variable Usage**:
- Can use variables: `{{doc.doctype}}`
- Example: If processing a Sales Invoice, you might use `{{doc.doctype}}` to dynamically set this

**Common DocTypes**:
- Task
- Issue
- Sales Invoice
- Purchase Order
- Lead
- Customer
- Any custom DocType

### 2. Name
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The specific document name/ID where the comment will be added.

**How It Works**:
- Enter the exact document name (e.g., "TASK-001", "SINV-2024-001")
- If the document doesn't exist, the node will fail
- Must match an existing document of the selected DocType

**Variable Usage**:
- Commonly uses variables from workflow context
- `{{doc.name}}` - Uses the document that triggered the workflow
- `{{task_name}}` - Uses a task name from previous nodes
- `{{created_doc.name}}` - Uses a document created earlier in workflow

**Examples**:
- Static: `TASK-001`
- Dynamic: `{{doc.name}}`
- From variable: `{{invoice_id}}`
- Concatenated: `INV-{{year}}-{{number}}`

### 3. Comment
**Field Type**: Text Area (Multi-line)
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The actual comment text to be added to the document.

**How It Works**:
- Supports multi-line text
- Can include variables for dynamic content
- HTML formatting is typically escaped for security
- Comments appear in the document's comment thread with timestamp and user info

**Variable Usage**:
You can create dynamic comments using variables:
- `Workflow started by {{trigger_user_id}}`
- `Status changed to {{new_status}} at {{timestamp}}`
- `Approval granted by {{approver_name}} with notes: {{approval_notes}}`
- `Calculated total: {{currency}} {{calculated_amount}}`

**Best Practices for Comments**:
1. **Be Descriptive**: Include relevant context
   - Good: "Invoice approved by John Doe on 2024-01-15. Amount: $5,000"
   - Bad: "Approved"

2. **Use Timestamps**: Include when actions occurred
   - Example: `Process completed at {{current_datetime}}`

3. **Include User Context**: Show who triggered or approved
   - Example: `Initiated by {{trigger_user_id}} from {{source_system}}`

4. **Add Workflow Context**: Reference the workflow
   - Example: `[Workflow: {{workflow_name}}] Automated processing complete`

**Comment Examples**:

**For Approval Workflows**:
```
✅ Approved by {{approver_name}}
Department: {{department}}
Amount: {{currency}} {{amount}}
Approval Level: {{approval_level}}
Notes: {{approval_comments}}
```

**For Error Logging**:
```
⚠️ Workflow Error Detected
Error: {{error_message}}
Node: {{failed_node}}
Time: {{timestamp}}
Action Required: Manual review needed
```

**For Status Updates**:
```
📋 Status Update
Previous Status: {{old_status}}
New Status: {{new_status}}
Changed By: Automated Workflow
Reason: {{status_change_reason}}
```

**For Audit Trail**:
```
🔍 Audit Log Entry
Action: {{action_type}}
User: {{user_email}}
IP Address: {{ip_address}}
Timestamp: {{audit_timestamp}}
Details: {{audit_details}}
```

## Variables Available

### Input Variables
The node can access all variables from the workflow context:

- `{{doc}}` - The current document object
- `{{doc.fieldname}}` - Any field from the current document
- `{{trigger_user_id}}` - User who triggered the workflow
- `{{workflow_name}}` - Name of the running workflow
- `{{task_name}}` - Current task identifier
- Any custom variables set by previous nodes

### Output Variables
This node does not create new variables, but it does return:
- Success/failure status
- The comment that was added (for logging)

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Approval Workflow Documentation
**Scenario**: Document each approval step
```
Comment: "Level 1 Approval completed by {{manager_name}} on {{date}}.
Proceeding to Level 2 (Director) approval."
```

### 2. Error Handling and Recovery
**Scenario**: Log when errors occur and are resolved
```
Comment: "API call failed at {{time}}. Error: {{error_msg}}.
Retrying attempt {{retry_count}} of 3."
```

### 3. Calculation Results
**Scenario**: Store calculated values
```
Comment: "Discount Calculation:
Original Price: {{original_price}}
Discount Rate: {{discount_rate}}%
Final Price: {{final_price}}
Savings: {{savings_amount}}"
```

### 4. Integration Logs
**Scenario**: Track external system interactions
```
Comment: "Synced with CRM system
CRM ID: {{crm_id}}
Sync Status: {{sync_status}}
Last Updated: {{sync_timestamp}}"
```

### 5. Compliance Documentation
**Scenario**: Create audit trails
```
Comment: "Compliance Check Performed
Regulation: {{regulation_name}}
Check Result: {{compliance_status}}
Verified By: System Automation
Certificate: {{certificate_number}}"
```

## Best Practices

### 1. Comment Structure
- Start with a clear identifier (emoji or prefix)
- Include timestamp information
- Add user context when relevant
- Keep comments concise but informative

### 2. Variable Validation
- Ensure variables exist before using them
- Provide fallback values for optional variables
- Test with sample data before production

### 3. Performance Considerations
- Avoid adding too many comments in loops
- Batch comments when possible
- Consider comment size limits

### 4. Security
- Don't include sensitive data (passwords, tokens)
- Sanitize user input variables
- Be careful with personal information

### 5. Maintenance
- Use consistent comment formats across workflows
- Document comment structure for team members
- Regular cleanup of old comments if needed

## Troubleshooting

### Comment Not Appearing
**Possible Causes**:
1. Document name doesn't exist
2. DocType mismatch
3. Permissions issue
4. Workflow execution failed before comment node

**Solutions**:
- Verify document exists with exact name
- Check DocType spelling and case
- Ensure workflow user has comment permissions
- Check workflow execution logs

### Variables Not Replaced
**Possible Causes**:
1. Variable doesn't exist in context
2. Incorrect variable syntax
3. Variable is null or undefined

**Solutions**:
- Use correct syntax: `{{variable_name}}`
- Check previous nodes set the variable
- Add error handling for missing variables
- Test with debug mode to see available variables

### Special Characters Issues
**Possible Causes**:
1. Unescaped quotes in comment text
2. HTML tags being interpreted
3. Unicode characters not supported

**Solutions**:
- Escape special characters properly
- Use plain text instead of HTML
- Test with various character sets

## Advanced Usage

### Conditional Comments
While the node doesn't have built-in conditions, you can:
1. Use conditional nodes before this node
2. Build dynamic comments with inline conditions
3. Use multiple comment nodes for different scenarios

### Comment Threading
- Comments are added to the document's comment thread
- They appear chronologically with other manual and automated comments
- Each comment shows the user who added it (workflow user)

### Integration with Notifications
Comments can trigger notifications if:
- Document has subscribers
- Notification rules are configured
- @mentions are included in comments

### Bulk Comments
For multiple documents:
1. Use within a loop node
2. Iterate through document list
3. Add comments to each document

## Limitations

1. **Text Only**: No rich media attachments
2. **No Editing**: Comments cannot be edited after creation
3. **Character Limit**: Subject to system comment length limits
4. **No Private Comments**: All comments are visible to users with access

## Related Nodes

- **Send Notification**: For real-time alerts
- **Update DocType**: To change document fields
- **Create Task**: To create follow-up tasks
- **Send Mail**: To send email notifications
- **Add Tags to Doc**: To add categorization

## Performance Tips

1. **Avoid Loops**: Don't add comments in tight loops
2. **Batch Processing**: Group multiple updates when possible
3. **Async Execution**: Comments are added synchronously
4. **Cache Variables**: Reuse calculated values

## Security Considerations

1. **Data Privacy**: Don't log sensitive information
2. **User Permissions**: Respect document access controls
3. **Input Validation**: Sanitize variable content
4. **Audit Compliance**: Follow regulatory requirements

## Examples in Different Contexts

### Sales Process
```
Comment: "Quote {{quote_number}} converted to Sales Order {{so_number}}.
Customer: {{customer_name}}
Value: {{order_value}}
Expected Delivery: {{delivery_date}}"
```

### HR Process
```
Comment: "Leave Application {{leave_id}} processed.
Employee: {{employee_name}}
Type: {{leave_type}}
Days: {{leave_days}}
Status: {{approval_status}}"
```

### Manufacturing
```
Comment: "Production Order {{po_number}} status update:
Item: {{item_code}}
Completed Qty: {{completed_qty}}/{{total_qty}}
Completion: {{completion_percentage}}%"
```

### Support Ticket
```
Comment: "Ticket escalated to Level {{escalation_level}}
Priority changed from {{old_priority}} to {{new_priority}}
SLA Status: {{sla_status}}
Assigned to: {{assigned_team}}"
```

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/add_tags_to_doc.md
# Add Tags to Doc

## Overview
The Add Tags to Doc node allows you to automatically add tags to any document in your system. Tags are useful for categorization, filtering, reporting, and organizing documents. This node enables dynamic tagging based on workflow conditions and data.

**Category**: Utilities
**Type**: Action Node
**Label**: Add Tags to Document

## When to Use
Use the Add Tags to Doc node when you need to:
- Categorize documents automatically based on criteria
- Add status tags for easy filtering
- Mark documents for follow-up actions
- Create dynamic categorization based on workflow logic
- Tag documents for reporting and analytics
- Add compliance or audit tags
- Mark priority levels or urgency
- Group related documents together

## Configuration Fields

### 1. Doctype
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The type of document you want to add tags to.

**How It Works**:
- Enter the exact DocType name (case-sensitive)
- The DocType must exist in your system
- Common DocTypes include Task, Issue, Lead, Customer, Sales Invoice, etc.

**Variable Usage**:
- Can use variables from workflow context
- `{{doc.doctype}}` - Use the DocType from the current document
- `{{target_doctype}}` - Use a variable set earlier in the workflow
- Direct text: `Task`, `Sales Invoice`, `Customer`

**Examples**:
- Static: `Task`
- Dynamic: `{{doc.doctype}}`
- Variable: `{{selected_doctype}}`

**Important Notes**:
- DocType names are case-sensitive
- Must match exactly as defined in the system
- Custom DocTypes are supported

### 2. Docname
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The specific document name/ID to which tags will be added.

**How It Works**:
- Enter the exact document name (e.g., "TASK-001", "CUST-00123")
- Document must exist in the specified DocType
- Tags will be appended to existing tags (won't replace them)

**Variable Usage**:
- `{{doc.name}}` - Current document from workflow
- `{{created_document}}` - Document created in previous node
- `{{customer_id}}` - Custom variable from workflow
- Direct value: `TASK-001`

**Examples**:
- Static: `TASK-2024-001`
- From trigger: `{{doc.name}}`
- From variable: `{{invoice_number}}`
- Concatenated: `{{prefix}}-{{number}}`

**Validation**:
- Document must exist
- User must have write permissions
- Document must not be cancelled/deleted

### 3. Tags
**Field Type**: Text Area (Multi-line)
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The tags to add to the document. Enter one tag per line or separate with commas.

**How It Works**:
- Each line becomes a separate tag
- Alternatively, use comma-separated values
- Tags are trimmed of whitespace
- Duplicate tags are automatically filtered
- Empty lines are ignored
- Special characters are allowed but should be used carefully

**Variable Usage**:
You can create dynamic tags using variables:
```
{{status}}-priority
urgent-{{department}}
{{year}}-{{quarter}}
{{category}}_{{subcategory}}
region-{{sales_region}}
```

**Format Options**:

**Line-Separated** (Recommended):
```
high-priority
needs-review
2024-Q1
customer-vip
```

**Comma-Separated**:
```
high-priority, needs-review, 2024-Q1, customer-vip
```

**Dynamic Examples**:
```
{{priority}}-priority
{{workflow_name}}
status-{{current_status}}
assigned-{{assigned_to}}
due-{{due_date}}
```

**Tag Naming Best Practices**:
1. **Use Consistent Format**: Choose a naming convention and stick to it
   - Good: `status-pending`, `status-approved`, `status-rejected`
   - Bad: `pending`, `Approved`, `REJECTED`

2. **Include Context**: Make tags self-explanatory
   - Good: `priority-high`, `dept-sales`, `year-2024`
   - Bad: `high`, `sales`, `2024`

3. **Avoid Special Characters**: Use hyphens or underscores
   - Good: `follow-up`, `needs_review`
   - Bad: `follow up`, `needs@review`, `needs#review`

4. **Keep It Short**: Tags should be concise
   - Good: `urgent`, `vip-customer`
   - Bad: `this-document-needs-urgent-attention-from-management`

## Variables Available

### Input Variables
Access all workflow context variables:
- `{{doc}}` - Current document object
- `{{doc.field_name}}` - Any field from the current document
- `{{trigger_user_id}}` - User who triggered the workflow
- `{{workflow_name}}` - Current workflow name
- Custom variables from previous nodes

### Output Variables
This node returns:
- Success/failure status
- List of tags that were added
- Document name that was tagged

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Priority Tagging
**Scenario**: Tag documents based on value or urgency
```
Tags:
priority-{{priority_level}}
value-{{value_category}}
sla-{{sla_status}}
```

**Example Output**:
- `priority-high`
- `value-above-10k`
- `sla-critical`

### 2. Status Workflow
**Scenario**: Tag documents as they move through stages
```
Tags:
stage-{{current_stage}}
reviewed-by-{{reviewer}}
{{approval_status}}
{{timestamp}}
```

### 3. Department Categorization
**Scenario**: Add department and team tags
```
Tags:
dept-{{department}}
team-{{team_name}}
manager-{{manager_id}}
cost-center-{{cost_center}}
```

### 4. Time-Based Tagging
**Scenario**: Add temporal tags for reporting
```
Tags:
year-{{current_year}}
quarter-{{current_quarter}}
month-{{current_month}}
week-{{week_number}}
fiscal-{{fiscal_period}}
```

### 5. Compliance Tagging
**Scenario**: Mark documents for compliance tracking
```
Tags:
compliance-required
audit-{{audit_year}}
regulation-{{regulation_type}}
review-pending
certified-{{certification_date}}
```

### 6. Customer Segmentation
**Scenario**: Tag based on customer attributes
```
Tags:
customer-{{customer_tier}}
region-{{customer_region}}
industry-{{industry_type}}
account-{{account_type}}
```

### 7. Automated Classification
**Scenario**: Use AI or logic to classify and tag
```
Tags:
sentiment-{{sentiment_analysis}}
category-{{auto_category}}
risk-{{risk_level}}
action-{{recommended_action}}
```

## Best Practices

### 1. Tag Strategy
- **Establish Naming Conventions**: Document and share tag formats
- **Use Hierarchical Structure**: `category-subcategory-detail`
- **Limit Tag Count**: Don't over-tag documents (5-10 tags ideal)
- **Regular Cleanup**: Remove obsolete tags periodically

### 2. Performance
- **Batch Operations**: Group tagging operations when possible
- **Avoid Loops**: Don't tag in tight loops without delays
- **Cache Tag Lists**: Reuse tag sets for similar documents

### 3. Consistency
- **Standardize Formats**: Always lowercase or always title case
- **Document Standards**: Create a tag dictionary
- **Validate Tags**: Check against allowed tag list if needed

### 4. Search Optimization
- **Searchable Tags**: Use tags that help in searching
- **Avoid Redundancy**: Don't duplicate information in fields
- **Meaningful Tags**: Make tags human-readable

## Troubleshooting

### Tags Not Being Added
**Possible Causes**:
1. Document doesn't exist
2. DocType name incorrect
3. No write permissions
4. Document is cancelled
5. Invalid tag format

**Solutions**:
- Verify document exists with exact name
- Check DocType spelling and case sensitivity
- Ensure workflow user has tagging permissions
- Check document status
- Validate tag format (no invalid characters)

### Duplicate Tags
**Possible Causes**:
1. Tag already exists on document
2. Multiple nodes adding same tag
3. Workflow running multiple times

**Solutions**:
- System automatically prevents duplicates
- Check workflow logic for redundancy
- Add conditions to prevent re-tagging

### Variable Not Replaced
**Possible Causes**:
1. Variable doesn't exist
2. Incorrect syntax
3. Variable is empty/null

**Solutions**:
- Use correct syntax: `{{variable}}`
- Check variable is set in previous nodes
- Provide default values for optional variables
- Test with debug logging

## Advanced Usage

### Conditional Tagging
Create dynamic tag sets based on conditions:
```
{{amount > 10000 ? "high-value" : "standard"}}
{{days_overdue > 0 ? "overdue" : "current"}}
{{risk_score > 75 ? "high-risk" : "low-risk"}}
```

### Tag Combinations
Build complex categorization:
```
{{department}}-{{priority}}-{{status}}
Results in: sales-high-pending
```

### Tag Arrays from Variables
If you have a list variable:
```
{{tag_list}}
Where tag_list = ["urgent", "review", "Q1-2024"]
```

### Integration with Filters
Tags added can be used for:
- List view filters
- Report filters
- Dashboard widgets
- Automated workflows triggers

## Examples by Industry

### Healthcare
```
Tags:
patient-{{patient_category}}
urgency-{{triage_level}}
department-{{medical_dept}}
insurance-{{insurance_status}}
```

### E-commerce
```
Tags:
order-{{order_status}}
shipping-{{shipping_method}}
payment-{{payment_status}}
customer-{{customer_segment}}
```

### Manufacturing
```
Tags:
batch-{{batch_number}}
quality-{{quality_status}}
line-{{production_line}}
shift-{{shift_number}}
```

### Education
```
Tags:
course-{{course_code}}
semester-{{semester}}
grade-{{grade_level}}
status-{{enrollment_status}}
```

## Performance Considerations

1. **Tag Limit**: Systems may have limits on tags per document
2. **Index Impact**: Many tags can affect search performance
3. **Storage**: Tags consume database space
4. **Processing**: Adding many tags is slower than few

## Security Notes

1. **Permissions**: Users need write access to add tags
2. **Tag Visibility**: Tags are visible to all with document access
3. **Sensitive Data**: Avoid putting sensitive info in tags
4. **Audit Trail**: Tag additions are logged in document history

## Integration Points

### With Other Nodes
- **After Create DocType**: Tag newly created documents
- **After Update DocType**: Tag after status changes
- **With Conditional Node**: Tag based on conditions
- **Before Send Notification**: Tag for notification routing

### With System Features
- **Filters**: Tags appear in list view filters
- **Reports**: Use tags for report grouping
- **Search**: Tags are searchable
- **Workflows**: Other workflows can trigger on tags

## Limitations

1. **No Tag Removal**: This node only adds tags, doesn't remove
2. **No Tag Renaming**: Cannot rename existing tags
3. **Text Only**: Tags are text strings only
4. **No Tag Metadata**: Cannot add properties to tags
5. **No Private Tags**: All tags are visible to document viewers

## Related Nodes

- **Add Comment**: For adding detailed notes
- **Update DocType**: For changing document fields
- **Send Notification**: Alert about tagged documents
- **Create Task**: Create tasks for tagged items
- **Var Filter**: Filter documents by tags

## Common Patterns

### Progressive Tagging
```
Node 1: Add initial tags (status-new)
Node 2: Process document
Node 3: Add result tags (processed-success)
Node 4: Add timestamp tag (completed-2024-01-15)
```

### Hierarchical Tagging
```
Tags:
level1-{{main_category}}
level2-{{sub_category}}
level3-{{specific_type}}
```

### Multi-dimensional Tagging
```
Tags:
temporal-{{time_dimension}}
geographic-{{location}}
organizational-{{department}}
financial-{{cost_center}}
```
