# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/assign_user.md
# Assign User

## Overview
The Assign User node automatically assigns a user to a document, creating accountability and ensuring follow-up actions. When a user is assigned to a document, they receive notifications and the document appears in their "Assigned to Me" list, making it easy to track responsibilities.

**Category**: Utilities
**Type**: Action Node
**Label**: Assign User

## When to Use
Use the Assign User node when you need to:
- Automatically assign tasks or documents to specific users
- Create accountability for document processing
- Route documents to the right person based on workflow logic
- Distribute workload among team members
- Escalate issues to managers or specialists
- Assign reviewers or approvers dynamically
- Create follow-up reminders for users
- Transfer ownership during workflow stages

## Configuration Fields

### 1. Select Doctype
**Field Type**: Select (Dropdown)
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty
**Data Source**: All DocTypes in the system

**Description**: Choose the document type for the assignment. This dropdown fetches all available DocTypes from your system.

**How It Works**:
- If left empty, uses the DocType from workflow context
- Defines which type of document will have the assignment
- Must be a valid DocType that supports assignments

**Variable Usage**:
- `{{doc.doctype}}` - Use current document's type
- `{{target_doctype}}` - Use a variable from workflow
- Direct text: `Task`, `Issue`, `Sales Invoice`

**Common DocTypes for Assignment**:
- Task - For task management
- Issue - For support tickets
- Sales Invoice - For payment follow-up
- Purchase Order - For procurement processing
- Lead - For sales follow-up
- Opportunity - For deal management
- Project - For project ownership

**Important Notes**:
- Not all DocTypes support assignments
- DocType must have assignment capability enabled
- Custom DocTypes can be configured for assignments

### 2. Name
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The specific document name/ID to which the user will be assigned.

**How It Works**:
- Enter exact document name (e.g., "TASK-001")
- Document must exist in the specified DocType
- Assignment creates a todo/notification for the user

**Variable Usage**:
- `{{doc.name}}` - Current document
- `{{created_task}}` - Task created earlier
- `{{ticket_number}}` - Support ticket ID
- Direct value: `TASK-2024-001`

**Examples**:
```
Static: TASK-001
From trigger: {{doc.name}}
From variable: {{invoice_id}}
Computed: {{prefix}}-{{number}}
```

**Validation**:
- Document must exist
- Document must not be cancelled
- Cannot assign to submitted documents (in some cases)

### 3. User Id
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The email ID or username of the user to be assigned to the document.

**How It Works**:
- Enter user's email (most common)
- User must exist in the system
- User will receive notification upon assignment
- Document appears in user's todo list

**Variable Usage**:
Dynamic assignment based on workflow logic:
- `{{doc.owner}}` - Assign to document owner
- `{{manager_email}}` - Assign to manager
- `{{next_approver}}` - Assign to next approver in chain
- `{{sales_rep}}` - Assign to sales representative
- `{{support_agent}}` - Assign to support agent

**Assignment Strategies**:

**Role-Based**:
```
{{role.accounts_manager}}
{{role.sales_head}}
{{department.head_email}}
```

**Round-Robin** (with custom logic):
```
{{next_available_agent}}
{{least_loaded_user}}
```

**Escalation-Based**:
```
{{level_1_support}}
{{level_2_support}}
{{manager_of_{{current_assignee}}}}
```

**Geographic**:
```
{{region_manager_{{customer_region}}}}
{{branch_head_{{branch_code}}}}
```

**Important Considerations**:
- User must have appropriate permissions
- Email must be valid and active
- Consider user's workload
- Check user's availability (vacation, etc.)

## Variables Available

### Input Variables
The node can access:
- `{{doc}}` - Current document object
- `{{doc.field_name}}` - Any field from document
- `{{trigger_user_id}}` - Workflow initiator
- `{{workflow_name}}` - Current workflow
- Custom variables from previous nodes
- User lists or role mappings

### Output Variables
The node returns:
- Assignment success status
- Assigned user details
- Assignment timestamp
- Todo/notification created

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Task Distribution
**Scenario**: Assign tasks based on workload
```
DocType: Task
Name: {{new_task_id}}
User Id: {{get_least_busy_user()}}
```

**Workflow Logic**:
1. Create task
2. Check team availability
3. Assign to available member
4. Send notification

### 2. Approval Routing
**Scenario**: Route documents through approval chain
```
DocType: Purchase Order
Name: {{doc.name}}
User Id: {{get_approver_by_amount(doc.total)}}
```

**Dynamic Approver Selection**:
- Amount = 10000: Director

### 3. Support Ticket Assignment
**Scenario**: Assign tickets based on category
```
DocType: Issue
Name: {{ticket_id}}
User Id: {{support_matrix[ticket_category]}}
```

**Category Mapping**:
- Technical: technical_support@company.com
- Billing: billing_support@company.com
- General: general_support@company.com

### 4. Sales Lead Distribution
**Scenario**: Distribute leads by territory
```
DocType: Lead
Name: {{doc.name}}
User Id: {{territory_manager[lead_territory]}}
```

### 5. Document Review Assignment
**Scenario**: Assign documents for review
```
DocType: Contract
Name: {{contract_id}}
User Id: {{legal_reviewer}}
```

### 6. Escalation Management
**Scenario**: Escalate overdue items
```
DocType: Task
Name: {{overdue_task}}
User Id: {{escalation_manager}}
```

## Best Practices

### 1. Assignment Strategy
- **Clear Ownership**: Assign to one person at a time
- **Availability Check**: Verify user is active
- **Load Balancing**: Distribute work evenly
- **Skill Matching**: Assign based on expertise

### 2. Notification Management
- **Immediate Alerts**: Assignments trigger notifications
- **Clear Context**: Include why document was assigned
- **Priority Indication**: Mark urgent assignments
- **Due Dates**: Set expectations for completion

### 3. Workflow Design
- **Sequential Assignment**: One person completes before next
- **Parallel Assignment**: Multiple people work simultaneously
- **Escalation Paths**: Define timeout and escalation
- **Reassignment Logic**: Handle user unavailability

### 4. User Experience
- **Clear Instructions**: Document what's expected
- **Context Preservation**: Include relevant information
- **Easy Access**: Ensure user can access document
- **Mobile Friendly**: Consider mobile notifications

## Advanced Configuration

### Dynamic User Selection

#### Based on Document Fields
```javascript
// Assign based on customer type
if (doc.customer_type == "VIP") {
    user_id = "senior_manager@company.com"
} else {
    user_id = "support@company.com"
}
```

#### Based on Time/Schedule
```javascript
// Assign based on shift
current_hour = new Date().getHours()
if (current_hour < 12) {
    user_id = "morning_shift@company.com"
} else {
    user_id = "evening_shift@company.com"
}
```

#### Based on Workload
```javascript
// Get user with least assignments
user_id = {{get_user_with_min_assignments()}}
```

#### Based on Skills
```javascript
// Match required skills
if (doc.requires_technical) {
    user_id = {{technical_expert}}
} else {
    user_id = {{general_support}}
}
```

## Troubleshooting

### Assignment Not Created
**Possible Causes**:
1. User doesn't exist
2. Invalid email format
3. Document doesn't exist
4. No assignment permissions
5. Document is cancelled/deleted

**Solutions**:
- Verify user email is correct
- Check user is active in system
- Confirm document exists
- Check workflow user permissions
- Verify document status

### User Not Receiving Notifications
**Possible Causes**:
1. Notification settings disabled
2. Email configuration issues
3. User preferences
4. System email queue problems

**Solutions**:
- Check user notification preferences
- Verify email account configuration
- Test email delivery
- Check email queue status

### Multiple Assignments Created
**Possible Causes**:
1. Workflow running multiple times
2. Loop without proper conditions
3. Duplicate assignment logic

**Solutions**:
- Add checks for existing assignments
- Implement idempotency
- Review workflow logic
- Add assignment limits

## Integration Examples

### With Other Nodes

#### After Document Creation
```
Create Task → Assign User → Send Notification
```

#### In Approval Workflow
```
Check Conditions → Assign Approver → Wait for Approval
```

#### With Escalation
```
Check Due Date → If Overdue → Assign to Manager
```

### With System Features

#### Todo List Integration
- Assignments appear in user's todo list
- Can set priority and due date
- Track completion status

#### Calendar Integration
- Assignments can show on calendar
- Set reminders
- Schedule follow-ups

#### Mobile App
- Push notifications for assignments
- Mobile todo list access
- Quick actions from mobile

## Performance Considerations

1. **Bulk Assignments**: Batch when assigning many documents
2. **User Lookup**: Cache user information
3. **Notification Queue**: Assignments queue notifications
4. **Database Impact**: Each assignment creates records

## Security and Permissions

1. **Assignment Rights**: User needs assignment permission
2. **Document Access**: Assigned user gets read access
3. **Notification Privacy**: Only assigned user sees todo
4. **Audit Trail**: Assignments are logged

## Limitations

1. **Single User**: Can only assign one user at a time
2. **No Role Assignment**: Cannot assign to roles/groups
3. **No Removal**: This node only adds assignments
4. **No Due Dates**: Due dates set separately
5. **No Priority**: Priority managed elsewhere

## Related Nodes

- **Add Comment**: Add notes about assignment
- **Send Notification**: Additional alerts
- **Update DocType**: Change document status
- **Create Task**: Create related tasks
- **Send Mail**: Send detailed instructions

## Common Patterns

### Approval Chain
```
Level 1: Assign to Team Lead
↓ (After approval)
Level 2: Assign to Manager
↓ (After approval)
Level 3: Assign to Director
```

### Round-Robin Assignment
```
Get Next User from Queue
→ Assign Document
→ Update Queue Position
→ Balance Workload
```

### Skill-Based Routing
```
Analyze Requirements
→ Match Skills Database
→ Find Available Expert
→ Assign to Expert
```

### Time-Based Escalation
```
Initial Assignment
→ Wait 24 Hours
→ Check if Completed
→ If Not: Assign to Manager
```

## Examples by Department

### Sales
```
New Lead → Assign to Sales Rep by Territory
Opportunity → Assign to Account Manager
Quote → Assign to Sales Manager for Approval
```

### Support
```
New Ticket → Assign by Category
Escalated → Assign to Senior Support
Critical → Assign to On-Call Engineer
```

### HR
```
Leave Request → Assign to Direct Manager
Recruitment → Assign to HR Recruiter
Onboarding → Assign to HR Coordinator
```

### Finance
```
Invoice → Assign to Accounts Receivable
Expense → Assign to Expense Approver
Budget → Assign to Finance Manager
```

## Tips for Effective Assignment

1. **Clear Ownership**: One person responsible at a time
2. **Timely Notifications**: Immediate assignment alerts
3. **Context Included**: Why and what needs to be done
4. **Follow-up Mechanism**: Track if acted upon
5. **Reassignment Logic**: Handle non-response
6. **Load Management**: Don't overload users
7. **Skill Matching**: Right person for the job
8. **Availability Checking**: Consider schedules
9. **Escalation Paths**: Define timeout actions
10. **Audit Trail**: Track all assignments

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/call_api.md
# Call API

## Overview
The Call API node enables workflows to interact with external services and APIs, allowing you to send HTTP requests, receive responses, and integrate third-party services into your automation. This powerful node supports all standard HTTP methods and can handle complex authentication, headers, and payloads.

**Category**: Utilities
**Type**: Action Node
**Label**: Call API

## When to Use
Use the Call API node when you need to:
- Integrate with third-party services (CRM, ERP, payment gateways)
- Send data to external webhooks
- Fetch data from external APIs
- Trigger actions in other systems
- Synchronize data between systems
- Validate information against external sources
- Send notifications through external services
- Process payments or transactions
- Get real-time data (weather, stock prices, exchange rates)
- Submit forms to external services

## Configuration Fields

### 1. API URL
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The complete URL endpoint for the API call.

**How It Works**:
- Enter the full URL including protocol (http:// or https://)
- Can include path parameters and query strings
- Supports both internal and external APIs
- URL is validated before execution

**Variable Usage**:
Build dynamic URLs using variables:
- `https://api.service.com/users/{{user_id}}`
- `{{base_url}}/api/v1/{{endpoint}}`
- `https://api.company.com/orders?status={{order_status}}&date={{date}}`
- `{{webhook_url}}?token={{api_token}}`

**URL Examples**:

**External APIs**:
```
https://api.stripe.com/v1/charges
https://api.sendgrid.com/v3/mail/send
https://api.slack.com/api/chat.postMessage
https://jsonplaceholder.typicode.com/posts
```

**Internal APIs**:
```
https://erp.company.com/api/resource/Sales Order
{{internal_api_base}}/api/method/custom_app.api.get_data
http://localhost:8000/api/webhook
```

**With Parameters**:
```
https://api.service.com/search?q={{search_term}}&limit=10
https://api.weather.com/forecast?city={{city}}&units=metric
{{api_base}}/users/{{user_id}}/orders?status=pending
```

**Best Practices**:
- Always use HTTPS for sensitive data
- Store base URLs in variables for easy maintenance
- Validate URL format before using
- URL encode special characters in parameters
- Use environment-specific URLs (dev, staging, prod)

### 2. Method
**Field Type**: Select (Dropdown)
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: GET
**Options**: GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, HEAD, OPTIONS

**Description**: The HTTP method for the request.

**Method Descriptions**:

**GET** - Retrieve data
- Used for fetching information
- Should not modify server state
- Parameters sent in URL
- Cacheable
```
Example: Get user details
URL: https://api.example.com/users/123
```

**POST** - Create new resource
- Send data in request body
- Creates new records
- Not idempotent
```
Example: Create new order
URL: https://api.example.com/orders
Body: {"product": "ABC", "quantity": 5}
```

**PUT** - Update/Replace entire resource
- Replaces complete resource
- Idempotent operation
```
Example: Update user profile
URL: https://api.example.com/users/123
Body: Complete user object
```

**PATCH** - Partial update
- Updates specific fields
- More efficient than PUT
```
Example: Update order status
URL: https://api.example.com/orders/456
Body: {"status": "shipped"}
```

**DELETE** - Remove resource
- Deletes specified resource
- Usually returns 204 No Content
```
Example: Delete a record
URL: https://api.example.com/records/789
```

**HEAD** - Get headers only
- Like GET but without body
- Used for checking existence
```
Example: Check if file exists
URL: https://api.example.com/files/document.pdf
```

**OPTIONS** - Get allowed methods
- Returns permitted operations
- Used for CORS preflight
```
Example: Check API capabilities
URL: https://api.example.com/resource
```

### 3. Payload (json)
**Field Type**: Auto-Complete Custom Component
**Required**: Yes (can be empty object {})
**Default**: {}

**Description**: The JSON data to send with the request (for POST, PUT, PATCH).

**How It Works**:
- Must be valid JSON format
- Sent in request body
- Not used for GET requests (typically)
- Supports nested objects and arrays

**Variable Usage in Payload**:
```json
{
  "customer_id": "{{doc.customer}}",
  "order_total": {{doc.grand_total}},
  "items": {{items_array}},
  "metadata": {
    "workflow": "{{workflow_name}}",
    "triggered_by": "{{trigger_user_id}}",
    "timestamp": "{{current_timestamp}}"
  }
}
```

**Common Payload Examples**:

**Create Customer**:
```json
{
  "name": "{{customer_name}}",
  "email": "{{customer_email}}",
  "phone": "{{customer_phone}}",
  "address": {
    "street": "{{address_line1}}",
    "city": "{{city}}",
    "country": "{{country}}"
  }
}
```

**Send Notification**:
```json
{
  "to": ["{{recipient_email}}"],
  "subject": "Order {{order_id}} Confirmed",
  "body": "Your order has been confirmed and will be delivered by {{delivery_date}}",
  "template_id": "order_confirmation",
  "variables": {
    "order_number": "{{order_id}}",
    "amount": {{order_total}}
  }
}
```

**Update Status**:
```json
{
  "id": "{{record_id}}",
  "status": "{{new_status}}",
  "updated_by": "{{user}}",
  "notes": "{{status_notes}}"
}
```

**Complex Nested Structure**:
```json
{
  "transaction": {
    "type": "payment",
    "amount": {{amount}},
    "currency": "{{currency}}",
    "customer": {
      "id": "{{customer_id}}",
      "email": "{{email}}"
    },
    "line_items": {{line_items_json}},
    "metadata": {
      "order_id": "{{order_id}}",
      "invoice_id": "{{invoice_id}}"
    }
  }
}
```

### 4. Headers
**Field Type**: Auto-Complete Custom Component
**Required**: No
**Default**: {}

**Description**: HTTP headers to send with the request.

**Common Headers**:

**Authentication**:
```json
{
  "Authorization": "Bearer {{api_token}}",
  "X-API-Key": "{{api_key}}",
  "X-Auth-Token": "{{auth_token}}"
}
```

**Content Type**:
```json
{
  "Content-Type": "application/json",
  "Accept": "application/json",
  "Accept-Language": "en-US"
}
```

**Custom Headers**:
```json
{
  "X-Request-ID": "{{request_uuid}}",
  "X-Client-ID": "{{client_id}}",
  "X-Webhook-Signature": "{{computed_signature}}"
}
```

**Complete Example**:
```json
{
  "Authorization": "Bearer {{access_token}}",
  "Content-Type": "application/json",
  "Accept": "application/json",
  "X-API-Version": "2.0",
  "X-Request-Source": "Workflow Automation",
  "User-Agent": "FunnelWorkflow/1.0"
}
```

### 5. Response Variable
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: Variable name to store the API response for use in subsequent nodes.

**How It Works**:
- Response is stored as JSON object
- Can be accessed in later nodes
- Includes status code, headers, and body
- Variable name should be descriptive

**Variable Naming**:
- Use descriptive names: `customer_data`, `payment_response`
- Avoid spaces and special characters
- Use underscore for multi-word: `order_details`
- Consider prefixing: `api_user_data`, `ext_inventory`

**Response Structure**:
```javascript
{
  "status_code": 200,
  "headers": {
    "content-type": "application/json",
    "x-request-id": "abc123"
  },
  "body": {
    // Actual API response data
  },
  "success": true
}
```

**Accessing Response Data**:
If response variable is `api_response`:
- Full response: `{{api_response}}`
- Status code: `{{api_response.status_code}}`
- Response body: `{{api_response.body}}`
- Specific field: `{{api_response.body.user.email}}`
- Headers: `{{api_response.headers}}`

## Common API Integration Examples

### 1. Payment Processing
**Stripe Payment**:
```
URL: https://api.stripe.com/v1/charges
Method: POST
Headers: {
  "Authorization": "Bearer {{stripe_secret_key}}"
}
Payload: {
  "amount": {{amount_in_cents}},
  "currency": "usd",
  "source": "{{payment_token}}",
  "description": "Order {{order_id}}"
}
Response Variable: payment_result
```

### 2. Email Service
**SendGrid Email**:
```
URL: https://api.sendgrid.com/v3/mail/send
Method: POST
Headers: {
  "Authorization": "Bearer {{sendgrid_api_key}}",
  "Content-Type": "application/json"
}
Payload: {
  "personalizations": [{
    "to": [{"email": "{{recipient}}"}]
  }],
  "from": {"email": "noreply@company.com"},
  "subject": "{{email_subject}}",
  "content": [{
    "type": "text/html",
    "value": "{{email_body}}"
  }]
}
Response Variable: email_status
```

### 3. SMS Notification
**Twilio SMS**:
```
URL: https://api.twilio.com/2010-04-01/Accounts/{{account_sid}}/Messages.json
Method: POST
Headers: {
  "Authorization": "Basic {{base64_auth}}"
}
Payload: {
  "From": "{{twilio_phone}}",
  "To": "{{recipient_phone}}",
  "Body": "{{sms_message}}"
}
Response Variable: sms_result
```

### 4. CRM Integration
**HubSpot Contact**:
```
URL: https://api.hubapi.com/contacts/v1/contact
Method: POST
Headers: {
  "Authorization": "Bearer {{hubspot_token}}",
  "Content-Type": "application/json"
}
Payload: {
  "properties": [
    {"property": "email", "value": "{{email}}"},
    {"property": "firstname", "value": "{{first_name}}"},
    {"property": "lastname", "value": "{{last_name}}"},
    {"property": "company", "value": "{{company}}"}
  ]
}
Response Variable: crm_contact
```

### 5. Webhook Notification
**Generic Webhook**:
```
URL: {{webhook_url}}
Method: POST
Headers: {
  "Content-Type": "application/json",
  "X-Webhook-Secret": "{{webhook_secret}}"
}
Payload: {
  "event": "order.completed",
  "data": {
    "order_id": "{{order_id}}",
    "customer": "{{customer_id}}",
    "total": {{order_total}},
    "items": {{order_items}}
  },
  "timestamp": "{{current_timestamp}}"
}
Response Variable: webhook_response
```

## Error Handling

### Common HTTP Status Codes

**Success Codes (2xx)**:
- 200 OK - Request successful
- 201 Created - Resource created
- 204 No Content - Success with no body

**Client Errors (4xx)**:
- 400 Bad Request - Invalid request
- 401 Unauthorized - Authentication required
- 403 Forbidden - Access denied
- 404 Not Found - Resource doesn't exist
- 429 Too Many Requests - Rate limited

**Server Errors (5xx)**:
- 500 Internal Server Error
- 502 Bad Gateway
- 503 Service Unavailable
- 504 Gateway Timeout

### Error Handling Strategies

1. **Check Status Code**:
```
If api_response.status_code != 200
  → Handle Error
  → Log Error Details
  → Send Alert
```

2. **Retry Logic**:
```
Attempt API Call
If Failed && Retryable Error
  → Wait
  → Retry (Max 3 times)
  → If Still Fails → Error Handler
```

3. **Fallback Options**:
```
Try Primary API
If Failed
  → Try Backup API
  → Or Use Cached Data
  → Or Queue for Later
```

## Best Practices

### 1. Security
- **Never hardcode credentials** - Use variables
- **Use HTTPS** for sensitive data
- **Validate SSL certificates**
- **Implement request signing** when required
- **Rotate API keys** regularly
- **Use OAuth 2.0** when available

### 2. Performance
- **Implement timeouts** (default 30 seconds)
- **Use connection pooling**
- **Cache responses** when appropriate
- **Implement rate limiting**
- **Use pagination** for large datasets
- **Compress large payloads**

### 3. Reliability
- **Add retry logic** for transient failures
- **Log all API calls** for debugging
- **Monitor API health**
- **Have fallback options**
- **Handle partial failures**
- **Validate responses**

### 4. Maintenance
- **Version your APIs**
- **Document all integrations**
- **Use descriptive variable names**
- **Centralize configuration**
- **Test in staging first**
- **Monitor deprecation notices**

## Troubleshooting

### Request Failing
**Possible Causes**:
1. Invalid URL
2. Wrong method
3. Malformed JSON payload
4. Missing required headers
5. Authentication issues
6. Network problems
7. API service down

**Solutions**:
- Validate URL format
- Check method matches API requirements
- Use JSON validator for payload
- Verify all required headers
- Check API credentials
- Test with tools like Postman
- Check API service status

### No Response
**Possible Causes**:
1. Timeout
2. Network blocking
3. Firewall issues
4. Wrong endpoint

**Solutions**:
- Increase timeout
- Check network connectivity
- Verify firewall rules
- Confirm endpoint URL

### Invalid Response
**Possible Causes**:
1. API changed
2. Wrong content type
3. Encoding issues
4. Partial response

**Solutions**:
- Check API documentation
- Verify response format
- Handle encoding properly
- Implement response validation

## Advanced Features

### 1. Authentication Methods

**Bearer Token**:
```
Headers: {
  "Authorization": "Bearer {{token}}"
}
```

**Basic Auth**:
```
Headers: {
  "Authorization": "Basic {{base64(username:password)}}"
}
```

**API Key**:
```
Headers: {
  "X-API-Key": "{{api_key}}"
}
```

**OAuth 2.0**:
```
First: Get access token
Then: Use in Authorization header
```

### 2. Request Signing
Some APIs require request signing:
```javascript
signature = HMAC-SHA256(
  secret_key,
  method + url + timestamp + payload
)
Headers: {
  "X-Signature": "{{signature}}",
  "X-Timestamp": "{{timestamp}}"
}
```

### 3. Pagination Handling
For APIs that return paginated results:
```
Initial: GET /api/data?page=1
Check: response.has_more
Loop: GET /api/data?page={{next_page}}
Aggregate: Combine all results
```

### 4. Rate Limiting
Respect API rate limits:
```
Check: X-RateLimit-Remaining header
If low: Add delay
If exceeded: Wait until reset
Track: Request count per time window
```

## Integration Patterns

### 1. Request-Response
Simple synchronous call:
```
Call API → Process Response → Continue
```

### 2. Polling
Check status repeatedly:
```
Submit Job → Poll Status → When Complete → Get Result
```

### 3. Webhook Callback
Async with callback:
```
Submit Request with Callback URL → Continue
Later: Receive Webhook → Process Result
```

### 4. Batch Processing
Multiple API calls:
```
For Each Item → Call API → Aggregate Results
```

## Limitations

1. **Timeout**: Maximum 5 minutes per request
2. **Payload Size**: Limited to 10MB
3. **Response Size**: Limited to 10MB
4. **No Binary**: JSON/Text only (no file downloads)
5. **No Streaming**: No support for streaming APIs
6. **Rate Limits**: Subject to target API limits

## Related Nodes

- **Exec Python**: For complex API logic
- **Create DocType**: Store API responses
- **Update DocType**: Update with API data
- **Send Notification**: Alert on API results
- **Conditional Node**: Route based on response
- **Retry Node**: Implement retry logic

---

# ChatGPT Message

## Overview
The ChatGPT Message node integrates OpenAI's GPT models into your workflows, enabling AI-powered text generation, analysis, summarization, and intelligent decision-making. This node can process data, generate content, extract information, and provide intelligent responses based on your prompts.

**Category**: Utilities
**Type**: Action Node
**Label**: ChatGPT Message

## When to Use
Use the ChatGPT Message node when you need to:
- Generate dynamic content (emails, messages, documents)
- Analyze and summarize text data
- Extract structured information from unstructured text
- Translate content between languages
- Make intelligent routing decisions
- Generate code or technical documentation
- Answer questions based on provided context
- Classify or categorize data
- Perform sentiment analysis
- Create personalized responses
- Generate creative content

## Configuration Fields

### 1. System Prompt
**Field Type**: Text Area (Multi-line)
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: Sets the AI assistant's role, behavior, and context. This message defines how ChatGPT should respond and what persona or expertise it should embody.

**How It Works**:
- Provides context and instructions to the AI
- Remains consistent across the conversation
- Defines the AI's role and expertise
- Sets tone, style, and constraints

**Best Practices for System Prompts**:

**Professional Assistant**:
```
You are a professional customer service representative for {{company_name}}.
You should be helpful, courteous, and concise. Always maintain a professional
tone and focus on solving the customer's problem efficiently.
```

**Data Analyst**:
```
You are a data analyst expert. Analyze the provided data and return insights
in a structured format. Focus on actionable recommendations and key patterns.
Always support conclusions with data points.
```

**Content Writer**:
```
You are an experienced content writer specializing in {{industry}}.
Create engaging, SEO-friendly content that is informative and easy to read.
Use a {{tone}} tone and target {{audience}} audience.
```

**Technical Expert**:
```
You are a technical expert in {{technology}}. Provide accurate, detailed
technical information. Include code examples where relevant. Explain complex
concepts clearly.
```

**Variable Usage**:
```
You are a {{role}} for {{company_name}} in the {{department}} department.
Follow these guidelines: {{guidelines}}
Use this tone: {{communication_tone}}
Prioritize: {{priorities}}
```

**Advanced System Prompts**:

**With Output Format**:
```
You are a data extraction specialist. Extract information from the provided
text and return it in the following JSON format:
{
  "name": "extracted name",
  "email": "extracted email",
  "phone": "extracted phone",
  "summary": "brief summary"
}
Always return valid JSON. If information is not found, use null.
```

**With Constraints**:
```
You are a helpful assistant with the following constraints:
- Keep responses under {{max_words}} words
- Use only information provided in the context
- Do not make assumptions or add information
- If unsure, say "I don't have enough information"
- Format responses in {{output_format}}
```

### 2. User Prompt
**Field Type**: Text Area (Multi-line)
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The actual question, task, or content for ChatGPT to process. This is where you provide the specific instruction and any relevant data.

**How It Works**:
- Contains the specific task or question
- Can include variables with dynamic data
- Supports multi-line complex prompts
- This is what triggers the AI response

**Variable Usage**:
Build dynamic prompts using workflow variables:
```
Analyze this customer feedback and provide sentiment analysis:
Customer: {{customer_name}}
Feedback: {{feedback_text}}
Date: {{feedback_date}}
Product: {{product_name}}

Please provide:
1. Overall sentiment (positive/negative/neutral)
2. Key concerns mentioned
3. Suggested response
4. Priority level (high/medium/low)
```

**Prompt Examples by Use Case**:

**Email Generation**:
```
Write a professional email to {{customer_name}} regarding their order {{order_id}}.
Order Status: {{order_status}}
Expected Delivery: {{delivery_date}}
Items: {{order_items}}

The email should:
- Thank them for their purchase
- Provide order status update
- Include tracking information if available
- Offer support contact information
```

**Data Summarization**:
```
Summarize the following meeting notes into key action items:

Meeting: {{meeting_title}}
Date: {{meeting_date}}
Attendees: {{attendees}}
Notes: {{meeting_notes}}

Format the summary as:
1. Key Decisions
2. Action Items (with owners)
3. Next Steps
4. Deadlines
```

**Content Classification**:
```
Classify the following support ticket:

Subject: {{ticket_subject}}
Description: {{ticket_description}}
Customer Type: {{customer_type}}

Return classification as:
- Category: [Technical/Billing/General/Urgent]
- Priority: [High/Medium/Low]
- Department: [Support/Sales/Technical/Management]
- Estimated Resolution Time: [hours]
```

**Decision Making**:
```
Based on the following customer data, recommend the best subscription plan:

Current Usage: {{current_usage}}
Budget: {{budget}}
Company Size: {{company_size}}
Requirements: {{requirements}}
Growth Rate: {{growth_rate}}

Available Plans:
{{available_plans}}

Provide recommendation with reasoning.
```

**Code Generation**:
```
Generate a Python function that:
- Takes input: {{input_parameters}}
- Processes: {{processing_requirements}}
- Returns: {{expected_output}}
- Handles these errors: {{error_scenarios}}

Include comments and type hints.
```

**Translation**:
```
Translate the following {{source_language}} text to {{target_language}}:

{{text_to_translate}}

Maintain the professional tone and ensure technical terms are accurately translated.
```

### 3. HTML (Display Element)
**Field Type**: HTML Element
**Required**: No
**Default**: Information text

**Description**: Displays helpful information about using the node, including variable usage examples and best practices.

**Content**: Shows usage instructions and variable syntax examples.

## Variables and Output

### Input Variables
The node can access all workflow variables:
- `{{doc}}` - Current document
- `{{doc.field_name}}` - Document fields
- `{{trigger_user_id}}` - User who triggered workflow
- `{{workflow_name}}` - Current workflow name
- Custom variables from previous nodes
- Arrays and objects from API calls

### Output Variables
The ChatGPT response can be stored in workflow variables:
- Response is stored as text
- Can be parsed if JSON format is requested
- Available for use in subsequent nodes
- Can be used for conditional routing

### Response Handling
```javascript
// If response stored in 'ai_response' variable
{{ai_response}} // Full response text

// If response is JSON (requested in prompt)
{{ai_response.category}} // Access JSON fields
{{ai_response.priority}}
{{ai_response.summary}}
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Customer Service Automation
**System Prompt**:
```
You are a customer service AI for an e-commerce company. Be helpful,
empathetic, and solution-focused.
```

**User Prompt**:
```
Customer Query: {{customer_message}}
Order History: {{order_history}}
Previous Issues: {{previous_tickets}}

Generate an appropriate response that addresses their concern.
```

### 2. Document Generation
**System Prompt**:
```
You are a professional business document writer. Create formal,
well-structured documents.
```

**User Prompt**:
```
Create a project proposal for:
Client: {{client_name}}
Project: {{project_description}}
Budget: {{budget}}
Timeline: {{timeline}}
Deliverables: {{deliverables}}
```

### 3. Data Analysis
**System Prompt**:
```
You are a data analyst. Analyze data and provide actionable insights
in a structured format.
```

**User Prompt**:
```
Analyze this sales data and identify trends:
{{sales_data}}

Provide:
1. Top performing products
2. Seasonal patterns
3. Growth opportunities
4. Risk factors
```

### 4. Content Moderation
**System Prompt**:
```
You are a content moderator. Evaluate content for policy violations
and appropriateness.
```

**User Prompt**:
```
Review this user-generated content:
{{content}}

Check for:
- Inappropriate language
- Spam indicators
- Policy violations
- Required actions

Return: {approved: boolean, reason: string, action: string}
```

### 5. Lead Qualification
**System Prompt**:
```
You are a sales qualification expert. Evaluate leads based on
fit and readiness to buy.
```

**User Prompt**:
```
Evaluate this lead:
Company: {{company_info}}
Interaction History: {{interactions}}
Budget Range: {{budget}}
Requirements: {{requirements}}

Provide:
- Lead Score (1-100)
- Qualification Status (Qualified/Not Qualified/Needs Nurturing)
- Recommended Next Action
```

## Best Practices

### 1. Prompt Engineering
- **Be Specific**: Clear, detailed instructions produce better results
- **Provide Context**: Include relevant background information
- **Set Constraints**: Define limits and boundaries
- **Request Format**: Specify desired output format
- **Use Examples**: Show desired output examples in prompt

### 2. Token Management
- **Monitor Length**: GPT has token limits (4K, 8K, 16K, etc.)
- **Optimize Prompts**: Remove unnecessary information
- **Summary First**: Put most important info at beginning
- **Chunk Large Data**: Break large texts into manageable pieces

### 3. Response Validation
- **Verify Output**: Don't assume response is always correct
- **Handle Errors**: GPT might not always return expected format
- **Fallback Logic**: Have alternatives if response is unsuitable
- **Test Edge Cases**: Test with various input types

### 4. Cost Optimization
- **Cache Responses**: Store and reuse common responses
- **Batch Similar Requests**: Group similar prompts
- **Choose Right Model**: Use appropriate model size
- **Limit Retries**: Set maximum retry attempts

## Advanced Techniques

### 1. Chain of Thought
```
Solve this step by step:
1. First, identify {{problem}}
2. Then, analyze {{factors}}
3. Consider {{constraints}}
4. Finally, provide {{solution}}

Show your reasoning for each step.
```

### 2. Few-Shot Learning
```
Here are examples of the task:
Input: "Great product!" → Output: "Positive"
Input: "Terrible service" → Output: "Negative"
Input: "It's okay" → Output: "Neutral"

Now classify: {{new_input}}
```

### 3. Structured Output
```
Return your response in this exact JSON format:
{
  "status": "success|error",
  "data": {
    "field1": "value",
    "field2": "value"
  },
  "confidence": 0.0-1.0
}
```

### 4. Role Playing
```
You are {{character}} from {{context}}.
Respond to this situation as they would:
{{situation}}

Stay in character and use their typical speech patterns.
```

## Error Handling

### Common Issues

1. **Token Limit Exceeded**
   - Solution: Shorten prompt or split into multiple calls

2. **Invalid JSON Response**
   - Solution: Add explicit JSON validation in prompt

3. **Timeout**
   - Solution: Simplify prompt or increase timeout

4. **Rate Limiting**
   - Solution: Add delays between requests

5. **Unexpected Output**
   - Solution: Refine prompt with more specific instructions

### Error Recovery
```
Try: Generate response with GPT
If fails or invalid:
  → Use fallback template
  → Or route to manual review
  → Log error for analysis
```

## Integration Examples

### With Other Nodes

**Email Workflow**:
```
ChatGPT Message (Generate Email)
→ Send Mail (Send Generated Content)
→ Add Comment (Log AI Generation)
```

**Classification Workflow**:
```
ChatGPT Message (Classify)
→ Conditional Node (Route based on classification)
→ Different paths for each category
```

**Approval Workflow**:
```
ChatGPT Message (Analyze Request)
→ Update DocType (Add AI Analysis)
→ Assign User (Route to Approver)
```

## Performance Tips

1. **Response Caching**: Store common responses
2. **Parallel Processing**: Run multiple prompts simultaneously
3. **Async Execution**: Don't block workflow for long prompts
4. **Batching**: Combine related prompts when possible
5. **Model Selection**: Use smaller models for simple tasks

## Security Considerations

1. **Data Privacy**: Don't send sensitive data to GPT
2. **PII Protection**: Mask personal information
3. **API Key Security**: Store keys securely
4. **Output Validation**: Verify AI responses before using
5. **Audit Logging**: Log all AI interactions

## Limitations

1. **No Internet Access**: GPT can't browse web in this context
2. **Knowledge Cutoff**: Limited to training data date
3. **Token Limits**: Input + output must fit token limit
4. **No Memory**: Each call is independent
5. **Potential Hallucinations**: May generate incorrect information
6. **Cost**: Each API call has associated cost

## Related Nodes

- **ChatGPT Multi Prompt**: For multiple prompts in sequence
- **Text Intent Detection**: For specific intent classification
- **Call API**: For direct OpenAI API calls
- **Conditional Node**: Route based on AI response
- **Update DocType**: Store AI-generated content

# ChatGPT Multi Prompt

## Overview
The ChatGPT Multi Prompt node allows you to send multiple prompts to ChatGPT in sequence, building a conversation or performing multiple AI tasks in a single node. This is useful for complex AI workflows that require iterative processing, data extraction, or multi-step analysis.

**Category**: Utilities
**Type**: Action Node
**Label**: Multi Prompt ChatGpt

## When to Use
Use the ChatGPT Multi Prompt node when you need to:
- Send multiple related prompts to ChatGPT in sequence
- Build a conversation with context from previous responses
- Perform multi-step data analysis or extraction
- Generate content that requires multiple iterations
- Extract structured data from unstructured text using follow-up prompts
- Create complex AI workflows with sequential processing
- Refine or elaborate on initial AI responses
- Perform different types of analysis on the same data

## Configuration Fields

### 1. System Prompt
**Field Type**: Text Area (3 rows)
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: Sets the AI assistant's role, behavior, and context that persists across all prompts in the sequence.

**How It Works**:
- Applies to all prompts in the sequence
- Defines the AI's expertise and behavior
- Remains consistent throughout the conversation
- Sets global constraints and formatting rules

**Best Practices**:
```
You are a data analysis expert. For all prompts:
- Provide detailed analysis with supporting data
- Use professional language
- Structure responses clearly
- Maintain consistency across responses
```

**Variable Usage**:
```
You are a {{role}} assistant for {{company_name}}.
Department: {{department}}
Context: {{business_context}}
Focus on: {{analysis_focus}}
```

### 2. Prompts (Edit Grid)
**Field Type**: Edit Grid (Dynamic list)
**Required**: No (but at least one prompt is needed)
**Default**: Empty list

**Description**: A list of prompts to send to ChatGPT sequentially. Each prompt can reference previous responses.

#### User Prompt (within Edit Grid)
**Field Type**: Text Area (3 rows)
**Required**: Yes (for each prompt entry)

**How It Works**:
- Each prompt is sent in sequence
- Later prompts can reference earlier responses
- Maintains conversation context
- Variables can be used in each prompt

**Sequential Prompt Examples**:

**Prompt 1**: Initial Analysis
```
Analyze this customer feedback:
{{feedback_text}}

Identify:
1. Main themes
2. Sentiment
3. Key issues
```

**Prompt 2**: Deep Dive
```
Based on your analysis, provide detailed recommendations for:
- The top 3 issues identified
- Include specific action items
- Estimate implementation effort
```

**Prompt 3**: Summary
```
Create an executive summary of your analysis and recommendations.
Keep it under 200 words and focus on business impact.
```

**Variable Usage in Prompts**:
```
Prompt 1: Analyze {{data_type}} for {{period}}
Prompt 2: Compare with {{benchmark}} and identify gaps
Prompt 3: Generate report in {{format}} format
```

### 3. HTML Display
**Field Type**: HTML Element
**Content**: `reply is saved in 'chatgpt_reply'`

**Description**: Informational text showing where the response is stored.

### 4. Extract Data
**Field Type**: Checkbox
**Required**: No
**Default**: False

**Description**: Enable structured data extraction from the AI responses.

**How It Works**:
- When checked, reveals the Extraction Prompt field
- Processes all responses to extract structured JSON data
- Useful for converting narrative responses to structured format
- Stores extracted data in separate variable

**Use Cases**:
- Extract entities from text
- Convert analysis to structured format
- Parse specific data points from responses
- Create JSON objects from narrative

### 5. Extraction Prompt (Conditional)
**Field Type**: Text Area (3 rows)
**Required**: Yes (when Extract Data is checked)
**Visible When**: Extract Data = true
**Validation**: Must contain the word "json"

**Description**: Defines how to extract structured data from the ChatGPT responses.

**How It Works**:
- Applied after all prompts are complete
- Extracts structured data from the conversation
- Must request JSON format output
- Can reference all previous responses

**Extraction Examples**:

**Customer Data Extraction**:
```
From the conversation above, extract the following in JSON format:
{
  "customer_name": "",
  "issues": [],
  "sentiment": "",
  "priority": "",
  "recommended_actions": []
}
```

**Analysis Summary**:
```
Convert the analysis into this JSON structure:
{
  "summary": "brief summary",
  "key_findings": ["finding1", "finding2"],
  "metrics": {
    "score": 0,
    "confidence": 0
  },
  "next_steps": []
}
```

**Entity Extraction**:
```
Extract all mentioned entities in JSON format:
{
  "people": [],
  "companies": [],
  "products": [],
  "locations": [],
  "dates": [],
  "amounts": []
}
```

### 6. HTML Display (Conditional)
**Field Type**: HTML Element
**Visible When**: Extract Data = true
**Content**: `json response is saved in 'chatgpt_response'`

**Description**: Shows where the extracted JSON data is stored.

## Variables and Output

### Input Variables
All workflow variables are accessible:
- `{{doc}}` - Current document
- `{{doc.field_name}}` - Document fields
- Custom variables from previous nodes
- Arrays and objects for iteration

### Output Variables

#### Without Data Extraction
- **`chatgpt_reply`**: Contains the final response text from all prompts

#### With Data Extraction
- **`chatgpt_reply`**: Contains the conversation text
- **`chatgpt_response`**: Contains the extracted JSON data

### Accessing Responses
```javascript
// Text response
{{chatgpt_reply}}

// Extracted JSON (if enabled)
{{chatgpt_response.customer_name}}
{{chatgpt_response.issues[0]}}
{{chatgpt_response.metrics.score}}
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Progressive Analysis
**Scenario**: Analyze data with increasing detail

**Prompts**:
1. "Provide overview of sales data: {{sales_data}}"
2. "Identify top 3 trends from your analysis"
3. "For each trend, provide actionable recommendations"
4. "Create implementation timeline for recommendations"

### 2. Content Generation Pipeline
**Scenario**: Create multi-format content

**Prompts**:
1. "Write a blog post about {{topic}}"
2. "Create 5 social media posts from the blog content"
3. "Generate email newsletter version"
4. "Extract key quotes for graphics"

### 3. Data Processing Workflow
**Scenario**: Process and structure unstructured data

**Prompts**:
1. "Summarize this document: {{document}}"
2. "Extract all mentioned dates and events"
3. "Identify all stakeholders and their roles"
4. "Create action items from the document"

**With Extraction**: Convert to JSON structure

### 4. Customer Support Analysis
**System Prompt**:
```
You are a customer support analyst. Analyze tickets for patterns and solutions.
```

**Prompts**:
1. "Categorize this support ticket: {{ticket_content}}"
2. "Identify root cause of the issue"
3. "Suggest solution based on similar past tickets"
4. "Draft response to customer"

**Extraction Prompt**:
```
Extract ticket analysis in JSON format:
{
  "category": "",
  "root_cause": "",
  "solution": "",
  "priority": "",
  "estimated_time": ""
}
```

### 5. Document Review Process
**Prompts**:
1. "Review this contract for key terms: {{contract}}"
2. "Identify any risks or concerns"
3. "Compare with standard template and note differences"
4. "Provide recommendations for negotiation"

## Best Practices

### 1. Prompt Sequencing
- **Build Context**: Each prompt should build on previous ones
- **Clear References**: Reference previous responses explicitly
- **Logical Flow**: Order prompts from general to specific
- **Avoid Redundancy**: Don't repeat questions

### 2. System Prompt Design
- **Consistent Voice**: Define personality once
- **Global Rules**: Set formatting and constraints
- **Role Definition**: Establish expertise
- **Output Standards**: Define quality expectations

### 3. Data Extraction
- **Clear Schema**: Define exact JSON structure
- **Validation**: Include format requirements
- **Completeness**: Request all needed fields
- **Fallbacks**: Handle missing data gracefully

### 4. Performance Optimization
- **Prompt Efficiency**: Combine related queries
- **Token Management**: Monitor total token usage
- **Response Caching**: Store and reuse when possible
- **Error Handling**: Plan for API failures

## Advanced Techniques

### 1. Conversation Building
```
Prompt 1: "Analyze the problem: {{problem}}"
Prompt 2: "Based on your analysis, what are the root causes?"
Prompt 3: "For each root cause, suggest solutions"
Prompt 4: "Rank solutions by feasibility and impact"
```

### 2. Iterative Refinement
```
Prompt 1: "Generate initial draft: {{requirements}}"
Prompt 2: "Improve the draft focusing on clarity"
Prompt 3: "Add specific examples and data"
Prompt 4: "Create final polished version"
```

### 3. Multi-Perspective Analysis
```
Prompt 1: "Analyze from customer perspective"
Prompt 2: "Analyze from business perspective"
Prompt 3: "Analyze from technical perspective"
Prompt 4: "Synthesize all perspectives"
```

### 4. Validation Chain
```
Prompt 1: "Process this data: {{input}}"
Prompt 2: "Verify accuracy of processing"
Prompt 3: "Identify any inconsistencies"
Prompt 4: "Provide confidence score"
```

## Error Handling

### Common Issues

1. **Token Limit Exceeded**
   - Solution: Reduce number of prompts or shorten content

2. **Context Lost Between Prompts**
   - Solution: Reference previous responses explicitly

3. **Invalid JSON Extraction**
   - Solution: Provide clearer extraction schema

4. **Timeout on Multiple Prompts**
   - Solution: Reduce prompt count or complexity

### Recovery Strategies
```
Try: Execute all prompts
If fails at prompt N:
  → Use responses from prompts 1 to N-1
  → Log failure point
  → Optionally retry from prompt N
```

## Integration Examples

### With Other Nodes

**Document Processing**:
```
Read Document → Multi Prompt Analysis → Update Document → Send Report
```

**Customer Interaction**:
```
Receive Message → Multi Prompt Response → Extract Data → Send Reply
```

**Data Pipeline**:
```
Fetch Data → Multi Prompt Processing → Store Results → Trigger Next
```

## Performance Considerations

1. **Total Processing Time**: Multiple prompts take longer
2. **Token Usage**: Cumulative across all prompts
3. **API Rate Limits**: Multiple calls count toward limits
4. **Context Window**: Total conversation must fit in model context
5. **Cost**: Each prompt incurs API costs

## Limitations

1. **Sequential Only**: Prompts run in order, not parallel
2. **Shared Context**: All prompts share same conversation
3. **No Branching**: Cannot conditionally skip prompts
4. **Token Accumulation**: Each prompt adds to total tokens
5. **Single Model**: All prompts use same model version

## Related Nodes

- **ChatGPT Message**: For single prompt interactions
- **Text Intent Detection**: For classification tasks
- **Call API**: For direct OpenAI API access
- **Conditional Node**: Route based on extracted data
- **Loop Node**: For iterating over multiple items

## Tips for Success

1. **Plan the Conversation**: Map out prompt sequence before building
2. **Test Incrementally**: Test each prompt before adding next
3. **Monitor Token Usage**: Track cumulative token consumption
4. **Use Extraction Wisely**: Only extract when structured data needed
5. **Handle Edge Cases**: Plan for incomplete or unexpected responses
6. **Document Purpose**: Comment on why each prompt is needed
7. **Validate Output**: Verify extracted JSON structure
8. **Consider Alternatives**: Sometimes multiple single prompts are better

---

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_ai_qa_agent.md
# ChatNext Assistant AI Q&A Agent

## Overview
The ChatNext Assistant AI Q&A Agent node enables AI-powered question and answer interactions within the ChatNext assistant interface. This node allows workflows to engage in intelligent conversations with users, gather information, provide assistance, and guide users through complex processes using AI agents.

**Category**: ChatNext Assistant
**Type**: Action Node (can also be used as Awaiting Action)
**Label**: Assistant QA Agent

## When to Use
Use the ChatNext Assistant AI Q&A Agent node when you need to:
- Create interactive AI-powered conversations in ChatNext
- Gather information from users through natural conversation
- Provide intelligent customer support
- Guide users through complex processes
- Answer questions based on context and knowledge base
- Qualify leads through conversational interfaces
- Perform intelligent data collection
- Create chatbot experiences within workflows
- Handle multi-turn conversations
- Provide personalized assistance based on user context

## Configuration Fields

### 1. Select AI Agent
**Field Type**: Select (Dropdown with search)
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty
**Data Source**: `/api/method/nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task.chat_agent_apis.get_ai_assistant_list`

**Description**: Choose the AI assistant agent to handle the conversation.

**How It Works**:
- Fetches list of available AI assistants from the system
- Each assistant has specific capabilities and training
- The selected assistant determines conversation style and knowledge
- Different assistants may have different permissions and access

**Available Agents**:
The dropdown dynamically loads available AI agents which may include:
- General Support Agent
- Technical Support Specialist
- Sales Assistant
- HR Assistant
- Finance Assistant
- Custom trained agents

**Selection Considerations**:
- Choose agent based on conversation purpose
- Consider agent's training and expertise
- Match agent to user's expected needs
- Check agent availability and permissions

**Variable Usage**:
```
{{agent_id}}
{{default_assistant}}
{{department_agent}}
```

### 2. First Message To Send AI (optional)
**Field Type**: Auto-Complete Custom Component
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The initial message to send to the user when the conversation starts. This sets the context and tone for the interaction.

**How It Works**:
- Sent automatically when conversation begins
- Can include variables for personalization
- Sets user expectations
- Provides context for the conversation

**Variable Usage**:
Build dynamic greetings using variables:
```
Hello {{user_name}}, I'm here to help you with {{issue_type}}.
Based on your account, I can see you're inquiring about {{product_name}}.
How can I assist you today?
```

**Message Examples**:

**Customer Support**:
```
Hi {{customer_name}}! I'm your AI assistant for today.
I can help you with:
- Order status and tracking
- Product information
- Technical support
- Returns and refunds

What would you like help with?
```

**Lead Qualification**:
```
Welcome! I'm here to understand your needs better.
Could you tell me about your current challenges with {{service_area}}?
```

**Technical Assistance**:
```
Hello! I'm the technical support AI.
I see you're experiencing issues with {{system_name}}.
Let me help you troubleshoot this step by step.
```

**HR Onboarding**:
```
Welcome to {{company_name}}, {{employee_name}}!
I'm here to guide you through the onboarding process.
Let's start with getting your workspace set up.
```

**Best Practices**:
- Keep initial message welcoming and clear
- State the AI's purpose and capabilities
- Include relevant context from workflow
- Set expectations for the conversation
- Use professional but friendly tone

### 3. Variable To Store Output
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: The variable name where the conversation output and collected data will be stored.

**How It Works**:
- Stores the complete conversation transcript
- Includes user responses and AI messages
- May contain extracted data and insights
- Available for use in subsequent workflow nodes

**Naming Conventions**:
- Use descriptive names: `support_conversation`, `lead_qualification`
- Avoid spaces and special characters
- Consider prefixing: `ai_conversation_data`
- Be consistent across workflows

**Output Structure**:
The stored variable typically contains:
```javascript
{
  "conversation": [
    {
      "role": "assistant",
      "message": "Hello, how can I help?",
      "timestamp": "2024-01-15T10:00:00Z"
    },
    {
      "role": "user",
      "message": "I need help with my order",
      "timestamp": "2024-01-15T10:00:30Z"
    }
  ],
  "extracted_data": {
    "issue_type": "order_inquiry",
    "order_number": "ORD-12345",
    "sentiment": "neutral"
  },
  "summary": "Customer inquired about order status",
  "resolution": "Provided tracking information",
  "duration": 300,
  "messages_count": 8
}
```

## Variables Available

### Input Variables
The node can access all workflow variables:
- `{{doc}}` - Current document context
- `{{user}}` - User information
- `{{trigger_data}}` - Data from workflow trigger
- Custom variables from previous nodes
- Session and context data

### Output Variables
Stored in the specified variable name:
- **conversation**: Full conversation transcript
- **extracted_data**: Structured data from conversation
- **summary**: AI-generated conversation summary
- **user_intent**: Identified user intent
- **resolution_status**: How the conversation ended
- **follow_up_required**: Boolean for follow-up needs

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Customer Support Automation
**Agent**: Customer Support AI
**First Message**:
```
Hello! I'm here to help with your support request.
Your ticket #{{ticket_id}} has been created.
Please describe your issue in detail.
```
**Variable**: customer_support_data

**Workflow**:
1. Receive support request
2. Start AI conversation
3. Gather issue details
4. Provide solutions or escalate
5. Log conversation

### 2. Lead Qualification
**Agent**: Sales Assistant AI
**First Message**:
```
Hi {{lead_name}}, thanks for your interest in our {{product_category}}.
I'd like to understand your needs better to recommend the best solution.
What's your primary use case?
```
**Variable**: lead_qualification_data

**Process**:
- Gather requirements
- Assess budget and timeline
- Identify decision makers
- Score and qualify lead
- Route to appropriate sales team

### 3. Employee Onboarding
**Agent**: HR Assistant AI
**First Message**:
```
Welcome to {{company_name}}, {{new_employee}}!
I'll help you complete your onboarding.
Let's start with your personal information verification.
```
**Variable**: onboarding_conversation

**Steps**:
- Verify personal details
- Explain company policies
- Guide through forms
- Answer questions
- Schedule orientations

### 4. Technical Troubleshooting
**Agent**: Technical Support AI
**First Message**:
```
I'll help diagnose your technical issue.
System: {{system_name}}
Error: {{error_code}}
When did this issue first occur?
```
**Variable**: troubleshooting_session

### 5. Product Recommendations
**Agent**: Shopping Assistant AI
**First Message**:
```
Hi! I'll help you find the perfect {{product_type}}.
What features are most important to you?
```
**Variable**: product_recommendation_data

## Best Practices

### 1. Agent Selection
- **Match Expertise**: Choose agent trained for the task
- **Consider Context**: Use appropriate agent for user type
- **Check Availability**: Ensure agent is active
- **Test Thoroughly**: Verify agent responses

### 2. First Message Design
- **Be Welcoming**: Start with friendly greeting
- **Set Context**: Explain purpose clearly
- **Guide User**: Suggest next steps
- **Include Personalization**: Use available user data

### 3. Variable Management
- **Clear Naming**: Use descriptive variable names
- **Structure Data**: Organize output for easy access
- **Handle Errors**: Plan for conversation failures
- **Extract Key Data**: Parse important information

### 4. Conversation Flow
- **Natural Language**: Keep interactions conversational
- **Progressive Disclosure**: Don't overwhelm with questions
- **Confirmation**: Verify important information
- **Graceful Exit**: Provide clear conclusion

## Advanced Configuration

### Dynamic Agent Selection
Use variables to select agents dynamically:
```
Agent: {{department}}_support_agent
Agent: {{language}}_assistant
Agent: {{skill_level}}_technical_ai
```

### Contextual First Messages
Build context-aware greetings:
```
{{time_greeting}}, {{user.name}}!
{{previous_interaction ? "Welcome back" : "Nice to meet you"}}!
I see you're calling about {{last_topic || "getting started"}}.
```

### Multi-Language Support
Configure for different languages:
```
First Message: {{user.language == "es" ? "Hola" : "Hello"}}...
Agent: multilingual_support_ai
```

## Integration Examples

### With Other ChatNext Nodes
```
AI Q&A Agent → Show Document → Get Confirmation → Process Request
```

### With Data Nodes
```

```

### With Conditional Logic
```
AI Q&A Agent → Evaluate Intent → Route to Department → Assign Agent
```

## Error Handling

### Common Issues

1. **Agent Not Available**
   - Solution: Have fallback agent configured
   - Check agent service status

2. **Conversation Timeout**
   - Solution: Set appropriate timeout values
   - Implement re-engagement logic

3. **Invalid User Input**
   - Solution: AI should handle gracefully
   - Guide user to valid inputs

4. **Context Loss**
   - Solution: Maintain session state
   - Refresh context periodically

### Recovery Strategies
```
Try: Start AI conversation
If agent unavailable:
  → Use fallback agent
  → Or route to human agent
If timeout:
  → Save partial data
  → Offer to continue later
```

## Performance Considerations

1. **Response Time**: AI responses may take 1-3 seconds
2. **Concurrent Conversations**: Limited by AI service capacity
3. **Context Window**: Long conversations may exceed limits
4. **Token Usage**: Each message consumes AI tokens
5. **Session Management**: Active sessions consume resources

## Security and Privacy

1. **Data Handling**: Conversations may contain sensitive information
2. **User Authentication**: Verify user identity when needed
3. **PII Protection**: Mask sensitive data in logs
4. **Compliance**: Ensure GDPR/privacy compliance
5. **Audit Trail**: Log conversations appropriately

## Limitations

1. **No Voice Support**: Text-based conversations only
2. **Context Limits**: Long conversations may lose context
3. **Real-time Constraints**: Not suitable for instant responses
4. **Language Support**: Limited to AI agent's training
5. **Complex Logic**: May struggle with complex business rules

## Related Nodes

- **ChatNext Assistant Message**: For one-way messages
- **ChatNext Assistant Text Question**: For simple text input
- **ChatNext Assistant Multi Actions**: For action selection
- **ChatNext Assistant Form Question**: For structured data collection
- **ChatGPT Message**: For backend AI processing

## Tips for Success

1. **Train Agents Well**: Provide good training data
2. **Test Conversations**: Run through common scenarios
3. **Monitor Performance**: Track conversation success rates
4. **Iterate and Improve**: Refine based on user feedback
5. **Provide Fallbacks**: Always have backup options
6. **Set Clear Expectations**: Tell users it's AI
7. **Keep Context Relevant**: Don't overload with information
8. **Handle Edge Cases**: Plan for unexpected inputs

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_close_modal.md
# ChatNext Assistant Close Modal

## Overview
The ChatNext Assistant Close Modal node allows workflows to programmatically close modal windows or dialog boxes in the ChatNext assistant interface. This simple but essential node helps manage the user interface flow by closing popups, forms, or modal dialogs after completing actions.

**Category**: ChatNext Assistant
**Type**: Action Node
**Label**: Close Assistant Modal

## When to Use
Use the ChatNext Assistant Close Modal node when you need to:
- Close a modal after form submission
- Dismiss dialogs after user confirmation
- Close popup windows after completing an action
- Clean up the interface after workflow completion
- Return users to the main interface
- Close error or success message modals
- Manage multi-step modal workflows
- Ensure proper UI state management
- Close modals before opening new ones
- Implement cancel or exit functionality

## Configuration Fields

### Submit Button Only
**Field Type**: Button
**Action**: Submit
**Theme**: Primary

**Description**: This node has no configurable fields - it simply contains a submit button to save the node configuration. The actual modal closing happens automatically when this node executes in the workflow.

**How It Works**:
- The node executes immediately when reached in the workflow
- Sends a close command to any open modal in ChatNext
- The modal closes smoothly with appropriate animation
- Returns focus to the main interface
- No user interaction required

## Variables Available

### Input Variables
The node can access all workflow variables but doesn't require any specific inputs:
- `{{modal_id}}` - Optional: specific modal to close (if multiple)
- `{{close_reason}}` - Optional: reason for closing
- All standard workflow variables remain accessible

### Output Variables
This node doesn't create output variables, but it:
- Returns success/failure status
- May trigger modal close events
- Updates UI state

## Common Use Cases

### 1. After Form Submission
**Scenario**: Close modal after user submits a form
```
Show Form Modal → User Fills Form → Process Data → Close Modal → Show Success Message
```

**Workflow**:
1. Display form in modal
2. User completes and submits
3. Process the submitted data
4. Close the modal
5. Show confirmation

### 2. Cancel Operation
**Scenario**: User cancels an operation
```
Show Options Modal → User Clicks Cancel → Close Modal → Return to Main
```

**Implementation**:
- Detect cancel action
- Close modal immediately
- No data processing needed
- Restore previous state

### 3. Multi-Step Wizard Completion
**Scenario**: Close modal after multi-step process
```
Step 1 Modal → Step 2 Modal → Step 3 Modal → Complete → Close All Modals
```

**Process**:
- Guide through steps
- Collect all data
- Process completion
- Close modal stack
- Return to dashboard

### 4. Error Recovery
**Scenario**: Close error modal after acknowledgment
```
Action Fails → Show Error Modal → User Acknowledges → Close Modal → Retry Option
```

### 5. Success Confirmation
**Scenario**: Auto-close success modal
```
Complete Action → Show Success Modal → Wait 3 Seconds → Close Modal
```

### 6. Quick Actions
**Scenario**: Close modal after quick action
```
Show Quick Action Modal → Execute Action → Close Modal Immediately
```

## Best Practices

### 1. Timing
- **After Completion**: Close only after action is complete
- **Save First**: Ensure data is saved before closing
- **User Feedback**: Provide feedback before closing
- **Smooth Transition**: Allow time for user to see results

### 2. User Experience
- **Confirmation**: Confirm important actions before closing
- **Animation**: Use smooth close animations
- **Focus Management**: Return focus appropriately
- **State Preservation**: Save state if needed

### 3. Error Handling
- **Validate First**: Check data before closing
- **Error States**: Don't close on errors unless intended
- **Recovery Options**: Provide ways to reopen if needed
- **Logging**: Log modal close events

### 4. Modal Stack Management
- **Single Close**: Close one modal at a time
- **Stack Awareness**: Know if multiple modals are open
- **Order Matters**: Close in correct order
- **Clean Up**: Ensure all modals are properly closed

## Integration Examples

### With Form Nodes
```
ChatNext Form Question → Validate Input → Save Data → Close Modal → Show Confirmation
```

### With Action Nodes
```
Show Actions Modal → User Selects Action → Execute Action → Close Modal → Update UI
```

### With Error Handling
```
Try Action → If Success: Close Modal → If Error: Keep Modal Open with Error
```

### With Conditional Logic
```
Check Condition → If Complete: Close Modal → If Incomplete: Show Next Step
```

## Advanced Usage

### Conditional Closing
While the node itself doesn't have conditions, use workflow logic:
```
If form.isValid:
  → Close Modal
Else:
  → Show Validation Errors (keep modal open)
```

### Delayed Closing
Combine with wait nodes:
```
Show Success Message → Wait 2 seconds → Close Modal
```

### Multiple Modal Management
```
Close Modal 1 → Close Modal 2 → Close Modal 3 (in sequence)
```

### With State Management
```
Save Modal State → Close Modal → (Later) Restore Modal with Saved State
```

## Common Patterns

### 1. Form Submit Pattern
```
1. Display Form Modal
2. User Fills Information
3. Validate Input
4. Process Data
5. Close Modal ← This Node
6. Show Success Toast
```

### 2. Confirmation Pattern
```
1. Show Confirmation Modal
2. User Confirms/Cancels
3. If Confirmed: Execute Action
4. Close Modal ← This Node
5. Continue Workflow
```

### 3. Error Recovery Pattern
```
1. Try Action
2. If Error: Show Error Modal
3. User Acknowledges
4. Close Modal ← This Node
5. Offer Retry
```

### 4. Wizard Pattern
```
1. Complete All Steps
2. Process Final Data
3. Close Modal ← This Node
4. Navigate to Results
```

## Troubleshooting

### Modal Not Closing
**Possible Causes**:
1. Modal ID mismatch
2. No modal is currently open
3. JavaScript errors preventing close
4. Modal is blocked by another process

**Solutions**:
- Verify modal is actually open
- Check browser console for errors
- Ensure proper workflow sequence
- Test in different browsers

### Multiple Modals Issue
**Possible Causes**:
1. Multiple modals stacked
2. Closing wrong modal
3. Z-index conflicts

**Solutions**:
- Close modals in reverse order
- Track modal stack properly
- Use unique modal identifiers

### Timing Issues
**Possible Causes**:
1. Closing too quickly
2. Async operations not complete
3. Animation conflicts

**Solutions**:
- Add small delay if needed
- Ensure operations complete first
- Wait for animations to finish

## Performance Considerations

1. **Instant Execution**: Node executes immediately
2. **No Processing**: Minimal resource usage
3. **UI Thread**: Operates on UI thread
4. **Animation Time**: Consider close animation duration
5. **Event Handlers**: May trigger close event handlers

## Limitations

1. **No Configuration**: Cannot customize close behavior
2. **Single Modal**: Closes one modal at a time
3. **No Conditions**: No built-in conditional logic
4. **Simple Action**: Only closes, no other options
5. **UI Dependent**: Requires ChatNext UI context

## Related Nodes

- **ChatNext Assistant Message**: Show messages in modals
- **ChatNext Assistant Form Question**: Create modal forms
- **ChatNext Assistant Multi Actions**: Show action modals
- **ChatNext Assistant Show File**: Display files in modals
- **ChatNext Assistant UI Action**: Trigger other UI actions

## Tips for Success

1. **Test Flow**: Test complete modal flow
2. **User Feedback**: Provide clear feedback before closing
3. **Error Handling**: Don't close on errors
4. **State Management**: Track modal state properly
5. **Accessibility**: Ensure keyboard navigation works
6. **Mobile Testing**: Test on mobile devices
7. **Browser Compatibility**: Test across browsers
8. **Clean Up**: Always close modals properly

## Security Considerations

1. **Data Safety**: Ensure data is saved before closing
2. **User Confirmation**: Confirm destructive actions
3. **Session State**: Maintain session integrity
4. **Audit Trail**: Log important modal closes
5. **Permission Checks**: Verify user can close modal

## Examples by Use Case

### E-commerce Checkout
```
Show Cart Modal → Review Items → Process Payment → Close Modal → Show Order Confirmation
```

### Document Upload
```
Open Upload Modal → Select Files → Upload Complete → Close Modal → Show File List
```

### User Settings
```
Open Settings Modal → Change Settings → Save → Close Modal → Apply Changes
```

### Search Interface
```
Open Search Modal → Enter Criteria → Show Results → Select Result → Close Modal
```

### Feedback Collection
```
Show Feedback Modal → Collect Rating → Get Comments → Submit → Close Modal → Thank User
```

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_document_approval.md
# ChatNext Assistant Document Approval

## ⚠️ DEPRECATED NODE
**Warning**: This node is deprecated and no longer maintained. While it may still function, it is recommended to use alternative approval workflow nodes instead.

## Overview
The ChatNext Assistant Document Approval node was designed to create approval workflows for documents through the ChatNext assistant interface. It allows/allowed documents to be presented for approval or rejection by specific users or roles.

**Category**: ChatNext Assistant
**Type**: Action Node (Awaiting)
**Label**: Ask Doc Approval
**Status**: DEPRECATED - DO NOT USE

## Why Deprecated
This node has been deprecated because:
- Better approval mechanisms are available
- Maintenance has been discontinued
- May have compatibility issues with newer versions
- Alternative nodes provide enhanced functionality

## Original Purpose
This node was intended to:
- Present documents for approval/rejection
- Route approvals to specific users or roles
- Display document fields for review
- Capture approval decisions with comments
- Support both user-specific and role-based approvals
- Integrate with ChatNext Assistant for interactive approvals

## Configuration Fields (Historical Reference)

### 1. Select Doctype
**Field Type**: Select (Dropdown)
**Required**: Yes
**Purpose**: Selected the document type requiring approval

### 2. Doc Name
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes
**Purpose**: Specified the document to be approved

### 3. Get Approval from Role
**Field Type**: Checkbox
**Required**: No
**Default**: False
**Purpose**: Toggle between user-specific and role-based approval

### 4. User Id (Conditional)
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes (when role_approval is false)
**Visible When**: Get Approval from Role = False
**Purpose**: Specific user who needs to approve

### 5. Role Name (Conditional)
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes (when role_approval is true)
**Visible When**: Get Approval from Role = True
**Purpose**: Role that can approve the document

### 6. Required Fields
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: No
**Purpose**: Fields to display during approval

### 7. Open ChatNext Assistant
**Field Type**: Checkbox
**Required**: No
**Default**: False
**Purpose**: Whether to open ChatNext Assistant interface

## Output Handles
This node had multiple output paths:
- **approved**: Continues when document is approved (bottom handle, label "A")
- **rejected**: Continues when document is rejected (right handle, label "R")

## Recommended Alternatives

### For Document Approvals
1. **Use Custom Approval Workflows**: Build approval logic with conditional nodes
2. **ChatNext Assistant Multi Actions**: Present approve/reject as actions
3. **Form-based Approvals**: Use form questions for approval data
4. **Role-based Routing**: Use conditional logic with user roles

### Modern Approval Pattern
Instead of this deprecated node, use:
```
1. Fetch Document →
2. ChatNext Multi Actions (Approve/Reject) →
3. Conditional Node (Route based on decision) →
4. Update Document Status
```

## Migration Guide

### If You're Currently Using This Node

#### Step 1: Identify Usage
Find all workflows using this node:
- Search for "chatnext_assistant_document_approval" in workflows
- Document the approval logic needed

#### Step 2: Replace with Alternative Pattern

**Old Pattern**:
```
Document Approval Node → [Approved Path] or [Rejected Path]
```

**New Pattern**:
```
1. ChatNext Assistant Multi Actions
   - Action 1: Approve
   - Action 2: Reject
   - Action 3: Request More Info
2. Store decision in variable
3. Conditional Node to route based on decision
4. Update document accordingly
```

#### Step 3: Configure New Nodes

**Multi Actions Configuration**:
```
Title: "Document Approval Required"
Message: "Please review document {{doc_name}}"
Actions:
- Label: "Approve", Value: "approved"
- Label: "Reject", Value: "rejected"
- Label: "Request Changes", Value: "changes"
Variable: approval_decision
```

**Conditional Routing**:
```
If {{approval_decision}} == "approved":
  → Update Status to Approved
  → Send Approval Notification
If {{approval_decision}} == "rejected":
  → Update Status to Rejected
  → Send Rejection Notification
```

## Historical Use Cases

### 1. Invoice Approval
Previously used for:
- Presenting invoices for approval
- Routing to finance team
- Capturing approval with comments

### 2. Leave Request Approval
Previously used for:
- Manager approval of leave requests
- HR secondary approval
- Automatic status updates

### 3. Purchase Order Approval
Previously used for:
- Amount-based approval routing
- Multi-level approvals
- Budget verification

## Why Alternative Approaches Are Better

### 1. More Flexibility
- Custom approval flows
- Multiple approval options beyond approve/reject
- Conditional routing based on complex logic

### 2. Better User Experience
- Modern UI components
- Mobile-friendly interfaces
- Rich interaction options

### 3. Enhanced Features
- Comments and attachments
- Delegation capabilities
- Audit trail improvements
- Real-time notifications

## If You Must Use This Node

### Critical Warnings
1. **No Support**: This node receives no updates or bug fixes
2. **Compatibility**: May break with system updates
3. **Security**: May not receive security patches
4. **Performance**: Not optimized for current system

### Minimal Configuration
If absolutely necessary:
1. Select the DocType
2. Enter Doc Name (can use variables like {{doc.name}})
3. Choose between user or role approval
4. Enter User ID or Role Name accordingly
5. List Required Fields (comma-separated)
6. Decide if ChatNext Assistant should open

## Troubleshooting Legacy Implementations

### Common Issues
1. **Node Not Working**: Likely due to deprecated dependencies
2. **Approval Not Routing**: Check output handle connections
3. **User Not Notified**: Notification system may have changed
4. **Fields Not Displaying**: Field API may have been updated

### Debug Steps
1. Check browser console for errors
2. Verify user/role exists and is active
3. Confirm document exists and is accessible
4. Test with simple approval first
5. Consider immediate migration to new pattern

## Future Removal Notice
This node may be completely removed in future versions. Plan migration immediately to avoid workflow disruption.

## Support and Questions
For help migrating from this deprecated node:
1. Review alternative approval patterns
2. Test new implementation thoroughly
3. Document your approval requirements
4. Consult current best practices

## Summary
**DO NOT USE THIS NODE FOR NEW WORKFLOWS**. It exists only for backward compatibility and will be removed. Migrate existing workflows to modern alternatives as soon as possible.
