# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_form_question.md
# Assistant Form Question

## Overview
The Assistant Form Question node allows you to display interactive forms within ChatNext Assistant conversations. This node can present either pre-built forms or dynamically constructed forms to collect structured information from users. It supports various form fields and validation rules, making it ideal for gathering detailed user input in a conversational interface.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Collect structured data from users through forms
- Present multiple input fields in a single interaction
- Validate user input with specific rules
- Create dynamic forms based on variables or conditions
- Gather complex information that requires different field types (text, dropdowns, checkboxes, etc.)
- Implement multi-step data collection processes

## Configuration Fields

### Dynamic Form Settings
- **Dynamic Form**: Checkbox to enable dynamic form creation
  - When enabled: Create forms using JSON configuration
  - When disabled: Select from pre-built forms

### Variable Form Configuration (appears when Dynamic Form is enabled)
- **Is Variable Form**: Checkbox to use variables for form structure
  - When enabled: Form structure comes from a variable
  - When disabled: Form structure defined directly in configuration

### Form Source Selection
**When Dynamic Form is disabled:**
- **Select Form**: Dropdown to choose from existing Microapp Form Widgets
  - Required field
  - Fetches forms from `/api/resource/Microapp%20Form%20Widget`
  - Displays form label and name

**When Dynamic Form and Variable Form are enabled:**
- **Form Io Variable**: Text field specifying the variable containing form JSON
  - Default value: "formio_json"
  - Contains the variable name that holds the form structure

**When Dynamic Form is enabled but Variable Form is disabled:**
- **Form Io Json**: Textarea for direct JSON form configuration
  - Required field
  - HTML format input
  - Define the complete form structure in JSON format

### Data Configuration
- **Pre-fill data (JSON)**: Textarea for initial form data
  - Optional field
  - JSON format to populate form fields with default values
  - Use variables like `{{variable_name}}` to dynamically populate fields

### Response Handling
- **Answer Variable**: Text field (required)
  - Name of the variable to store user's form submission
  - Stores the complete form response as JSON
  - Description: "Name of variable where the answer (JSON) from user to be stored into"

### Data Management
- **Enable Data Update on Submit**: Checkbox
  - When enabled: Allows updating existing data on form submission
  - When disabled: Creates new data entries only

## Variables Usage
This node supports extensive variable usage:

### Input Variables
- `{{form_structure_variable}}`: For dynamic form JSON structure
- `{{prefill_data_variable}}`: For pre-populating form fields
- `{{conditional_values}}`: For conditional field display

### Output Variables
- The specified Answer Variable will contain the form submission data
- Format: JSON object with field names as keys and user inputs as values

### Example Variable Usage
```json
{
  "user_name": "{{customer_name}}",
  "email": "{{customer_email}}",
  "default_status": "{{current_status}}"
}
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Customer Information Collection
```json
{
  "components": [
    {
      "type": "textfield",
      "key": "full_name",
      "label": "Full Name",
      "validate": {"required": true}
    },
    {
      "type": "email",
      "key": "email",
      "label": "Email Address",
      "validate": {"required": true}
    }
  ]
}
```

### 2. Feedback Form
- Collect ratings, comments, and suggestions
- Use dropdowns for categories and text areas for detailed feedback

### 3. Application Forms
- Multi-step application processes
- File uploads for documents
- Conditional fields based on previous answers

### 4. Survey Collection
- Radio buttons for single choice questions
- Checkboxes for multiple selections
- Rating scales for satisfaction surveys

### 5. Dynamic Configuration Forms
- Forms that change based on user type or context
- Conditional field display based on previous selections

## Best Practices

### Form Design
1. **Keep Forms Focused**: Limit forms to essential fields to avoid overwhelming users
2. **Use Clear Labels**: Provide descriptive labels and help text for complex fields
3. **Implement Validation**: Use appropriate validation rules to ensure data quality
4. **Progressive Disclosure**: Use conditional fields to show relevant options only

### Variable Management
1. **Descriptive Variable Names**: Use clear names for answer variables (e.g., "customer_info", "application_data")
2. **Pre-fill Smart Defaults**: Use existing data to reduce user input effort
3. **Validate JSON Structure**: Ensure form JSON is properly formatted before use

### User Experience
1. **Provide Context**: Explain why the form is needed and how data will be used
2. **Show Progress**: For long forms, indicate progress or break into multiple steps
3. **Error Handling**: Provide clear error messages and validation feedback
4. **Mobile Optimization**: Ensure forms work well on different screen sizes

### Data Handling
1. **Secure Sensitive Data**: Be mindful of data privacy and security requirements
2. **Backup Strategies**: Consider enabling data update features for editable forms
3. **Data Validation**: Implement both client-side and server-side validation

## Related Nodes
- **Assistant Text Question**: For simple text input collection
- **Assistant Multi Actions**: For presenting action choices after form submission
- **Assistant Message**: For providing instructions or context before forms
- **Data to Doc**: For saving form responses to documents
- **Trigger Funnel**: For starting other processes based on form data
- **Variable Filter**: For processing form responses before storage
- **Assistant UI Action**: For custom UI interactions with form data

## Technical Notes
- Form structure follows Form.io JSON schema
- Supports all standard HTML5 input types
- Conditional logic available for dynamic field display
- Integrates with Frappe's permission system
- Response data automatically serialized as JSON
- Supports file uploads and media attachments (implementation dependent)

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_message.md
# Assistant Message

## Overview
The Assistant Message node displays text messages within ChatNext Assistant conversations. This node is used to send informational content, instructions, or responses to users. It supports both plain text and HTML formatting, making it versatile for various communication needs in conversational workflows.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Send informational messages to users
- Provide instructions or guidance
- Display dynamic content using variables
- Show formatted text with HTML elements
- Present system responses or notifications
- Give feedback or confirmations
- Display error messages or warnings
- Share status updates or progress information

## Configuration Fields

### Message Content
- **Message**: Textarea (required)
  - The main content to display to the user
  - HTML format input supported
  - Fixed size with 3 rows
  - Supports variable interpolation using `{{variable_name}}`
  - Can contain plain text or HTML markup

### HTML Rendering Options
- **Render as Html**: Checkbox
  - Default: false (renders as plain text)
  - When enabled: Message content is rendered as HTML
  - When disabled: Message content is displayed as plain text
  - **Security Warning**: Shows warning about potential security risks when enabled

### Security Warning Display
- **HTML Warning**: HTML element (conditional)
  - Appears only when "Render as Html" is enabled
  - Displays: "Rendering as Html might be harmful. use this only if necessary."
  - Color: Red text to emphasize the warning
  - Non-persistent, display-only element

## Variables Usage
This node supports extensive variable usage for dynamic content:

### Input Variables
- `{{user_name}}`: Display user's name in messages
- `{{current_time}}`: Show current timestamp
- `{{status}}`: Display process status
- `{{data_value}}`: Show data from previous nodes
- `{{calculation_result}}`: Display computed values

### Example Variable Usage
```
Hello {{user_name}}, your order #{{order_id}} has been {{status}}.
Expected delivery: {{delivery_date}}

Next steps:
- {{next_action}}
- Contact us at {{support_email}}
```

### HTML with Variables
When HTML rendering is enabled:
```html
Welcome {{user_name}}!
Your account balance is: ${{balance}}

  Last login: {{last_login}}
  Status: {{account_status}}

```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Welcome Messages
```
Welcome to our service, {{user_name}}!
We're excited to help you with {{service_type}}.
```

### 2. Status Updates
```
Processing your request...
Current status: {{processing_status}}
Estimated completion: {{eta}}
```

### 3. Error Messages
```
Sorry, we encountered an issue:
{{error_message}}

Please try again or contact support.
```

### 4. Confirmation Messages
```
✅ Success! Your {{action_type}} has been completed.
Reference ID: {{reference_id}}
```

### 5. Instruction Messages
```
Please follow these steps:
1. {{step_1}}
2. {{step_2}}
3. {{step_3}}

Need help? Type "help" or contact {{support_contact}}
```

### 6. Data Display
```html

  Your Information
  Name: {{full_name}}
  Email: {{email}}
  Status: {{status}}

```

## Best Practices

### Content Design
1. **Keep Messages Clear**: Use simple, direct language
2. **Be Concise**: Avoid overly long messages that overwhelm users
3. **Use Consistent Tone**: Match your brand voice and style
4. **Include Context**: Provide relevant information for user understanding

### Variable Usage
1. **Validate Variables**: Ensure variables exist before using them
2. **Provide Fallbacks**: Consider default values for missing variables
3. **Format Appropriately**: Format dates, numbers, and text consistently
4. **Escape Special Characters**: Be careful with user-generated content

### HTML Usage
1. **Use Sparingly**: Only enable HTML when necessary for formatting
2. **Sanitize Content**: Ensure HTML content is safe and properly escaped
3. **Test Thoroughly**: Verify HTML renders correctly across different devices
4. **Accessibility**: Use semantic HTML and consider screen readers

### Security Considerations
1. **Avoid User HTML**: Never allow user input to be rendered as HTML directly
2. **Sanitize Variables**: Clean variables that might contain HTML or scripts
3. **Content Security**: Be cautious with dynamic HTML content from external sources
4. **Regular Review**: Periodically review HTML-enabled messages for security

### User Experience
1. **Progressive Information**: Break complex information into multiple messages
2. **Visual Hierarchy**: Use formatting to emphasize important information
3. **Actionable Content**: Include clear next steps when appropriate
4. **Personalization**: Use variables to personalize the experience

## Related Nodes
- **Assistant Text Question**: For collecting user input after messages
- **Assistant Form Question**: For complex data collection following information display
- **Assistant Multi Actions**: For presenting action options after messages
- **Trigger Funnel**: For starting other processes based on message interactions
- **Variable Filter**: For processing data before displaying in messages
- **Assistant UI Action**: For custom UI interactions
- **Send Message WhatsApp**: For similar messaging on WhatsApp platform
- **Send Mail**: For sending similar content via email

## Technical Notes
- Message content supports HTML formatting when enabled
- Variables are interpolated at runtime
- HTML content should be properly formed and validated
- Security warnings appear automatically when HTML rendering is enabled
- No output variables are created by this node
- Content is delivered through the ChatNext Assistant interface
- Supports emoji and special characters in text mode
- HTML mode requires careful handling of user-generated content

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_multi_actions.md
# Document Actions (ChatNext Assistant Multi Actions)

## Overview
The Document Actions node (also known as ChatNext Assistant Multi Actions) presents users with multiple actionable buttons in the ChatNext Assistant interface. This node allows users to choose from different actions that can be performed on documents, with optional form-based data collection for each action. It creates dynamic workflows where user selections determine the next steps in the process.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Present multiple action choices to users
- Create branching workflows based on user selection
- Collect additional information for specific actions
- Provide document-specific operations
- Implement approval/rejection workflows
- Create dynamic action menus
- Allow users to choose from different processing options
- Implement multi-step decision processes

## Configuration Fields

### Document Context
- **Select Doctype**: Dropdown (required)
  - Selects the document type to work with
  - Fetches from `/api/method/nextai.funnel.apis.fetch_data.get_searched_doc_list`
  - Searchable with 1000 item limit
  - Filters: `doctype=DocType&limit=20.523`

### Document Reference
- **Doc Name**: Text field (required)
  - Variable name or direct reference to the document
  - Supports variable interpolation like `{{doc_name}}`
  - Plain text input format

### User Assignment
- **User ID or User Role**: Text field (required)
  - Specify target user or role for the actions
  - Can be user ID or role name
  - Supports variable values like `{{current_user}}`

- **Do not assign to users**: Checkbox
  - Default: false
  - When enabled: Actions won't be assigned to specific users
  - Useful for system-wide or anonymous actions

### Action Configuration
- **Actions**: Data grid (at least 1 action required)
  - **Action**: Text field (required, 1-100 characters)
    - The button label displayed to users
    - Examples: "Approve", "Reject", "Review", "Process"

### Assistant Integration
- **Open ChatNext Assistant**: Checkbox
  - Default: false
  - When enabled: Actions open in ChatNext Assistant interface
  - Required for form-based data collection

### Form Questions (conditional, appears when "Open ChatNext Assistant" is enabled)
For each action, you can configure form-based data collection:

- **Info required for this action**: Checkbox
  - When enabled: Shows form configuration options
  - Allows collecting additional data before action execution

### Dynamic Form Configuration (when form questions are enabled)
- **Dynamic Form**: Checkbox
  - When enabled: Create forms using JSON
  - When disabled: Select from pre-built forms

**When Dynamic Form is disabled:**
- **Select Form**: Dropdown (required)
  - Choose from existing Microapp Form Widgets
  - Fetches from `/api/resource/Microapp Form Widget`

**When Dynamic Form is enabled:**
- **FormIO Json**: Textarea (required)
  - JSON configuration for the form structure
  - HTML format input
  - Define complete form schema

### Form Data Management
- **Pre-fill data (JSON)**: Textarea
  - JSON data to pre-populate form fields
  - Supports variable interpolation

- **Answer Variable**: Text field (required when form is enabled)
  - Variable name to store form responses
  - Description: "Name of variable where the answer (JSON) from user to be stored into"

- **Enable Data Update on Submit**: Checkbox
  - Allows updating existing data on form submission
  - Default: false

### Internal Configuration (hidden fields)
- **Output Handles Array Field**: Hidden field
  - Default: "buttons"
  - System field for managing output connections

- **Output Handle Key Field**: Hidden field
  - Default: "option"
  - System field for routing user selections

## Variables Usage

### Created Variables
This node automatically creates:
- `selected_option` (string): The action button chosen by the user
- `options_data` (object): Contains data from action execution
  - Keys: Dynamic based on `$data.answer_key`

### Input Variables
- `{{doctype}}`: Document type to operate on
- `{{doc_name}}`: Specific document identifier
- `{{user_id}}`: Target user for actions
- `{{form_data}}`: Pre-fill data for forms
- `{{current_user}}`: Current user context

### Example Variable Usage
```json
{
  "doctype": "{{document_type}}",
  "doc_variable": "{{current_document}}",
  "user_data": "{{assigned_user}}",
  "prefill_data": {
    "reviewer": "{{current_user}}",
    "review_date": "{{today}}"
  }
}
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Document Approval Workflow
```json
{
  "actions": [
    {"option": "Approve"},
    {"option": "Reject"},
    {"option": "Request Changes"}
  ]
}
```

### 2. Order Processing Actions
```json
{
  "actions": [
    {"option": "Process Order"},
    {"option": "Hold for Review"},
    {"option": "Cancel Order"},
    {"option": "Request Information"}
  ]
}
```

### 3. Support Ticket Actions
```json
{
  "actions": [
    {"option": "Resolve Ticket"},
    {"option": "Escalate to Manager"},
    {"option": "Request More Info"},
    {"option": "Transfer to Department"}
  ]
}
```

### 4. Content Review Process
```json
{
  "actions": [
    {"option": "Publish"},
    {"option": "Schedule for Later"},
    {"option": "Return for Edits"},
    {"option": "Archive"}
  ]
}
```

### 5. Quality Control Actions
```json
{
  "actions": [
    {"option": "Pass Quality Check"},
    {"option": "Fail - Minor Issues"},
    {"option": "Fail - Major Issues"},
    {"option": "Needs Re-inspection"}
  ]
}
```

## Best Practices

### Action Design
1. **Clear Action Labels**: Use descriptive, action-oriented button text
2. **Logical Grouping**: Group related actions together
3. **Limit Options**: Don't overwhelm users with too many choices
4. **Consistent Naming**: Use consistent terminology across workflows

### Form Integration
1. **Conditional Forms**: Only request forms for actions that truly need additional input
2. **Smart Pre-filling**: Use variables to reduce user input requirements
3. **Validation**: Implement proper form validation for required fields
4. **User Experience**: Keep forms concise and relevant to the action

### Variable Management
1. **Descriptive Variables**: Use clear variable names for answers
2. **Data Structure**: Plan the structure of collected data carefully
3. **Error Handling**: Consider what happens if variables are missing
4. **State Management**: Track action states across workflow steps

### Workflow Design
1. **Clear Outcomes**: Each action should have a well-defined outcome
2. **Branching Logic**: Plan different paths for different actions
3. **User Feedback**: Provide confirmation messages for completed actions
4. **Rollback Capability**: Consider implementing undo functionality where appropriate

### Security Considerations
1. **Permission Checks**: Ensure users have appropriate permissions for actions
2. **Data Validation**: Validate all form inputs thoroughly
3. **Audit Trail**: Log all actions taken for compliance
4. **Role-based Access**: Use user roles to control available actions

## Related Nodes
- **Assistant Form Question**: For complex form-based data collection
- **Assistant Message**: For providing context before presenting actions
- **Data to Doc**: For saving action results to documents
- **Trigger Funnel**: For starting different processes based on selected actions
- **Variable Filter**: For processing action data
- **Document Event Trigger**: For responding to document state changes
- **Update Doctype**: For modifying documents based on actions
- **Send Notification**: For notifying users of action completion
- **Assistant Text Question**: For simple text collection with actions

## Technical Notes
- Actions create multiple output handles for workflow branching
- Each action can have its own output path in the flow
- Form responses are stored as JSON in specified variables
- Supports both synchronous and asynchronous action processing
- Integrates with Frappe's permission system
- Can handle both document-specific and general actions
- Output handles are dynamically generated based on action configuration
- Supports custom styling and theming for buttons

# Public Assistant Trigger

## Overview
The Public Assistant Trigger node creates a publicly accessible entry point for ChatNext Assistant conversations. This trigger allows external users or systems to initiate funnel workflows through the ChatNext Assistant interface without requiring authentication. It's designed for public-facing conversational interfaces and automated processes.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Trigger
- **Element Type**: Trigger
- **Chatbot Node**: Yes

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Create public entry points for customer service chats
- Allow anonymous users to start conversations
- Implement public information kiosks
- Provide support chat for website visitors
- Create accessible help systems
- Set up automated public services
- Enable guest user interactions
- Build public-facing AI assistants

## Configuration Fields

### Action Definition
- **Name of Action**: Text field (required)
  - Defines the name/identifier for this public trigger
  - Plain text format
  - Used as the node label automatically
  - Should be descriptive and unique
  - Examples: "Customer Support", "Product Inquiry", "General Help"

### Internal Fields
- **node_label**: Hidden field
  - Automatically calculated from "Name of Action"
  - System field for internal labeling
  - Not user-editable

## Variables Usage
This node doesn't create specific variables but can access context from the trigger event:

### Available Context Variables
- `{{trigger_source}}`: Source of the trigger activation
- `{{session_id}}`: Unique session identifier
- `{{timestamp}}`: When the trigger was activated
- `{{user_agent}}`: Browser/client information (if web-based)
- `{{ip_address}}`: Client IP address (if available)

### Example Usage in Subsequent Nodes
```json
{
  "welcome_message": "Welcome to {{action_name}} support!",
  "session": "{{session_id}}",
  "started_at": "{{timestamp}}"
}
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Customer Support Chat
```
Name of Action: "Customer Support"
```
- Provides 24/7 support access
- Handles common inquiries
- Escalates complex issues

### 2. Product Information Assistant
```
Name of Action: "Product Inquiry"
```
- Answers product questions
- Provides specifications
- Assists with selection

### 3. Service Request Portal
```
Name of Action: "Service Request"
```
- Collects service requests
- Routes to appropriate departments
- Tracks request status

### 4. General Help Desk
```
Name of Action: "General Help"
```
- Provides general information
- Directs users to resources
- Offers step-by-step guidance

### 5. Anonymous Feedback Collection
```
Name of Action: "Feedback Portal"
```
- Collects user feedback
- Processes suggestions
- Routes complaints appropriately

### 6. Public Information Kiosk
```
Name of Action: "Information Kiosk"
```
- Provides location information
- Offers service details
- Displays operating hours

## Best Practices

### Naming Convention
1. **Descriptive Names**: Use clear, meaningful names that describe the purpose
2. **Consistent Naming**: Follow organizational naming standards
3. **User-Friendly**: Names should be understandable to end users
4. **Unique Identifiers**: Avoid duplicate names across triggers

### Security Considerations
1. **Public Access**: Remember this is publicly accessible
2. **Rate Limiting**: Implement appropriate rate limiting
3. **Data Privacy**: Don't collect sensitive information without consent
4. **Input Validation**: Validate all user inputs thoroughly
5. **Monitoring**: Log and monitor public access patterns

### User Experience
1. **Clear Purpose**: Make the trigger's purpose immediately clear
2. **Welcoming Interface**: Design friendly, approachable interactions
3. **Quick Response**: Ensure fast response times for public users
4. **Fallback Options**: Provide alternatives when the system is unavailable
5. **Accessibility**: Consider users with different abilities and technologies

### Performance Optimization
1. **Resource Management**: Monitor resource usage for public endpoints
2. **Caching**: Implement caching for frequently requested information
3. **Load Balancing**: Distribute load across multiple instances if needed
4. **Error Handling**: Gracefully handle high traffic and errors

### Content Management
1. **Regular Updates**: Keep information current and accurate
2. **Content Review**: Regularly review and update responses
3. **Multi-language Support**: Consider multiple language options
4. **Cultural Sensitivity**: Ensure content is appropriate for diverse audiences

## Related Nodes
- **Assistant Message**: For sending welcome messages and information
- **Assistant Text Question**: For collecting basic information from public users
- **Assistant Form Question**: For structured data collection
- **Assistant Multi Actions**: For presenting options to public users
- **Variable Filter**: For processing public user inputs
- **Send Notification**: For alerting staff about public inquiries
- **Data to Doc**: For storing public interactions
- **Trigger Funnel**: For starting different processes based on public requests
- **ChatGPT Message**: For AI-powered responses to public queries

## Technical Notes
- Publicly accessible without authentication requirements
- Generates unique session identifiers for each interaction
- Supports high-concurrency public access
- Integrates with ChatNext Assistant interface
- Can be embedded in websites or accessed directly
- Maintains session state throughout the conversation
- Supports both web and API access methods
- Automatically handles session management and cleanup
- Can be rate-limited for security and performance
- Logs all public interactions for analysis and monitoring

## Implementation Considerations
1. **Deployment**: Ensure proper deployment in public-facing environments
2. **Monitoring**: Set up monitoring and alerting for public endpoints
3. **Scaling**: Plan for potential high-volume public usage
4. **Backup**: Implement backup systems for critical public services
5. **Documentation**: Provide clear documentation for public API usage

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_question_single_choice.md
# Single Choice Question

## Overview
The Single Choice Question node presents users with a multiple-choice question in the ChatNext Assistant interface, allowing them to select exactly one option from a predefined list. This node creates branching workflows where different user selections can lead to different paths in the funnel process. It's ideal for surveys, decision trees, and guided workflows.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Present multiple choice questions
- Create branching workflows based on user selection
- Conduct surveys or polls
- Guide users through decision trees
- Collect categorical data
- Implement approval/rejection processes
- Route users to different paths based on choices
- Create interactive quizzes or assessments

## Configuration Fields

### Question Content
- **Question**: Textarea (required)
  - The question text to display to users
  - HTML format input supported
  - Fixed size with 3 rows
  - Can include formatting and variables
  - Supports `{{variable}}` interpolation

### Answer Options
- **Options**: Data grid (required, at least 1 option)
  - **Option**: Text field (required)
    - The text displayed as a choice option
    - Plain text format
    - Each option should be clear and distinct
    - Users will see these as clickable buttons or radio buttons

### Response Handling
- **Answer Variable**: Text field (required)
  - Name of variable where the selected answer will be stored
  - Description: "Name of variable where the answer from user to be stored into"
  - The variable will contain the selected option text

### Error Handling
- **Reply If Received Wrong Answer**: Text field (required)
  - Default: "please select from the options"
  - Message shown when user provides invalid input
  - Helps guide users back to valid choices

### Internal Configuration (hidden fields)
- **node_label**: Hidden field
  - Automatically calculated from question text
  - Default: "Single Choice Question"
  - Used for node display in the workflow builder

- **Output Handles Array Field**: Hidden field
  - Default: "options"
  - System field for managing multiple output paths

- **Output Handle Key Field**: Hidden field
  - Default: "option"
  - System field for routing based on selected option

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
Use variables to make questions dynamic:
- `{{user_name}}`: Personalize questions
- `{{product_name}}`: Context-specific questions
- `{{previous_answer}}`: Reference earlier responses

### Output Variables
This node creates:
- **Answer Variable** (as specified): Contains the selected option text
- Available for use in subsequent nodes as `{{answer_variable_name}}`

### Example Variable Usage
```
Question: "How would you rate your experience with {{product_name}}, {{user_name}}?"
Options:
- Excellent
- Good
- Fair
- Poor

Answer stored in: {{satisfaction_rating}}
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Customer Satisfaction Survey
```
Question: "How satisfied are you with our service?"
Options:
- Very Satisfied
- Satisfied
- Neutral
- Dissatisfied
- Very Dissatisfied
Answer Variable: customer_satisfaction
```

### 2. Support Priority Level
```
Question: "What is the priority level of your issue?"
Options:
- Critical (System Down)
- High (Major Impact)
- Medium (Minor Impact)
- Low (General Inquiry)
Answer Variable: issue_priority
```

### 3. Product Interest Survey
```
Question: "Which product category interests you most?"
Options:
- Software Solutions
- Hardware Products
- Consulting Services
- Training Programs
Answer Variable: product_interest
```

### 4. Appointment Preference
```
Question: "What is your preferred appointment time?"
Options:
- Morning (9 AM - 12 PM)
- Afternoon (12 PM - 5 PM)
- Evening (5 PM - 8 PM)
- Weekend
Answer Variable: appointment_preference
```

### 5. Approval Workflow
```
Question: "Do you approve this request?"
Options:
- Approve
- Reject
- Request More Information
Answer Variable: approval_decision
```

### 6. Department Routing
```
Question: "Which department can best help you?"
Options:
- Sales
- Technical Support
- Billing
- General Inquiry
Answer Variable: department_selection
```

## Best Practices

### Question Design
1. **Clear and Concise**: Write questions that are easy to understand
2. **Neutral Tone**: Avoid leading or biased questions
3. **Single Concept**: Ask about one thing at a time
4. **Complete Options**: Include all reasonable answer choices
5. **Mutually Exclusive**: Ensure options don't overlap

### Option Configuration
1. **Descriptive Labels**: Use clear, specific option text
2. **Logical Order**: Arrange options in a logical sequence
3. **Balanced Choices**: Provide balanced positive/negative options
4. **Avoid "Other"**: When possible, include specific options instead
5. **Consistent Format**: Use consistent formatting across options

### Variable Management
1. **Descriptive Names**: Use clear variable names like "satisfaction_rating"
2. **Consistent Naming**: Follow naming conventions across your workflow
3. **Document Variables**: Keep track of what each variable stores
4. **Validation**: Consider validating answers in subsequent nodes

### User Experience
1. **Context**: Provide enough context for users to make informed choices
2. **Progress**: Show progress in multi-question sequences
3. **Error Messages**: Use helpful, encouraging error messages
4. **Accessibility**: Ensure options are accessible to all users

### Workflow Design
1. **Branching Logic**: Plan different paths for different answers
2. **Default Paths**: Consider what happens with unexpected inputs
3. **Loop Prevention**: Avoid creating infinite loops with error handling
4. **Testing**: Test all possible answer paths thoroughly

## Related Nodes
- **Assistant Message**: For providing context before questions
- **Assistant Multi Actions**: For more complex action-based choices
- **Assistant Text Question**: For open-ended follow-up questions
- **Variable Filter**: For processing or transforming answers
- **Trigger Funnel**: For starting different processes based on answers
- **Assistant Form Question**: For collecting structured additional information
- **Send Notification**: For notifying about specific answer choices
- **Data to Doc**: For saving survey responses
- **ChatGPT Message**: For AI-powered follow-up based on answers

## Technical Notes
- Creates multiple output handles automatically based on options
- Each option can lead to a different workflow path
- Supports both button-style and radio button interfaces
- Answer validation ensures users select from available options
- Error handling redirects users back to the question
- Integrates seamlessly with ChatNext Assistant interface
- Supports mobile and desktop interfaces
- Can handle dynamic option lists through variables
- Automatically logs user selections for analytics
- Supports internationalization for multi-language options

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_show_file.md
# Assistant Preview File

## Overview
The Assistant Preview File node displays files to users within the ChatNext Assistant interface. This node allows users to preview documents, images, and other file types directly in the conversation without requiring separate downloads. It supports both file path references and File document references, with optional download functionality.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Show documents to users during conversations
- Display images, PDFs, or other media files
- Provide file previews before download
- Share generated reports or certificates
- Show attachment contents in context
- Display reference materials
- Present visual content like charts or diagrams
- Allow users to review files before taking action

## Configuration Fields

### File Reference
- **File Doc Name or Path**: Text field (required)
  - Can be either:
    - Direct file path (e.g., `/files/document.pdf`)
    - File document name from Frappe File doctype
  - Supports variable interpolation like `{{file_path}}`
  - Plain text format input

### File Source Type
- **Open File Doc**: Checkbox
  - Default: false (uses file path)
  - When enabled: Treats input as File document name
  - When disabled: Treats input as direct file path
  - Description: "When checked it would open the File doc instead of file path"

### Download Options
- **Enable Download**: Checkbox
  - Default: false
  - When enabled: Shows download button with file preview
  - When disabled: Preview only, no download option
  - Allows users to save files locally

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{file_path}}`: Dynamic file path or document name
- `{{document_name}}`: Reference to document attachments
- `{{generated_file}}`: Path to dynamically created files
- `{{attachment_url}}`: URL to file attachments

### Example Variable Usage
```
File Doc Name or Path: {{generated_report_path}}
Open File Doc: true
Enable Download: true
```

Or for direct file paths:
```
File Doc Name or Path: /files/{{user_id}}/certificate_{{certificate_id}}.pdf
Open File Doc: false
Enable Download: true
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Document Review and Approval
```
File Path: {{document_for_review}}
Open File Doc: true
Enable Download: false
```
- Display contracts, proposals, or reports for review
- Allow users to read content before approval/rejection

### 2. Certificate or Report Generation
```
File Path: {{generated_certificate}}
Open File Doc: false
Enable Download: true
```
- Show generated certificates, invoices, or reports
- Allow users to download their documents

### 3. Reference Material Display
```
File Path: /files/help/{{topic}}_guide.pdf
Open File Doc: false
Enable Download: false
```
- Display help documents, manuals, or guides
- Provide contextual reference materials

### 4. Image Gallery or Portfolio
```
File Path: {{product_image}}
Open File Doc: true
Enable Download: false
```
- Show product images, portfolio items, or galleries
- Display visual content in conversations

### 5. Attachment Preview
```
File Path: {{email_attachment}}
Open File Doc: true
Enable Download: true
```
- Preview email attachments before action
- Allow download of important attachments

### 6. Invoice or Receipt Display
```
File Path: {{invoice_file}}
Open File Doc: true
Enable Download: true
```
- Display billing documents
- Allow customers to download receipts

## Best Practices

### File Management
1. **File Paths**: Use absolute paths or proper File document references
2. **File Validation**: Ensure files exist before attempting to display
3. **File Size**: Consider file size limitations for preview
4. **File Types**: Test with different file formats (PDF, images, documents)
5. **Permissions**: Verify user has access to view the file

### User Experience
1. **Loading States**: Handle file loading gracefully
2. **Error Handling**: Provide clear messages if files can't be displayed
3. **Mobile Optimization**: Ensure files display properly on mobile devices
4. **Accessibility**: Provide alternative text for images when possible
5. **Performance**: Optimize large files for quick loading

### Security Considerations
1. **Access Control**: Verify user permissions before showing files
2. **File Validation**: Validate file types and content
3. **Path Sanitization**: Sanitize file paths to prevent directory traversal
4. **Sensitive Content**: Be careful with confidential documents
5. **Download Logging**: Log file downloads for audit purposes

### Variable Usage
1. **Dynamic Paths**: Use variables for flexible file references
2. **Error Fallbacks**: Consider default files if variables are empty
3. **Path Validation**: Validate variable content before use
4. **Null Handling**: Handle cases where file variables might be null

## Related Nodes
- **Create Print File**: For generating files to display
- **Assistant Message**: For providing context about files
- **Assistant Multi Actions**: For actions after file preview
- **Data to Doc**: For creating File documents
- **File to OCR**: For processing file content
- **Send File Message WhatsApp**: For file sharing on WhatsApp
- **Assistant Form Question**: For collecting feedback about files
- **Variable Filter**: For processing file-related variables
- **Create Doctype**: For creating structured file references

## Technical Notes
- Supports various file formats (PDF, images, documents, etc.)
- File preview capabilities depend on browser support
- Large files may require streaming or chunked loading
- File document references integrate with Frappe's File system
- Download functionality respects file permissions
- Supports both embedded preview and modal display
- Automatically handles file type detection
- Integrates with Frappe's file security system
- Supports file thumbnails for quick preview
- Can handle both public and private files based on permissions

## File Format Support
- **PDF**: Full preview with pagination
- **Images**: Direct display (JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP)
- **Documents**: Preview available for Word, Excel (browser dependent)
- **Text Files**: Direct text display
- **Videos**: Video player integration
- **Audio**: Audio player for supported formats
- **Archives**: File listing for ZIP files (preview dependent)

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_text_question.md
# ChatNext Assistant Text Question

## Overview
The ChatNext Assistant Text Question node collects text input from users through the ChatNext assistant interface. It presents a question and waits for the user's text response, with optional validation to ensure data quality. This is one of the most commonly used nodes for interactive data collection in ChatNext workflows.

**Category**: ChatNext Assistant
**Type**: Action Node (Awaiting)
**Label**: Assistant Question

## When to Use
Use the ChatNext Assistant Text Question node when you need to:
- Collect text responses from users
- Gather information like names, descriptions, or feedback
- Ask open-ended questions
- Collect email addresses with validation
- Get numeric input with validation
- Capture user preferences or comments
- Collect data that needs regex validation
- Build conversational workflows
- Create interactive forms step-by-step
- Validate user input before proceeding

## Configuration Fields

### 1. Question
**Field Type**: Text Area (3 rows)
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty
**Input Format**: HTML

**Description**: The question or prompt to display to the user.

**Variable Usage**:
```
Hello {{user_name}}, what is your email address?
Please describe your issue with {{product_name}}
Enter the invoice number for order {{order_id}}
```

**Best Practices**:
- Be clear and specific about what information you need
- Provide context when necessary
- Use friendly, conversational tone
- Include format hints (e.g., "Enter date as MM/DD/YYYY")
- Keep questions concise but informative

**Examples**:
- "What is your full name?"
- "Please provide your phone number (including country code)"
- "Describe the issue you're experiencing"
- "Enter your employee ID"
- "What is your preferred delivery address?"

### 2. Render as Html
**Field Type**: Checkbox
**Required**: No
**Default**: False

**Description**: Enable HTML rendering for the question text.

**⚠️ Warning**: When enabled, displays warning: "Rendering as Html might be harmful. use this only if necessary."

**When to Enable**:
- Need formatted text (bold, italic, lists)
- Want to include links
- Need to display structured content
- Want to use colors or styling

**Security Note**: Only enable if you trust the content source. HTML rendering can pose security risks if user-generated content is included.

**HTML Examples**:
```html
Contact Information
Please provide your primary email address:


  Must be a valid email
  Will be used for notifications

```

### 3. HTML Warning (Conditional)
**Field Type**: HTML Element
**Visible When**: Render as Html = True
**Content**: Warning message about HTML rendering risks

### 4. Answer Variable
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: Name of the variable where the user's answer will be stored.

**Naming Conventions**:
- Use descriptive names: `user_email`, `feedback_text`, `phone_number`
- No spaces or special characters
- Use underscore for multi-word: `delivery_address`
- Be consistent across workflow

**Usage in Later Nodes**:
```
{{user_email}} - Access the stored answer
{{feedback_text}} - Use in email body
{{phone_number}} - Pass to CRM system
```

### 5. Validate Answer
**Field Type**: Checkbox
**Required**: No
**Default**: False

**Description**: Enable validation of user input.

**When to Enable**:
- Need specific format (email, number)
- Want to ensure data quality
- Prevent invalid submissions
- Enforce business rules

### 6. Reply If Received Wrong Answer (Conditional)
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes (when validation enabled)
**Default**: "Please enter a valid answer."
**Visible When**: Validate Answer = True

**Description**: Message shown when validation fails.

**Customization Examples**:
- "Please enter a valid email address (e.g., user@example.com)"
- "Phone number must include country code (e.g., +1234567890)"
- "Please enter a number between 1 and 100"
- "Invalid format. Please use MM/DD/YYYY"
- "Employee ID must be 6 digits"

### 7. Validation Type (Conditional)
**Field Type**: Select (Dropdown)
**Required**: Yes (when validation enabled)
**Visible When**: Validate Answer = True
**Options**: Number, Email, Regex

**Description**: Type of validation to apply.

**Options Explained**:

**Number**:
- Validates numeric input
- Accepts integers and decimals
- Rejects alphabetic characters
- Use for: quantities, IDs, amounts

**Email**:
- Validates email format
- Checks for @ symbol and domain
- Standard email validation
- Use for: email addresses

**Regex**:
- Custom pattern matching
- Most flexible option
- Requires regex pattern
- Use for: custom formats, codes, IDs

### 8. Regex (Conditional)
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: Yes (when validation type is Regex)
**Visible When**: Validation Type = regex

**Description**: Regular expression pattern for validation.

**Common Patterns**:

**Phone Numbers**:
```regex
^\+?[1-9]\d{1,14}$  # International format
^\d{3}-\d{3}-\d{4}$  # US format (123-456-7890)
```

**Postal/ZIP Codes**:
```regex
^\d{5}(-\d{4})?$  # US ZIP code
^[A-Z]\d[A-Z] \d[A-Z]\d$  # Canadian postal code
```

**Custom IDs**:
```regex
^EMP\d{6}$  # Employee ID (EMP followed by 6 digits)
^INV-\d{4}-\d{2}$  # Invoice (INV-2024-01)
^[A-Z]{2}\d{8}$  # Product code (AB12345678)
```

**Dates**:
```regex
^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$  # MM/DD/YYYY
^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$  # YYYY-MM-DD
```

## Variables Available

### Input Variables
The node can access all workflow variables:
- `{{doc}}` - Current document context
- `{{user}}` - User information
- `{{trigger_data}}` - Data from workflow trigger
- Custom variables from previous nodes

### Output Variables
**Primary Output**: Stored in the specified answer variable
- Contains the validated text input from user
- Available for all subsequent nodes
- Can be used in conditions and routing

**Example Usage**:
If answer variable is `customer_email`:
```
{{customer_email}} - Use in email nodes
{{customer_email}} - Store in database
{{customer_email}} - Display in messages
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Email Collection with Validation
```
Question: "Please provide your email address for order updates"
Render as HTML: No
Answer Variable: customer_email
Validate Answer: Yes
Validation Type: Email
Wrong Answer Reply: "Please enter a valid email address (e.g., name@company.com)"
```

### 2. Feedback Collection
```
Question: "How was your experience with our service today?"
Render as HTML: No
Answer Variable: service_feedback
Validate Answer: No
```

### 3. Employee ID Validation
```
Question: "Enter your 6-digit employee ID"
Answer Variable: employee_id
Validate Answer: Yes
Validation Type: Regex
Regex: ^[0-9]{6}$
Wrong Answer Reply: "Employee ID must be exactly 6 digits"
```

### 4. Phone Number Collection
```
Question: "What's the best phone number to reach you?"
Answer Variable: contact_phone
Validate Answer: Yes
Validation Type: Regex
Regex: ^\+?[1-9]\d{1,14}$
Wrong Answer Reply: "Please enter a valid phone number with country code"
```

### 5. Order Number Lookup
```
Question: "Enter your order number (starts with ORD-)"
Answer Variable: order_number
Validate Answer: Yes
Validation Type: Regex
Regex: ^ORD-\d{6}$
Wrong Answer Reply: "Order number must be in format ORD-123456"
```

## Best Practices

### 1. Question Design
- **Be Specific**: Clear about what you're asking
- **Provide Context**: Why you need the information
- **Format Hints**: Show expected format
- **Keep Simple**: One question at a time

### 2. Validation Strategy
- **Always Validate**: Critical data (emails, IDs)
- **User-Friendly Messages**: Clear error messages
- **Test Patterns**: Test regex thoroughly
- **Consider Edge Cases**: International formats

### 3. Variable Management
- **Descriptive Names**: Clear variable names
- **Consistent Naming**: Same pattern throughout
- **Document Usage**: Note where variables are used
- **Avoid Overwriting**: Unique names for each question

### 4. User Experience
- **Progressive Disclosure**: Ask essential questions first
- **Helpful Errors**: Specific validation messages
- **Examples**: Show format examples
- **Allow Corrections**: Let users retry

## Integration Examples

### Sequential Questions
```
Text Question (Name) →
Text Question (Email) →
Text Question (Phone) →
Create Contact Record
```

### Conditional Routing
```
Text Question (Issue Type) →
Conditional Node →
  If "technical" → Technical Support Flow
  If "billing" → Billing Support Flow
  If "general" → General Support Flow
```

### Data Collection Form
```
Text Question 1 → Store in var1
Text Question 2 → Store in var2
Text Question 3 → Store in var3
Create Document with all variables
```

## Troubleshooting

### User Input Not Stored
**Possible Causes**:
1. Variable name has spaces or special characters
2. Variable name conflicts with system variables
3. Validation failing silently

**Solutions**:
- Use underscore instead of spaces
- Check variable isn't reserved
- Test without validation first

### Validation Always Fails
**Possible Causes**:
1. Regex pattern incorrect
2. Validation type mismatch
3. Extra whitespace in input

**Solutions**:
- Test regex pattern online
- Verify validation type selection
- Trim whitespace in processing

### HTML Not Rendering
**Possible Causes**:
1. Checkbox not enabled
2. Invalid HTML syntax
3. Security restrictions

**Solutions**:
- Enable "Render as HTML"
- Validate HTML syntax
- Check security settings

## Advanced Usage

### Multi-Language Support
```html

  {{language == 'es' ? 'Por favor, ingrese su correo' : 'Please enter your email'}}

```

### Dynamic Validation
Use variables in regex pattern:
```
Regex: {{validation_pattern}}
Where validation_pattern is set based on country/region
```

### Conditional Questions
Chain questions based on previous answers:
```
If {{answer1}} contains "business":
  → Ask for company name
Else:
  → Ask for personal details
```

## Limitations

1. **Text Only**: Cannot collect files or images
2. **Single Input**: One text field per node
3. **No Rich Text**: User cannot format their input
4. **Synchronous**: Waits for user response
5. **No Multi-line**: For longer text, use textarea-type nodes

## Related Nodes

- **ChatNext Assistant Form Question**: For complex forms
- **ChatNext Assistant Question Single Choice**: For predefined options
- **ChatNext Assistant Multi Actions**: For button selections
- **ChatNext Assistant AI Q&A Agent**: For conversational input
- **WhatsApp Question Nodes**: Similar for WhatsApp channel

## Security Considerations

1. **HTML Rendering**: Use cautiously with user content
2. **Input Sanitization**: Validate and sanitize all input
3. **PII Handling**: Be careful with sensitive data
4. **Storage**: Consider encryption for sensitive answers
5. **Regex Safety**: Avoid overly complex patterns that could cause delays

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_trigger.md
# ChatNext Assistant Trigger

## Overview
The ChatNext Assistant Trigger node initiates workflows from within the Frappe interface by adding action buttons to pages. It allows users to trigger ChatNext Assistant conversations from various locations including doctypes, workspaces, or specific routes. This trigger creates context-aware entry points for assistant workflows.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Trigger
- **Element Type**: Trigger
- **Trigger Event**: `chatnext_assistant_trigger`

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Add assistant triggers to specific pages
- Create context-aware workflow entry points
- Enable assistant interactions from doctypes
- Add action buttons to workspaces
- Trigger workflows from custom routes
- Create page-specific assistant actions
- Enable document-specific assistance
- Implement regex-based triggers

## Configuration Fields

### Name of Action
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: The name/label of the action trigger
- **Used as**: Node label and button text (if button label not specified)

### Page Type
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Options**:
  - **DocType**: Trigger on document type pages
  - **DocList**: Trigger on list views
  - **Workspace**: Trigger on workspace pages
  - **Specific Route**: Trigger on exact route match
  - **Regex**: Trigger on regex pattern match
  - **All**: Trigger on all pages

### Doctype
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required when page type is DocType/DocList)
- **Visible When**: Page Type = "doctype" or "doclist"
- **Data Source**: Dynamic list from `/api/resource/DocType`
- **Description**: Select the document type to attach trigger to

### Route
- **Field Type**: Text field (required when page type is route)
- **Visible When**: Page Type = "route"
- **Description**: Specific route path to trigger on

### Route Regex
- **Field Type**: Text field (required when page type is regex)
- **Visible When**: Page Type = "regex"
- **Description**: Regex pattern to match routes

### Workspace
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required when page type is workspace)
- **Visible When**: Page Type = "workspace"
- **Data Source**: Dynamic list from `/api/resource/Workspace`
- **Description**: Select workspace to attach trigger to

### Button Display Options

#### Show as Button
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Visible When**: Page Type = "doctype" and doctype is selected
- **Label Template**: "Show as Button in all '{{doctype}}' docs"
- **Description**: Display as action button on documents

#### Button Label
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Visible When**: Show as Button = true
- **Default**: Uses "Name of Action" if empty
- **Description**: Custom label for the button

### Visibility Conditions

#### Add Button Visibility Condition
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Visible When**: Show as Button = true
- **Description**: Add conditional visibility based on field values

#### Doc Field to Check
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required when visibility condition enabled)
- **Visible When**: Visibility Condition = true
- **Data Source**: Dynamic from `/api/method/nextai.funnel.doctype.funnel_task.api.get_doc_fields`
- **Description**: Field to check for visibility condition

#### Field Value to Check
- **Field Type**: Text field (required when visibility condition enabled)
- **Visible When**: Visibility Condition = true
- **Description**: Comma-separated list of values to show button
- **Example**: "Draft, Open, In Progress"

## Variables Usage

### Context Variables Available
- `{{doc}}`: Current document data (when triggered from doctype)
- `{{route}}`: Current route path
- `{{workspace}}`: Workspace name (when triggered from workspace)
- `{{user}}`: Current user information
- `{{page_type}}`: Type of page trigger activated from

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Document Action Button
```
Name of Action: "Process Invoice"
Page Type: DocType
Doctype: Sales Invoice
Show as Button: true
Button Label: "Process with Assistant"
Visibility Condition: true
Doc Field: status
Field Values: Draft, Submitted
```

### 2. Workspace Assistant
```
Name of Action: "HR Assistant"
Page Type: Workspace
Workspace: HR
```

### 3. List View Actions
```
Name of Action: "Bulk Update"
Page Type: DocList
Doctype: Customer
```

### 4. Custom Route Trigger
```
Name of Action: "Dashboard Assistant"
Page Type: Specific Route
Route: /app/dashboard
```

### 5. Pattern-Based Trigger
```
Name of Action: "Report Helper"
Page Type: Regex
Route Regex: ^/app/query-report/.*
```

## Best Practices

### Trigger Placement
1. **Strategic Positioning**: Place triggers where users need assistance
2. **Context Awareness**: Use page context for relevant help
3. **Clear Labeling**: Use descriptive action names
4. **Conditional Display**: Show only when relevant
5. **Performance**: Avoid too many triggers on single page

### Visibility Management
1. **Status-Based**: Show based on document status
2. **Permission-Aware**: Respect user permissions
3. **Field Conditions**: Use appropriate field checks
4. **Multiple Values**: Support comma-separated values
5. **Fallback Options**: Handle missing fields gracefully

### User Experience
1. **Clear Actions**: Make trigger purpose obvious
2. **Consistent Placement**: Maintain button position consistency
3. **Visual Hierarchy**: Don't overwhelm interface
4. **Loading States**: Show progress indicators
5. **Error Handling**: Graceful failure messages

## Related Nodes
- **Assistant Message**: First message after trigger
- **Assistant Text Question**: Collect additional context
- **Assistant Multi Actions**: Present action options
- **Variable Filter**: Process trigger context
- **Data to Doc**: Save trigger interactions
- **Assistant Public Trigger**: For public access
- **Manual Trigger**: For manual workflow start

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_assistant_ui_action.md
# ChatNext Assistant UI Action

## Overview
The ChatNext Assistant UI Action node provides interactive UI actions within the ChatNext Assistant interface. It allows users to create documents, update existing documents, or redirect to different routes through the assistant conversation. This node enables dynamic document management and navigation without leaving the chat interface.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Create new documents from within the chat interface
- Update existing documents based on user input
- Redirect users to specific pages or forms
- Provide document creation as a chat action
- Enable in-chat document editing
- Navigate users to relevant resources
- Complete workflows with document creation
- Update records based on conversation context

## Configuration Fields

### Action Type
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Default**: "create_document"
- **Options**:
  - **Create Document**: Creates a new document with prefilled data
  - **Update Document**: Updates an existing document
  - **Redirect To**: Redirects to a specific route/URL

### For Create Document Action

#### Doctype
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Visible When**: Action Type = "Create Document"
- **Data Source**: Dynamic list from `/api/resource/DocType`
- **Description**: Select the document type to create
- **Search Enabled**: Yes

#### Prefill Json
- **Field Type**: Textarea (required)
- **Visible When**: Action Type = "Create Document"
- **Rows**: 3
- **Format**: JSON
- **Description**: JSON data to prefill in the new document
- **Supports Variables**: Yes

### For Update Document Action

#### Document Json
- **Field Type**: Textarea (required)
- **Visible When**: Action Type = "Update Document"
- **Rows**: 3
- **Format**: JSON
- **Description**: JSON containing document name and fields to update
- **Supports Variables**: Yes

### For Redirect Action

#### Route
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Visible When**: Action Type = "Redirect To"
- **Format**: Plain text
- **Description**: Route or URL to redirect to
- **Supports Variables**: Yes

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{user_data}}`: User information for document creation
- `{{form_responses}}`: Form data collected from chat
- `{{document_id}}`: ID of document to update
- `{{calculated_values}}`: Computed values for fields
- `{{context_data}}`: Contextual information from conversation

### Example Variable Usage

#### Create Document
```json
{
  "customer_name": "{{user_name}}",
  "email": "{{user_email}}",
  "request_type": "{{selected_service}}",
  "priority": "{{priority_level}}",
  "description": "{{issue_description}}"
}
```

#### Update Document
```json
{
  "name": "{{document_name}}",
  "status": "{{new_status}}",
  "assigned_to": "{{assignee}}",
  "comments": "{{update_comments}}"
}
```

#### Redirect
```
/app/{{doctype}}/{{document_name}}
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Create Support Ticket
```
Action Type: Create Document
Doctype: Issue
Prefill Json: {
  "subject": "{{issue_subject}}",
  "description": "{{issue_details}}",
  "customer": "{{customer_id}}",
  "priority": "High"
}
```

### 2. Update Order Status
```
Action Type: Update Document
Document Json: {
  "name": "{{order_id}}",
  "status": "Processing",
  "expected_delivery": "{{delivery_date}}",
  "notes": "Order confirmed via chat"
}
```

### 3. Navigate to Dashboard
```

Route: /app/dashboard/{{user_role}}-dashboard
```

### 4. Create Lead from Chat
```
Action Type: Create Document
Doctype: Lead
Prefill Json: {
  "lead_name": "{{contact_name}}",
  "email_id": "{{email}}",
  "mobile_no": "{{phone}}",
  "source": "ChatNext Assistant",
  "interest": "{{product_interest}}"
}
```

### 5. Update Customer Profile
```
Action Type: Update Document
Document Json: {
  "name": "{{customer_id}}",
  "preferred_contact": "{{contact_preference}}",
  "communication_language": "{{language}}",
  "last_interaction": "{{current_datetime}}"
}
```

## Best Practices

### Document Creation
1. **Validation**: Ensure all required fields are included in prefill JSON
2. **Permissions**: Verify user has permission to create the doctype
3. **Default Values**: Include sensible defaults for optional fields
4. **Error Handling**: Handle creation failures gracefully
5. **Confirmation**: Show confirmation after successful creation

### Document Updates

2. **Field Validation**: Ensure update fields are valid for the doctype
3. **Partial Updates**: Only update necessary fields

5. **Conflict Resolution**: Handle concurrent update scenarios

2. **Dynamic Routes**: Use variables for flexible navigation

### Variable Usage

2. **Variable Availability**: Ensure all variables exist before use
3. **Escaping**: Properly escape special characters in JSON
4. **Type Matching**: Ensure variable types match field types
5. **Null Handling**: Handle empty or null variable values

## Related Nodes

- **Assistant Message**: For confirming actions

- **Data to Doc**: For alternative document creation
- **Update Doc**: For backend document updates
- **Variable Filter**: For preparing document data
- **Assistant Text Question**: For collecting individual fields
- **Trigger Funnel**: For complex document workflows
- **Call API**: For custom document operations

## Technical Notes
- Integrates with Frappe's document model
- Respects field-level permissions
- Validates against doctype field definitions
- Supports custom fields and doctypes
- Creates documents in draft state by default
- Update operations are atomic
- Redirect works with both internal and external URLs
- Actions are performed in user context
- Supports document workflow states
- Maintains document version history

## Error Handling
- Invalid JSON format triggers error message
- Missing required fields prevents submission
- Permission errors are clearly displayed
- Network failures are handled gracefully
- Document lock conflicts are managed
- Invalid routes show error page
- Validation errors display field-specific messages

## Security Considerations
1. **Permission Checks**: All operations respect user permissions
2. **Input Sanitization**: JSON input is sanitized
3. **XSS Prevention**: Route inputs are validated

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/chatnext_dynamic_multi_actions.md
# ChatNext Dynamic Multi Actions

## Overview
The ChatNext Dynamic Multi Actions node provides dynamic action selection based on approval matrices within the ChatNext Assistant interface. It allows workflows to dynamically present different action options based on predefined approval policies, enabling flexible decision trees and approval workflows that adapt to different contexts and requirements.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action
- **Error Handle**: Supported

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Implement dynamic approval workflows
- Present different actions based on approval policies
- Create adaptive decision trees
- Route workflows through approval matrices
- Enable conditional action paths
- Implement role-based action selection
- Create dynamic approval chains
- Handle complex approval scenarios

## Configuration Fields

### Select Manually
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Position**: Right-aligned label
- **Description**: Enable manual selection of approval matrix

### Select Approval Matrix
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (autocomplete)
- **Visible When**: Select Manually = true
- **Data Source**: Dynamic from `/api/method/nextai.funnel.apis.fetch_data.get_searched_doc_list`
- **Filter**: `doctype=Approval Policy Matrix&limit=20.523&fields=name,matrix_name`
- **Template Display**: `{{name}} ({{matrix_name}})`
- **Search Enabled**: Yes
- **Sort By**: name
- **Description**: Choose the approval policy matrix to determine available actions

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{approval_context}}`: Context for approval decision
- `{{user_role}}`: Current user's role for matrix selection
- `{{document_value}}`: Value or amount for approval thresholds
- `{{request_type}}`: Type of request requiring approval
- `{{previous_approvals}}`: History of previous approvals

### Output Variables
The node dynamically generates outputs based on the selected approval matrix actions.

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Purchase Order Approval
```
Select Manually: true
Select Approval Matrix: "PO Approval Matrix"
```
- Routes based on purchase order value
- Different approvers for different amounts
- Multiple approval levels

### 2. Leave Request Processing
```
Select Manually: false (automatic based on context)
```
- Automatically selects leave approval matrix
- Routes to appropriate manager
- Handles different leave types

### 3. Expense Claim Approval
```
Select Manually: true
Select Approval Matrix: "Expense Approval Policy"
```
- Multi-level expense approval
- Department-specific routing
- Amount-based escalation

### 4. Document Release Workflow
```
Select Manually: true
Select Approval Matrix: "Document Release Matrix"
```
- Quality check approvals
- Department head sign-offs
- Final release authorization

### 5. Customer Credit Approval
```
Select Manually: false
```
- Automatic credit limit approval
- Risk-based routing
- Multiple stakeholder approval

## Best Practices

### Matrix Selection
1. **Clear Naming**: Use descriptive matrix names
2. **Documentation**: Document each matrix's purpose
3. **Version Control**: Track matrix changes
4. **Testing**: Test all approval paths
5. **Fallback Options**: Include default approval paths

### Workflow Design
1. **Logical Flow**: Design intuitive approval flows
2. **Escalation Paths**: Define clear escalation routes
3. **Timeout Handling**: Set approval timeouts
4. **Delegation**: Allow approval delegation
5. **Audit Trail**: Maintain approval history

### Performance
1. **Cache Matrices**: Cache frequently used matrices
2. **Optimize Queries**: Optimize matrix lookups
3. **Lazy Loading**: Load matrices on demand
4. **Batch Processing**: Process multiple approvals together
5. **Index Fields**: Index approval matrix fields

### User Experience
1. **Clear Instructions**: Provide clear approval instructions
2. **Status Updates**: Show approval status
3. **Notifications**: Send timely notifications
4. **Mobile Access**: Enable mobile approvals
5. **Quick Actions**: Provide quick approve/reject options

## Related Nodes
- **Assistant Multi Actions**: For static action selection
- **Assistant Document Approval**: For document-specific approvals
- **Variable Filter**: For filtering approval options
- **Send Notification**: For approval notifications
- **Update Doc**: For updating approval status
- **Trigger Funnel**: For triggering approval sub-workflows
- **Data to Doc**: For logging approval decisions
- **Assistant Message**: For approval confirmations

## Technical Notes
- Integrates with Approval Policy Matrix doctype
- Supports dynamic action generation
- Handles complex approval hierarchies
- Maintains approval state throughout workflow
- Supports parallel and sequential approvals
- Implements approval delegation mechanisms
- Provides approval history tracking
- Supports conditional approval logic
- Handles approval timeouts and escalations
- Integrates with user role permissions

## Approval Matrix Structure
The Approval Policy Matrix typically includes:
1. **Approval Levels**: Hierarchical approval stages
2. **Conditions**: Criteria for approval routing
3. **Approvers**: Users or roles who can approve
4. **Actions**: Available actions at each level
5. **Escalation Rules**: Timeout and escalation logic
6. **Delegation Rules**: Who can delegate approvals
7. **Notification Settings**: Communication preferences

## Error Handling
- Invalid matrix selection shows error message
- Missing approvers trigger escalation
- Timeout conditions activate fallback paths
- Circular approval detection prevents loops
- Permission errors are logged and reported
- Network failures trigger retry mechanisms
- Invalid conditions use default routing

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/copy_doc_fields.md
# Copy Document Fields

## Overview
The Copy Document Fields node copies field values between documents. It enables field mapping, document duplication, and data synchronization between related documents with support for field transformation during the copy process.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Document
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Duplicate documents with modifications
- Copy fields between related documents
- Synchronize data across doctypes
- Create document templates
- Archive document versions
- Transfer field values
- Clone records
- Migrate data between documents

## Configuration Fields

### Source Document

#### Source Doctype
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: Document type to copy from
- **Source**: All available doctypes

#### Source Document Name
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Specific document to copy from
- **Supports**: Variables like {{source_doc}}

### Target Document

#### Target Doctype
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: Document type to copy to

#### Target Document Name
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Description**: Existing document or new name
- **Leave empty**: To create new document

### Field Mapping

#### Fields to Copy
- **Field Type**: Multi-select or mapping table
- **Options**:
  - All fields
  - Selected fields
  - Custom mapping
- **Default**: All compatible fields

#### Field Map
- **Field Type**: Key-value pairs
- **Format**: source_field: target_field
- **Description**: Custom field mapping

#### Exclude Fields
- **Field Type**: Multi-select
- **Description**: Fields to skip during copy
- **Default**: System fields (modified, created, etc.)

### Copy Options

#### Include Child Tables
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Copy child table records

#### Override Existing
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Overwrite existing field values

#### Transform Values
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Apply transformations during copy

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{source_document}}`: Source document name
- `{{target_document}}`: Target document name
- `{{field_mapping}}`: Dynamic field mapping

### Output Variables
- `{{copied_document}}`: Target document name
- `{{copied_fields}}`: List of copied fields
- `{{copy_status}}`: Success or failure

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Duplicate Order
```
Source: Sales Order - SO-001
Target: New Sales Order
Exclude: workflow_state, docstatus
Modify: delivery_date, customer_po
```

### 2. Quote to Order
```
Source Doctype: Quotation
Target Doctype: Sales Order
Field Map: Custom mapping
Transform: Status fields
```

### 3. Template Creation
```
Source: Existing document
Target: Template document
Exclude: Transaction-specific fields
Include: Configuration fields
```

### 4. Archive Document
```
Source: Active document
Target: Archive doctype
Add: archived_date, archived_by
Preserve: All original fields
```

### 5. Cross-Module Copy
```
Source: CRM Lead
Target: Customer
Field Map:
  lead_name: customer_name
  email_id: email_id
```

## Best Practices

1. **Field Compatibility**: Ensure field types match
2. **Validation**: Validate after copying
3. **Permissions**: Check user permissions
4. **Child Tables**: Handle child records properly
5. **Unique Fields**: Handle unique constraints
6. **Audit Trail**: Log copy operations
7. **Testing**: Test with various document types

## Related Nodes
- **Create Doctype**: For new documents
- **Update Doctype**: For updates
- **Data to Doc**: For complex operations
- **Variable Filter**: For field transformation

## Technical Notes
- Validates field compatibility
- Handles field type conversions
- Preserves field permissions
- Manages linked fields
- Supports custom fields
- Handles attachments
- Maintains referential integrity
- Logs all copy operations

---

# Create Document

## Overview
The Create Document node creates new Frappe documents with specified field values. It provides a straightforward way to create records in the database with validation, permission checking, and automatic field population.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Document
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Create new records from workflow data
- Generate documents programmatically
- Save form submissions
- Create child documents
- Generate transactional records
- Create user accounts
- Log workflow events
- Initialize new entities

## Configuration Fields

### Doctype
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: Document type to create
- **Source**: All available doctypes

### Field Values
- **Field Type**: Key-value pairs (required)
- **Description**: Field names and their values
- **Format**: field_name: value
- **Supports**: Variables in values

### Document Options

#### Name/ID
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Explicit document name
- **Default**: Auto-generated based on naming series

#### Submit After Creation
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Auto-submit if document is submittable

#### Ignore Mandatory
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Skip mandatory field validation

#### Ignore Permissions
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Bypass permission checks

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- Any workflow variable can be used in field values
- `{{variable_name}}`: Direct variable reference
- Supports nested objects for child tables

### Output Variables
- `{{created_doc_name}}`: Name of created document
- `{{created_doc}}`: Complete document object
- `{{creation_status}}`: Success or error status

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Create Customer
```
Doctype: Customer
Fields:
  customer_name: {{company_name}}
  customer_type: "Company"
  territory: {{region}}
  customer_group: "Commercial"
```

### 2. Create Task
```
Doctype: Task
Fields:
  subject: {{task_title}}
  description: {{task_description}}
  assigned_to: {{assignee}}
  due_date: {{deadline}}
```

### 3. Create Sales Order
```
Doctype: Sales Order
Fields:
  customer: {{customer_id}}
  delivery_date: {{requested_date}}
  items: {{order_items_array}}
Submit After Creation: true
```

### 4. Create User
```
Doctype: User
Fields:
  email: {{user_email}}
  first_name: {{first_name}}
  last_name: {{last_name}}
  roles: [{"role": "Customer"}]
```

### 5. Create Log Entry
```
Doctype: Activity Log
Fields:
  activity_type: "Workflow Execution"
  user: {{current_user}}
  timestamp: {{current_datetime}}
  details: {{workflow_details}}
```

## Best Practices

1. **Field Validation**: Ensure all required fields are provided
2. **Data Types**: Match variable types to field types
3. **Naming**: Use naming series or provide unique names
4. **Permissions**: Respect user permissions
5. **Child Tables**: Structure nested data correctly
6. **Error Handling**: Handle creation failures gracefully
7. **Duplicates**: Check for existing records if needed
8. **Testing**: Test with various data scenarios

## Related Nodes
- **Update Doctype**: For modifying existing documents
- **Data to Doc**: For more complex document operations
- **Copy Doc Fields**: For duplicating documents
- **Variable Filter**: For preparing document data

## Technical Notes
- Validates against doctype schema
- Triggers document hooks and events
- Respects field permissions
- Handles auto-name generation
- Supports custom fields
- Creates audit trail entries
- Manages linked documents
- Supports bulk creation with loops

# Create Funnel Trigger Link

## Overview
The Create Funnel Trigger Link node generates unique URLs that trigger funnel workflows when accessed. These links enable external systems, email campaigns, or users to start workflows through web access.

## Category and Type

- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

- **Description**: Funnel to trigger
- **Source**: Available funnels

#### Link Type
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - One-time use
  - Multi-use
  - Time-limited
- **Default**: Multi-use

#### Expiration
- **Field Type**: DateTime field
- **Visible When**: Link Type = "Time-limited"
- **Description**: Link expiration date

### Parameters

#### Pass Parameters
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Allow URL parameters

#### Pre-defined Parameters
- **Field Type**: Key-value pairs
- **Description**: Parameters to include
- **Format**: param_name: value

#### Secure Link
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Generate signed secure link

### Tracking

- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Track link usage

#### Redirect After
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Description**: URL to redirect after trigger
- **Optional**: Can show success message instead

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables

### Output Variables

## Common Use Cases

Generate unique links per recipient
```

### 5. Onboarding Links

Secure: true
```

## Best Practices

1. **Security**: Use secure links for sensitive workflows
2. **Expiration**: Set appropriate expiration times
3. **Tracking**: Monitor link usage and performance
4. **Parameters**: Validate parameters in target funnel
5. **Error Handling**: Handle invalid/expired links
6. **Short URLs**: Consider URL shortening for sharing
7. **Testing**: Test links before distribution

## Technical Notes
- Generates unique UUID-based links
- Supports URL parameter passing
- Implements link signing for security
- Tracks click analytics
- Handles link expiration
- Supports custom domains
- Provides link management interface
- Integrates with URL shorteners
