# Create Print File

## Overview
The Create Print File node generates PDF files from documents using print formats. It converts Frappe documents into formatted PDF files for reports, invoices, certificates, and other printable documents.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Document
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Generate PDF invoices
- Create report documents
- Generate certificates
- Produce printable forms
- Create document archives
- Email PDF attachments
- Generate batch documents
- Export formatted data

## Configuration Fields

### Document Selection

#### Doctype
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: Document type to print
- **Source**: All doctypes with print formats

#### Document Name
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Specific document to print
- **Supports**: Variables like {{document_id}}

#### Print Format
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Description**: Print format template to use
- **Default**: Standard format for doctype
- **Source**: Available print formats for selected doctype

### Output Options

#### File Name
- **Field Type**: Text field

- **Default**: {doctype}-{name}.pdf
- **Supports**: Variables

#### Save Location

- **Options**:
  - Private files
  - Public files
  - Temporary
  - Return as variable
- **Default**: Private files

#### Letterhead
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (optional)
- **Description**: Letterhead template to use
- **Source**: Configured letterheads

### PDF Options

#### Page Size
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**: A4, Letter, Legal, A3
- **Default**: A4

#### Orientation
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**: Portrait, Landscape
- **Default**: Portrait

#### Include Attachments
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Merge document attachments into PDF

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{doc_name}}`: Document to print
- `{{print_format_name}}`: Dynamic format selection
- `{{file_name}}`: Dynamic file naming

### Output Variables
- `{{pdf_file_path}}`: Path to generated PDF
- `{{pdf_file_url}}`: URL to access PDF
- `{{pdf_file_size}}`: File size in bytes
- `{{pdf_page_count}}`: Number of pages

## Common Use Cases

```

```

### 5. Email Attachment Preparation


2. **File Naming**: Use descriptive, unique file names

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/cron_node.md
# Cron Trigger

## Overview
The Cron Trigger node initiates workflows on a schedule using cron expressions. It enables recurring automated tasks, scheduled reports, periodic data synchronization, and time-based workflow execution.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Trigger
- **Type**: Trigger
- **Element Type**: Trigger

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Run workflows on schedule
- Generate periodic reports
- Perform regular data backups
- Send scheduled notifications
- Sync data at intervals
- Clean up old records
- Monitor system health
- Execute maintenance tasks

## Configuration Fields

### Cron Expression
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Cron schedule expression
- **Format**: Standard cron syntax
- **Examples**:
  - `0 9 * * *` (Daily at 9 AM)
  - `0 */2 * * *` (Every 2 hours)
  - `0 0 * * 1` (Weekly on Monday)
  - `0 0 1 * *` (Monthly on 1st)

### Timezone
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Default**: System timezone
- **Description**: Timezone for schedule execution

### Enable/Disable
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Toggle schedule activation

### Start Date
- **Field Type**: Date field (optional)
- **Description**: Date to start schedule

### End Date
- **Field Type**: Date field (optional)
- **Description**: Date to stop schedule

### Max Runs
- **Field Type**: Number field (optional)
- **Description**: Maximum number of executions

### Description
- **Field Type**: Textarea
- **Description**: Purpose of scheduled workflow

## Variables Usage

### Output Variables
- `{{trigger_time}}`: Execution timestamp
- `{{previous_run}}`: Last execution time
- `{{next_run}}`: Next scheduled execution
- `{{run_count}}`: Number of executions

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Daily Report Generation
```
Cron Expression: 0 6 * * *
Description: Generate daily sales report at 6 AM
Timezone: America/New_York
```

### 2. Hourly Data Sync
```
Cron Expression: 0 * * * *
Description: Sync data every hour
Max Runs: 24 (one day limit)
```

### 3. Weekly Backup
```
Cron Expression: 0 2 * * 0
Description: Weekly backup every Sunday at 2 AM
```

### 4. Monthly Invoice Generation
```
Cron Expression: 0 0 1 * *
Description: Generate invoices on 1st of month
```

### 5. Business Hours Check
```
Cron Expression: */15 9-17 * * 1-5
Description: Every 15 mins during business hours
```

## Cron Expression Guide

### Format
```
* * * * *
│ │ │ │ │
│ │ │ │ └─ Day of week (0-7, Sun=0 or 7)
│ │ │ └─── Month (1-12)
│ │ └───── Day of month (1-31)
│ └─────── Hour (0-23)
└───────── Minute (0-59)
```

### Special Characters
- `*` : Any value
- `,` : Value list (e.g., 1,3,5)
- `-` : Range (e.g., 1-5)
- `/` : Step values (e.g., */5)

## Best Practices

1. **Time Zones**: Consider timezone for international operations
2. **Overlap Prevention**: Avoid overlapping executions
3. **Error Handling**: Handle missed executions
4. **Resource Planning**: Consider system load
5. **Monitoring**: Track execution success
6. **Documentation**: Document schedule purpose
7. **Testing**: Test cron expressions before deployment

## Related Nodes
- **Wait Till Date**: For one-time scheduled execution
- **Manual Trigger**: For on-demand execution
- **Document Event Trigger**: For event-based triggers
- **Email Trigger**: For email-based triggers

## Technical Notes
- Uses system cron scheduler
- Persists across server restarts
- Handles daylight saving time
- Logs all executions
- Supports seconds precision (extended cron)
- Can catch up missed executions
- Respects system maintenance windows

---

# Data to Document

## Overview
The Data to Doc node creates or updates Frappe documents from workflow data. It maps workflow variables to document fields with automatic validation, permission checking, and error handling. This node is essential for persisting workflow data into the database.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Document
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Create new documents from workflow data
- Update existing documents with workflow results
- Save form submissions to database
- Log workflow activities
- Create child documents
- Batch create multiple documents
- Transform and save data
- Archive workflow outputs

## Configuration Fields

### Doctype
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: Target document type
- **Source**: All available doctypes in system

### Operation Mode
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Options**:
  - Insert (create new)
  - Update (modify existing)
  - Insert or Update (upsert)

### Document Name
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Required for**: Update operations
- **Description**: Name of document to update

### Field Mapping
- **Field Type**: Key-value pairs
- **Description**: Map workflow variables to document fields
- **Format**: field_name: {{variable_name}}

### Submit Document
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Auto-submit document after creation

### Ignore Permissions
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Bypass permission checks (admin only)

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- Any workflow variable can be mapped to document fields
- Supports nested objects for child tables
- Arrays for multi-value fields

### Output Variables
- `document_name`: Name of created/updated document
- `document_status`: Operation status
- `document_data`: Full document data after save

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Create Customer from Form
```json
{
  "doctype": "Customer",
  "customer_name": "{{form_name}}",
  "email_id": "{{form_email}}",
  "mobile_no": "{{form_phone}}",
  "customer_type": "Individual"
}
```

### 2. Log Workflow Activity
```json
{
  "doctype": "Workflow Log",
  "workflow_name": "{{workflow_id}}",
  "status": "{{current_status}}",
  "timestamp": "{{current_datetime}}",
  "user": "{{user_email}}"
}
```

### 3. Create Order with Items
```json
{
  "doctype": "Sales Order",
  "customer": "{{customer_name}}",
  "items": [
    {
      "item_code": "{{item_1}}",
      "qty": "{{qty_1}}"
    }
  ]
}
```

### 4. Update Task Status
```json
{
  "doctype": "Task",
  "name": "{{task_id}}",
  "status": "Completed",
  "completion_date": "{{today}}",
  "completed_by": "{{assigned_user}}"
}
```

## Best Practices

1. **Field Validation**: Ensure all required fields are mapped
2. **Data Types**: Match variable types to field types
3. **Error Handling**: Handle document validation errors
4. **Permissions**: Consider user permissions
5. **Naming**: Use naming series or explicit names
6. **Child Tables**: Structure nested data properly
7. **Workflows**: Respect document workflows
8. **Bulk Operations**: Use batch mode for multiple documents

## Related Nodes
- **Update Doctype**: For simple updates
- **Create Doctype**: For simple creation
- **Copy Doc Fields**: For document duplication
- **Variable Filter**: For data preparation
- **Execute Python**: For complex document operations

## Technical Notes
- Validates against doctype field definitions
- Respects field permissions and hidden fields
- Triggers document hooks and events
- Maintains audit trail
- Supports custom fields
- Handles file attachments
- Manages linked documents
- Supports document versioning

  - On Change (specific field changed)
  - On Submit (document submitted)
  - On Cancel (document cancelled)

- **Description**: Specific field to monitor for changes

## Variables Available

- Implements retry mechanisms

# Email Trigger

## Overview
The Email Trigger node initiates workflows when emails are received. It monitors email accounts for incoming messages and triggers workflows based on sender, subject, content, or other email attributes, enabling email-driven automation.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Email
- **Type**: Trigger
- **Element Type**: Trigger

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Process incoming support emails
- Handle email-based requests
- Extract data from email notifications
- Automate email responses
- Parse email attachments
- Route emails to workflows
- Create tickets from emails
- Monitor specific email patterns

## Configuration Fields

### Email Account
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: Email account to monitor
- **Source**: Configured email accounts in system

### Trigger Conditions

#### Filter by Sender
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Description**: Email address or domain to filter
- **Supports**: Wildcards (*@domain.com)

#### Filter by Subject
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Description**: Subject line pattern to match
- **Supports**: Regular expressions

#### Filter by Content
- **Field Type**: Textarea
- **Description**: Keywords or patterns in email body
- **Supports**: Multiple keywords (comma-separated)

#### Has Attachments
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Description**: Only trigger if email has attachments

### Processing Options

#### Mark as Read
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Mark email as read after processing

#### Move to Folder
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Description**: Folder to move email after processing

#### Delete After Processing
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Delete email after workflow execution

- `{{email_attachments}}`: List of attachment details
- `{{email_date}}`: Email received date
- `{{email_message_id}}`: Unique message identifier

### 4. Newsletter Subscriber

```
Action: Add sender to mailing list
Delete After Processing: true
```

### 5. Automated Responses
```
Filter by Content: "information request"
Action: Send automated information packet
Mark as Read: true
```

## Best Practices

1. **Specific Filters**: Use specific filters to avoid false triggers
2. **Email Account**: Use dedicated email accounts for automation
3. **Error Handling**: Handle malformed emails gracefully
4. **Attachment Security**: Scan attachments for security
5. **Rate Limiting**: Implement processing limits
6. **Duplicate Detection**: Check for duplicate processing
7. **Logging**: Log all processed emails
8. **Testing**: Test with various email formats

- **Send Bulk Mail**: For mass email operations

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/exec_python.md
# Execute Python

## Overview
The Execute Python node executes custom Python code within the workflow. It provides Python capabilities for data processing and custom logic within a sandboxed environment. The code runs with security restrictions using Frappe's safe_exec. **No imports are allowed** - only built-in Python functions can be used.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Utility
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Perform data transformations and calculations
- Implement custom business logic
- Process and manipulate variables
- Apply conditional logic to workflow data
- Format and validate data
- Perform date/time calculations
- Process lists and dictionaries
- Create derived variables from existing data

## Configuration Fields

### Node Label
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Custom label for the node in the workflow

### Python Code
- **Field Type**: Code editor (required)
- **Language**: Python
- **Description**: Python code to execute
- **Note**: Variables are accessible and modifiable via `variables` dictionary
- **Default**: `# do required updates\n# update variable e.g.\n# variables["test"] = "test"`

### Execute Immediately
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Key**: `skip_queue_and_execute_immediately`
- **Description**: Execute node immediately without queuing
- **Default**: False

### Execute as User
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Key**: `execute_current_node_as_user`
- **Description**: Execute the node as a specific user (email or username)

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- All workflow variables accessible via `variables` dictionary
- Access: `variables.get('variable_name')` or `variables['variable_name']`
- Modify: `variables['new_variable'] = value`
- **Note**: `frappe` module is NOT available (disabled for security)

### Output Variables
- The function's return value is stored as the result
- Can return any Python type (dict, list, string, number, etc.)
- Modified variables persist in the workflow

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Variable Manipulation
```python
# Access existing variables
order_amount = variables.get('order_amount', 0)
tax_rate = variables.get('tax_rate', 0.1)

# Calculate and store new variables
variables['tax_amount'] = order_amount * tax_rate
variables['total_amount'] = order_amount + variables['tax_amount']

# Return a result
return {'status': 'calculated', 'total': variables['total_amount']}
```

### 2. Data Processing
```python
# Process list of items
items = variables.get('items', [])
processed_items = []

for item in items:
    if item.get('quantity', 0) > 0:
        processed_items.append({
            'name': item.get('name'),
            'total': item.get('quantity') * item.get('price', 0)
        })

variables['processed_items'] = processed_items
variables['items_count'] = len(processed_items)

return processed_items
```

### 3. String Manipulation
```python
# Format and process strings
first_name = variables.get('first_name', '')
last_name = variables.get('last_name', '')
email = variables.get('email', '')

# Create formatted values
variables['full_name'] = f"{first_name} {last_name}".strip()
variables['username'] = email.split('@')[0] if '@' in email else email
variables['domain'] = email.split('@')[1] if '@' in email else ''

return variables['full_name']
```

### 4. Conditional Logic
```python
# Implement business rules
status = variables.get('status', '')
amount = variables.get('amount', 0)
customer_type = variables.get('customer_type', 'regular')

# Apply business logic
if status == 'approved' and amount > 1000:
    if customer_type == 'premium':
        variables['discount'] = amount * 0.15
    else:
        variables['discount'] = amount * 0.05
else:
    variables['discount'] = 0

variables['final_amount'] = amount - variables['discount']
return variables['final_amount']
```

### 5. Working with Built-in Functions
```python
# No imports allowed - use built-in functions only

# String and number operations
text = variables.get('text', '')
variables['uppercase'] = text.upper()
variables['word_count'] = len(text.split())

# List operations with built-ins
numbers = variables.get('numbers', [])
if numbers:
    variables['maximum'] = max(numbers)
    variables['minimum'] = min(numbers)
    variables['sum_total'] = sum(numbers)
    variables['average'] = sum(numbers) / len(numbers)

# Type conversions
value = variables.get('value', '0')
try:
    variables['as_integer'] = int(value)
    variables['as_float'] = float(value)
except ValueError:
    variables['conversion_error'] = 'Invalid number format'

return True
```

## Best Practices

1. **Variable Access**: Always use `variables.get()` with defaults to handle missing values
2. **Error Handling**: Use try-except blocks for robust error handling
3. **Security**: Code runs in sandboxed environment - frappe module not available
4. **Performance**: Avoid heavy computations that might timeout
5. **Return Values**: Always return something meaningful for debugging
6. **Variable Updates**: Remember to update `variables` dictionary for persistence
7. **Documentation**: Comment your code for maintainability
8. **Testing**: Test with various input scenarios including empty/None values

## Related Nodes
- **Variable Filter**: For conditional branching with Python expressions
- **Frappe Exec Dotted Path**: For calling specific Frappe functions with full access
- **Data to Doc**: For creating Frappe documents
- **Call API**: For making external API calls
- **Execute JavaScript**: For JavaScript-based transformations

## Technical Notes
- Runs in sandboxed environment using Frappe's `safe_exec`
- **No imports allowed** - Cannot use `import` or `from...import` statements
- `frappe` module is set to None for security reasons
- Limited to Python built-in functions only (no datetime, json, math, etc.)
- Variables must be accessed/modified via `variables` dictionary
- Code is wrapped in a function context automatically
- Errors are caught and logged in workflow history
- Memory and execution time limits apply
- For full Python capabilities with imports, use **Frappe Exec Dotted Path** node instead

---

**Last Updated:** August 30, 2024 at 08:32:51 PM

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/file_to_ocr.md
# File to OCR

## Overview
The File to OCR node extracts text from images and PDF files using Optical Character Recognition (OCR). It converts scanned documents, images with text, and non-searchable PDFs into machine-readable text for processing in workflows.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Utility
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Extract text from scanned documents
- Process image-based forms
- Convert PDFs to searchable text
- Read text from photos
- Digitize paper documents
- Extract data from receipts
- Process business cards
- Analyze document images

## Configuration Fields

### File Input

#### File Source
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Options**:
  - File path
  - File document
  - Variable containing file
  - URL
- **Default**: File path

#### File Path/URL
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Path, URL, or variable containing file
- **Supports**: Variables like {{file_path}}

### OCR Settings

#### OCR Language
- **Field Type**: Multi-select dropdown
- **Options**: Multiple language codes (eng, fra, deu, spa, etc.)
- **Default**: eng (English)
- **Description**: Languages to detect in document

#### OCR Engine
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Tesseract (open source)
  - Google Vision API
  - AWS Textract
  - Azure Computer Vision
- **Default**: Tesseract

#### Processing Mode
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Fast (lower accuracy)
  - Balanced
  - Accurate (slower)
- **Default**: Balanced

### Output Options

#### Output Format
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Plain text
  - Structured JSON
  - Markdown
  - HTML
- **Default**: Plain text

#### Preserve Layout
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Maintain original text layout

#### Confidence Threshold
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Range**: 0-100
- **Default**: 60
- **Description**: Minimum confidence for text recognition

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{input_file}}`: File to process
- `{{ocr_languages}}`: Dynamic language selection

### Output Variables
- `{{extracted_text}}`: Extracted text content
- `{{ocr_confidence}}`: Overall confidence score
- `{{text_blocks}}`: Structured text blocks (JSON format)
- `{{detected_languages}}`: Languages found in document
- `{{page_count}}`: Number of pages processed

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Invoice Processing
```
File Source: File document
OCR Language: eng
Output Format: Structured JSON
Extract: Invoice number, date, amount, vendor
```

### 2. ID Card Reading
```
File Source: Variable ({{uploaded_id}})
OCR Language: eng
Processing Mode: Accurate
Extract: Name, ID number, expiry date
```

### 3. Receipt Scanning
```
File Source: File path
Output Format: Structured JSON
Confidence Threshold: 70
Extract: Items, prices, total, date
```

### 4. Form Digitization
```
File Source: URL
Preserve Layout: true
OCR Language: eng, spa
Output Format: Structured JSON
```

### 5. Business Card Extraction
```
File Source: Variable
OCR Engine: Google Vision API
Output Format: Structured JSON
Extract: Name, company, email, phone
```

## Best Practices

1. **Image Quality**: Ensure good image quality for better accuracy
2. **Language Selection**: Select appropriate languages
3. **Preprocessing**: Consider image enhancement before OCR
4. **Validation**: Validate extracted data
5. **Error Handling**: Handle OCR failures gracefully
6. **Performance**: Balance accuracy vs speed needs
7. **Storage**: Clean up processed files
8. **Testing**: Test with various document types

## Supported File Types
- **Images**: JPEG, PNG, GIF, BMP, TIFF
- **Documents**: PDF, DJVU
- **Scanned Files**: Any image format with text
- **Multi-page**: PDF and TIFF with multiple pages

## Related Nodes
- **Create Print File**: Generate PDFs for OCR
- **Variable Filter**: Process extracted text
- **Data to Doc**: Save extracted data
- **Text Intent Detection**: Analyze extracted text
- **ChatGPT Message**: Process text with AI

## Technical Notes
- Supports multiple OCR engines
- Handles various image formats
- Preprocesses images for better accuracy
- Supports multi-page documents
- Detects text orientation
- Handles handwritten text (limited)
- Supports multiple languages simultaneously
- Provides confidence scores per word/line

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/frappe_exec_dotted_path.md
# Execute Frappe Function

## Overview
The Execute Frappe Function node executes Frappe framework functions using their dotted path notation. It provides access to any whitelisted Frappe function, enabling integration with core Frappe functionality and custom app methods.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Utility
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Call Frappe framework functions
- Execute custom app methods
- Access Frappe utilities
- Trigger server scripts
- Call API methods
- Execute business logic
- Access database functions
- Integrate with Frappe modules

## Configuration Fields

### Function Configuration

#### Function Path
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Dotted path to function
- **Format**: module.submodule.function
- **Example**: frappe.utils.now

#### Arguments
- **Field Type**: JSON/Key-value pairs
- **Description**: Function arguments
- **Format**: JSON object or key-value pairs
- **Supports**: Variables

#### Keyword Arguments
- **Field Type**: JSON field
- **Description**: Named arguments (kwargs)
- **Format**: {"key": "value"}
- **Supports**: Variables

### Execution Options

#### Run As
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Current user
  - System user
  - Specific user
- **Default**: Current user

#### Timeout
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Default**: 30 seconds
- **Description**: Maximum execution time

#### Error Handling
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Stop on error
  - Continue on error
  - Use fallback value
- **Default**: Stop on error

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- Can pass any workflow variable as argument
- `{{variable_name}}`: In arguments
- Supports complex objects

### Output Variables
- `{{function_result}}`: Return value from function
- `{{execution_status}}`: Success or error
- `{{error_message}}`: Error details if failed

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Get Current Time
```
Function: frappe.utils.now
No arguments needed
Returns: Current datetime
```

### 2. Send SMS
```
Function: frappe.core.doctype.sms_settings.sms_settings.send_sms
Arguments: [{{phone_numbers}}]
Kwargs: {"message": "{{sms_text}}"}
```

### 3. Get Document
```
Function: frappe.get_doc
Arguments: ["Customer", "{{customer_id}}"]
Returns: Document object
```

### 4. Run Report
```
Function: frappe.desk.query_report.run
Kwargs: {
  "report_name": "Sales Analytics",
  "filters": {{report_filters}}
}
```

### 5. Clear Cache
```
Function: frappe.clear_cache
Kwargs: {"user": "{{user_email}}"}
Clears user-specific cache
```

## Best Practices

1. **Whitelist Functions**: Ensure functions are whitelisted
2. **Parameter Validation**: Validate arguments before calling
3. **Error Handling**: Implement proper error handling
4. **Security**: Be cautious with user inputs
5. **Performance**: Consider function execution time
6. **Documentation**: Document custom function usage
7. **Testing**: Test with various input scenarios

## Related Nodes
- **Execute Python**: For inline Python code
- **Call API**: For HTTP API calls
- **Variable Filter**: For data preparation
- **Data to Doc**: For document operations

## Technical Notes
- Only whitelisted functions can be called
- Respects function permissions
- Handles return value serialization
- Supports async function calls
- Maintains execution context
- Logs all function calls
- Implements timeout mechanism
- Handles complex return types

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/manual_trigger.md
# Manual Trigger

## Overview
The Manual Trigger node allows users to manually start a workflow for a specific document. This is one of the most commonly used trigger types, enabling on-demand workflow execution through user action.

**Category**: Triggers
**Type**: Trigger Node
**Label**: Manual Trigger

## When to Use
Use Manual Trigger when you want to:
- Start a workflow from a button click on a document
- Allow users to initiate processes on-demand
- Create approval workflows that users can start manually
- Build document processing workflows that users control
- Test workflows during development

## Configuration Fields

### 1. Label
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: A descriptive label for this trigger that will appear in the workflow builder interface.

**Best Practices**:
- Use clear, action-oriented labels like "Start Approval Process" or "Send for Review"
- Keep labels concise but descriptive
- Include the document type if multiple triggers exist

**Example Values**:
- "Submit for Manager Approval"
- "Start Invoice Processing"
- "Request Quote Review"

### 2. Select Doctype
**Field Type**: Select (Dropdown)
**Required**: Yes
**Data Source**: All available DocTypes in the system

**Description**: Choose which document type this manual trigger will be available for.

**How it Works**:
- The dropdown fetches all DocTypes from your system
- Once selected, this trigger will appear as an action button on documents of this type
- Users with appropriate permissions can click the button to start the workflow

**Common DocTypes**:
- Sales Invoice
- Purchase Order
- Task
- Lead
- Opportunity
- Custom DocTypes specific to your business

**Important Notes**:
- The trigger will only appear on documents the user has read access to
- Consider document permissions when designing workflows
- The selected DocType determines what document data is available in the workflow

### 3. Identifier
**Field Type**: Text Field
**Required**: No
**Default**: Empty

**Description**: A unique identifier to distinguish between multiple manual triggers on the same DocType.

**When to Use**:
- When you have multiple manual triggers for the same DocType
- To create different workflows for different scenarios on the same document
- For role-based workflow variations

**Example Scenarios**:
1. **Multiple Approval Levels**:
   - Identifier: "manager_approval"
   - Identifier: "director_approval"
   - Identifier: "ceo_approval"

2. **Different Processing Types**:
   - Identifier: "standard_processing"
   - Identifier: "expedited_processing"
   - Identifier: "special_handling"

**Naming Conventions**:
- Use lowercase with underscores
- Be descriptive but concise
- Avoid special characters
- Consider prefixing with department or process type

### 4. Node Label (Hidden Field)
**Field Type**: Hidden
**Calculated**: Yes
**Formula**: `value = data.label`

**Description**: Automatically populated from the Label field for internal use.

**Note**: This field is not visible to users but is used internally for node identification.

## Variables Available in Workflow

When a Manual Trigger is activated, the following variables are automatically available in the workflow:

### Standard Variables

#### `doc`
**Type**: Object
**Description**: The complete document data that triggered the workflow

**Structure**:
```javascript
{
  "doctype": "Sales Invoice",
  "name": "SINV-0001",
  "customer": "ABC Company",
  "total": 5000,
  "status": "Draft",
  // All other document fields...
}
```

**Usage in Actions**:
- Reference with `{{doc.fieldname}}`
- Example: `{{doc.customer}}` returns "ABC Company"
- Nested fields: `{{doc.items.0.item_code}}` for first item

#### `event`
**Type**: String
**Value**: "manual_trigger"

**Description**: Identifies the trigger type for conditional logic.

**Usage**:
- Use in conditional nodes to handle different trigger types
- Useful for multi-trigger workflows

#### `trigger_user_id`
**Type**: String
**Description**: Email/ID of the user who clicked the trigger button

**Example**: "john.doe@company.com"

**Usage**:
- Send notifications to the triggering user
- Track who initiated the workflow
- Use for approval routing based on initiator

#### `workflow_name`
**Type**: String
**Description**: The name/ID of the current workflow instance

**Usage**:
- Reference in logs or notifications
- Create links back to the workflow
- Track workflow execution

#### `task_name`
**Type**: String
**Description**: The current task ID within the workflow

**Usage**:
- Debugging and tracking
- Creating unique identifiers
- Logging execution steps

## Setting Up Manual Trigger

### Step 1: Add to Workflow
1. Open the Funnel Builder
2. Drag "Manual Trigger" from the Triggers section
3. Drop it at the start of your workflow

### Step 2: Configure
1. **Set Label**: Enter a descriptive label
2. **Select DocType**: Choose the target document type
3. **Set Identifier** (optional): Add if multiple triggers needed
4. Click Submit to save configuration

### Step 3: Connect Actions
1. Connect the trigger to your first action node
2. The workflow will start from this action when triggered

### Step 4: Publish Workflow
1. Save and publish your workflow
2. The trigger button will appear on documents of the selected type

## User Experience

### For End Users
1. **Locating the Trigger**:
   - Open a document of the configured DocType
   - Look for a button with your trigger label
   - Usually appears in the document's action menu or toolbar

2. **Triggering the Workflow**:
   - Click the trigger button
   - Confirm the action if prompted
   - The workflow starts immediately

3. **Feedback**:
   - User typically sees a success message
   - The document may update to show workflow status
   - Notifications may be sent based on workflow design

### Permissions Required
- User must have read access to the document
- User must have permission to execute the funnel
- Additional workflow-specific permissions may apply

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Approval Workflows
**Scenario**: Submit expense reports for approval
**Setup**:
- DocType: "Expense Claim"
- Label: "Submit for Approval"
- Identifier: "expense_approval"

**Workflow**: Trigger → Check Amount → Route to Approver → Send Notification

### 2. Document Processing
**Scenario**: Convert quote to order
**Setup**:
- DocType: "Quotation"
- Label: "Convert to Sales Order"
- Identifier: "quote_to_order"

**Workflow**: Trigger → Validate Quote → Create Order → Update Status → Notify Sales

### 3. Communication Workflows
**Scenario**: Send invoice to customer
**Setup**:
- DocType: "Sales Invoice"
- Label: "Email to Customer"
- Identifier: "send_invoice"

**Workflow**: Trigger → Generate PDF → Compose Email → Send → Log Communication

### 4. Data Validation
**Scenario**: Validate and clean customer data
**Setup**:
- DocType: "Customer"
- Label: "Validate Information"
- Identifier: "data_validation"

**Workflow**: Trigger → Check Required Fields → Validate Email → Format Phone → Update Record

## Best Practices

### 1. Naming and Organization
- Use consistent naming patterns across triggers
- Group related triggers with prefixes
- Document trigger purpose in descriptions

### 2. User Interface
- Place trigger buttons logically on the document
- Use clear, action-oriented button labels
- Provide confirmation dialogs for critical actions

### 3. Error Handling
- Always include error handling nodes after the trigger
- Provide clear error messages to users
- Log failures for debugging

### 4. Performance
- Keep initial actions after trigger lightweight
- Use async processing for heavy operations
- Provide progress feedback for long-running workflows

### 5. Security
- Validate user permissions in the workflow
- Don't expose sensitive data in trigger labels
- Audit trail important trigger activations

## Troubleshooting

### Trigger Button Not Appearing
**Possible Causes**:
1. Workflow not published
2. User lacks permissions
3. Wrong DocType selected
4. Identifier mismatch

**Solutions**:
- Verify workflow is published and active
- Check user role permissions
- Confirm DocType matches document
- Review identifier configuration

### Workflow Not Starting
**Possible Causes**:
1. Connection issues between nodes
2. First action node has errors
3. Workflow execution queue is full

**Solutions**:
- Check node connections in builder
- Test first action node independently
- Monitor workflow execution logs
- Check system performance

### Variables Not Available
**Possible Causes**:
1. Incorrect variable syntax
2. Document not fully loaded
3. Field doesn't exist on DocType

**Solutions**:
- Use correct syntax: `{{doc.fieldname}}`
- Ensure document is saved before triggering
- Verify field names match DocType fields

## Advanced Usage

### Multiple Triggers on Same Document
```
Trigger 1: "Quick Approval" (identifier: "quick")
Trigger 2: "Full Review" (identifier: "full")
Trigger 3: "Escalate" (identifier: "escalate")
```

Each trigger can start different workflow paths while operating on the same document.

### Conditional Visibility
While the trigger configuration doesn't support conditions directly, you can:
1. Use client scripts to show/hide trigger buttons
2. Add validation in the first workflow node
3. Use role-based permissions to control access

### Trigger Chaining
One manual trigger can initiate a workflow that creates documents with their own manual triggers, creating a chain of user-initiated processes.

## Integration with Other Nodes

### Common Next Nodes
1. **Conditional Node**: Route based on document data
2. **Send Notification**: Alert relevant parties
3. **Update Document**: Change document status
4. **Create Task**: Generate follow-up tasks
5. **Send Email**: Communicate with stakeholders

### Data Flow Example
```
Manual Trigger (Sales Order)
    ↓ (doc, trigger_user_id)
Check Inventory
    ↓ (doc, inventory_status)
[If Available] → Confirm Order → Send Confirmation
[If Not Available] → Create Purchase Order → Notify Procurement
```

## Limitations

1. **Single Document**: Operates on one document at a time
2. **User Initiated**: Requires manual action, not automatic
3. **Synchronous Start**: Cannot be scheduled or delayed
4. **UI Dependent**: Requires user interface access

## Related Nodes

- **Document Event Trigger**: For automatic triggering on document events
- **Cron Trigger**: For scheduled workflow execution
- **Email Trigger**: For email-initiated workflows
- **On Funnel Trigger**: For workflow-to-workflow triggering

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/on_email_reply.md
# On Email Reply

## Overview
The On Email Reply node waits for an email reply and continues the workflow when received. It pauses workflow execution until the recipient responds to a sent email, enabling interactive email-based workflows and conversation handling.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Email
- **Type**: Awaiting Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Wait for customer responses
- Implement approval via email
- Collect feedback through email
- Handle email conversations
- Process email confirmations
- Implement email-based forms
- Create interactive email workflows
- Capture reply content for processing

## Configuration Fields

### Email Thread
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Email thread ID or message ID to monitor
- **Source**: From previous Send Mail node

### Timeout Settings

#### Wait Timeout
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Description**: Maximum time to wait for reply
- **Unit**: Hours/Days
- **Default**: 48 hours

#### Timeout Action
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Continue anyway
  - Stop workflow
  - Trigger timeout branch
- **Default**: Continue anyway

### Reply Filters

#### Expected Sender
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Only accept replies from specific sender

#### Subject Contains
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Required text in reply subject

#### Body Contains
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Required keywords in reply body

### Processing Options

#### Extract Reply Only
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Extract only new reply content (remove quoted text)

#### Parse Reply Format
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Plain text
  - Structured (JSON/XML)
  - Key-value pairs
- **Default**: Plain text

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{email_thread_id}}`: Thread to monitor
- `{{expected_sender}}`: Expected reply sender

### Output Variables
- `{{reply_received}}`: Boolean - whether reply was received
- `{{reply_from}}`: Sender of the reply
- `{{reply_subject}}`: Reply subject line
- `{{reply_body}}`: Reply body content
- `{{reply_date}}`: When reply was received
- `{{reply_attachments}}`: Any attachments in reply
- `{{timeout_occurred}}`: Whether timeout was reached

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Approval Workflow
```
Email Thread: {{approval_email_id}}
Expected Sender: {{approver_email}}
Body Contains: "APPROVED" or "REJECTED"
Timeout: 24 hours
```

### 2. Customer Feedback Collection
```
Email Thread: {{feedback_request_id}}
Wait Timeout: 72 hours
Extract Reply Only: true
Parse Format: Plain text
```

### 3. Order Confirmation
```
Email Thread: {{order_email_id}}
Body Contains: "CONFIRM"
Timeout: 4 hours
Timeout Action: Stop workflow
```

### 4. Support Ticket Update
```
Email Thread: {{ticket_email_thread}}
Extract Reply Only: true
Parse Format: Plain text
Continue on any reply
```

### 5. Email-based Form
```
Email Thread: {{form_email_id}}
Parse Format: Key-value pairs
Expected format: "Name: value"
```

## Best Practices

1. **Timeout Planning**: Set reasonable timeout periods
2. **Clear Instructions**: Send clear reply instructions
3. **Reply Parsing**: Handle various reply formats
4. **Error Handling**: Plan for no-reply scenarios
5. **Thread Tracking**: Maintain email thread integrity
6. **Spam Filtering**: Consider spam/auto-replies
7. **Multiple Recipients**: Handle reply-all scenarios
8. **Testing**: Test with different email clients

## Related Nodes
- **Send Mail**: To send initial email
- **Email Trigger**: For new email triggers
- **Reply to Mail**: To send responses
- **On Email Seen**: To track email opens
- **Variable Filter**: To parse reply content

## Technical Notes
- Monitors email account continuously
- Maintains thread context
- Handles email client variations
- Strips email signatures
- Removes quoted content
- Supports HTML and plain text
- Manages email encoding
- Preserves workflow state during wait

# On Email Seen

## Overview
The On Email Seen node triggers when an email is marked as seen or read. It enables tracking of email engagement, allowing workflows to respond when recipients open emails, useful for follow-ups and engagement analytics.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Email
- **Type**: Awaiting Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Track email open rates
- Trigger follow-ups on email opens
- Monitor engagement metrics
- Send notifications when emails are read
- Update CRM records on email views
- Implement read receipts
- Analyze email campaign effectiveness
- Trigger sales actions on email engagement

## Configuration Fields

### Email Tracking

#### Email to Track
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Email message ID to monitor
- **Source**: From Send Mail node

#### Tracking Method
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Pixel tracking
  - Read receipt
  - Mail client API
- **Default**: Pixel tracking

### Timeout Settings

#### Wait Timeout
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Default**: 72 hours
- **Description**: Maximum time to wait for email open

#### Timeout Action
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Continue anyway
  - Stop workflow
  - Trigger alternative action
- **Default**: Continue anyway

### Tracking Options

#### Track Multiple Opens
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Trigger on each open vs first open only

#### Track Location
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Capture geographic location if available

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{email_id}}`: Email to track
- `{{recipient_email}}`: Expected recipient

### Output Variables
- `{{email_opened}}`: Boolean - whether opened
- `{{open_timestamp}}`: When email was opened
- `{{open_count}}`: Number of times opened
- `{{device_info}}`: Device/client information
- `{{location}}`: Geographic location if available

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Sales Follow-up
```
Track: Quote email
When opened: Wait 2 hours
Then: Send follow-up call reminder
```

### 2. Marketing Analytics
```
Track: Campaign email
Capture: Open time, device, location
Update: Campaign metrics database
```

### 3. Document Confirmation
```
Track: Contract email
When opened: Log in CRM
Notify: Sales team of engagement
```

### 4. Support Verification
```
Track: Solution email
If not opened in 24 hours: Send SMS
Update: Ticket status
```

### 5. Newsletter Engagement
```
Track: Newsletter
Multiple opens: true
Segment: Engaged subscribers list
```

## Best Practices

1. **Privacy Compliance**: Follow email tracking regulations
2. **Transparent Tracking**: Inform recipients about tracking
3. **Fallback Plans**: Handle cases where tracking fails
4. **Client Compatibility**: Consider email clients that block tracking
5. **Performance**: Don't overload with tracking pixels
6. **Analytics**: Aggregate tracking data for insights
7. **Testing**: Test with various email clients

## Related Nodes
- **Send Mail**: To send tracked emails
- **Reply to Mail**: For follow-up responses
- **On Email Reply**: To track replies
- **Send Notification**: For engagement alerts

## Technical Notes
- Uses tracking pixels for open detection
- Some clients block tracking by default
- Read receipts require recipient consent
- Tracks user agent and IP information
- Handles multiple email clients
- Preserves user privacy settings
- Implements GDPR compliance
- Logs all tracking events

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/on_funnel_trigger.md
# On Funnel Trigger

## Overview
The On Funnel Trigger node triggers when another funnel workflow starts this funnel. It enables funnel chaining, workflow composition, and creation of reusable workflow components that can be called from other workflows.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Trigger
- **Type**: Trigger
- **Element Type**: Trigger

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Create reusable workflow components
- Chain multiple workflows together
- Build modular workflow architecture
- Implement sub-workflows
- Create workflow libraries
- Handle complex multi-step processes
- Share workflows between teams
- Implement workflow inheritance

## Configuration Fields

### Trigger Name
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Unique identifier for this trigger
- **Used By**: Other funnels reference this name

### Accept Parameters
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Accept variables from calling funnel

### Required Parameters
- **Field Type**: List field
- **Description**: List of required parameter names
- **Validation**: Ensures parameters are provided

### Parameter Validation
- **Field Type**: JSON field
- **Description**: Validation rules for parameters
- **Format**: JSON schema for validation

### Access Control
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Public (any funnel can trigger)
  - Private (only same workspace)
  - Restricted (specific funnels only)

### Allowed Funnels
- **Field Type**: Multi-select
- **Visible When**: Access Control = Restricted
- **Description**: Funnels allowed to trigger this

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
Variables passed from the calling funnel:
- `{{caller_funnel}}`: Name of calling funnel
- `{{caller_task}}`: Task ID of caller
- All passed parameters as variables

### Output Variables
Can return variables to calling funnel:
- Define return variables in configuration
- Values passed back on completion

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Email Template Workflow
```
Trigger Name: send_email_template
Required Parameters: recipient, subject, template_id
Use: Reusable email sending component
```

### 2. Data Validation Sub-workflow
```
Trigger Name: validate_customer_data
Required Parameters: customer_data
Return Variables: validation_result, error_messages
```

### 3. Approval Process Component
```
Trigger Name: approval_workflow
Required Parameters: document_type, document_id, approver
Access Control: Private
```

### 4. Notification Handler
```
Trigger Name: send_notifications
Accept Parameters: true
Parameter Validation: { "channels": ["email", "sms", "push"] }
```

### 5. Error Handler Workflow
```
Trigger Name: error_handler
Required Parameters: error_type, error_message, context
Access Control: Public
```

## Best Practices

1. **Naming Convention**: Use descriptive trigger names
2. **Parameter Documentation**: Document expected parameters
3. **Validation**: Validate all input parameters
4. **Error Handling**: Handle missing parameters gracefully
5. **Access Control**: Set appropriate access levels
6. **Version Control**: Version your reusable workflows
7. **Testing**: Test with various parameter combinations
8. **Return Values**: Define clear return contracts

## Related Nodes
- **Trigger Funnel**: To call other funnels
- **Manual Trigger**: For manual workflow start
- **Stop Current Workflow**: To return control
- **Variable Filter**: To process parameters
- **Wait for Nodes to Complete**: For synchronization

## Technical Notes
- Creates workflow dependency chain
- Maintains separate execution context
- Supports nested funnel calls
- Handles circular dependency detection
- Preserves caller context
- Supports async and sync execution
- Implements parameter passing by value
- Logs all inter-funnel communications

---

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/on_submit_assistant_trigger.md
# On Submit Assistant Trigger

## Overview
The On Submit Assistant Trigger node initiates ChatNext Assistant workflows when documents are submitted in Frappe. It provides context-aware assistance at the point of document submission, enabling automated post-submission processes and validations.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: ChatNext Assistant
- **Type**: Trigger
- **Element Type**: Trigger

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Trigger assistant on document submission
- Provide post-submission guidance
- Validate submitted documents
- Start approval workflows
- Send submission notifications
- Create follow-up tasks
- Generate submission reports
- Initiate document processing

## Configuration Fields

### Trigger Configuration

#### Doctype
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: Document type to monitor
- **Source**: Submittable doctypes only

#### Trigger Name
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Unique identifier for trigger

#### Show Assistant
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Modal dialog
  - Side panel
  - Notification
- **Default**: Modal dialog

### Conditions

#### Conditional Trigger
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Add conditions for triggering

#### Trigger Condition
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Visible When**: Conditional Trigger = true
- **Description**: Python expression to evaluate
- **Example**: doc.total_amount > 10000

### Assistant Options

#### Assistant Title
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Default**: "Document Submitted"
- **Description**: Title shown in assistant

#### Initial Message
- **Field Type**: Textarea
- **Description**: First message to display
- **Supports**: Variables from document

#### Auto-start
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Start conversation automatically

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
Available in assistant context:
- `{{doc}}`: Submitted document data
- `{{doctype}}`: Document type
- `{{doc_name}}`: Document name
- `{{user}}`: User who submitted
- `{{submission_time}}`: Timestamp

### Output Variables
- `{{assistant_session}}`: Session ID
- `{{user_responses}}`: Collected responses

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Order Submission
```
Doctype: Sales Order
Message: "Order {{name}} submitted successfully"
Actions: Send confirmation, Create delivery task
```

### 2. Expense Approval
```
Doctype: Expense Claim
Condition: doc.total_amount > 5000
Message: "High-value claim requires additional approval"
Route to approval workflow
```

### 3. Invoice Processing
```
Doctype: Sales Invoice
Message: "Invoice submitted. Send to customer?"
Options: Email now, Schedule, Skip
```

### 4. Quality Check
```
Doctype: Quality Inspection
Message: "Inspection complete. Next steps:"
Provide remediation guidance if failed
```

### 5. Contract Activation
```
Doctype: Contract
Message: "Contract activated. Set up recurring tasks?"
Create scheduled activities
```

## Best Practices

1. **User Context**: Provide relevant context
2. **Clear Actions**: Offer clear next steps
3. **Conditional Logic**: Use conditions wisely
4. **Performance**: Avoid heavy operations
5. **Error Handling**: Handle submission failures
6. **User Experience**: Don't block critical workflows
7. **Testing**: Test with various document states

## Related Nodes
- **ChatNext Assistant Message**: For follow-up messages
- **Document Event Trigger**: For other document events
- **ChatNext Assistant Multi Actions**: For action options
- **ChatNext Assistant Form Question**: For additional data

## Technical Notes
- Triggers after successful submission
- Has access to full document context
- Runs in user session context
- Can modify document post-submission
- Respects user permissions
- Handles bulk submissions
- Supports async operations
- Logs all interactions

---

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/on_whatsapp_reply.md
# On WhatsApp Reply

## Overview
The On WhatsApp Reply node waits for a WhatsApp message reply and continues the workflow when received. It pauses execution until the user responds to a previous message, enabling conversational workflows and interactive WhatsApp bots.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: WhatsApp Messages
- **Type**: Awaiting Action
- **Element Type**: Action
- **Is Chatbot Node**: Yes

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Wait for user responses
- Build conversational flows
- Implement interactive menus
- Collect free-form feedback
- Handle message threads
- Create Q&A sequences
- Process user confirmations
- Manage conversation state

## Configuration Fields

### Wait Configuration

#### Timeout
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Default**: 30 minutes
- **Description**: Maximum wait time for reply
- **Unit**: Minutes

#### Timeout Action
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Continue workflow
  - Stop workflow
  - Send reminder
- **Default**: Continue workflow

### Reply Handling

#### Accept Any Reply
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Accept any message as valid reply

#### Expected Pattern
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Visible When**: Accept Any Reply = false
- **Description**: Regex pattern to match

#### Case Sensitive
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Case-sensitive matching

### Output Configuration

#### Reply Variable
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Variable to store reply content

#### Extract Media
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Extract media from reply if present

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{conversation_id}}`: Current conversation context
- `{{expected_sender}}`: Specific sender to wait for

### Output Variables
- `{{reply_variable}}`: Reply message content
- `{{reply_type}}`: Type of reply (text/image/document)
- `{{reply_media}}`: Media attachment if present
- `{{reply_timestamp}}`: When reply was received
- `{{timeout_occurred}}`: Whether timeout was reached

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Confirmation Flow
```
Previous: "Confirm order? Reply YES or NO"
Wait for reply matching: YES|NO
Timeout: 10 minutes
Route based on response
```

### 2. Feedback Collection
```
Previous: "How was your experience?"
Accept Any Reply: true
Reply Variable: customer_feedback
Store feedback for analysis
```

### 3. Multi-step Form
```
Previous: "What's your address?"
Wait for any reply
Reply Variable: user_address
Continue to next question
```

### 4. Menu Navigation
```
Previous: "Reply 1 for Sales, 2 for Support"
Expected Pattern: ^[12]$
Route to appropriate department
```

### 5. Document Collection
```
Previous: "Please send your documents"
Extract Media: true
Wait for media message
Process received files
```

## Best Practices

1. **Clear Instructions**: Tell users what to reply
2. **Timeout Planning**: Set appropriate timeouts
3. **Reminder Messages**: Send reminders before timeout
4. **Pattern Design**: Use simple patterns for validation
5. **Error Handling**: Plan for unexpected replies
6. **Context Preservation**: Maintain conversation context
7. **Media Handling**: Check for media attachments
8. **Testing**: Test various reply scenarios

## Related Nodes
- **Send Message WhatsApp**: To send initial message
- **WhatsApp Trigger**: For new conversation triggers
- **WhatsApp Question Raw**: For structured questions
- **Variable Filter**: To process replies

## Technical Notes
- Maintains session state during wait
- Handles multiple message types
- Supports media attachments
- Preserves message threading
- Implements retry mechanisms
- Tracks conversation history
- Handles offline scenarios
- Supports quick replies

---

**Last Updated:** August 30, 2024 at 08:32:51 PM

# Personal WhatsApp Select Session

## Overview
The Personal WhatsApp Select Session node selects an active WhatsApp Web session for subsequent personal WhatsApp operations. It manages multiple WhatsApp connections and ensures the correct session is used.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Personal WhatsApp
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## Configuration Fields

### Session Selection
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: Available WhatsApp Web sessions
- **Options**: List of connected sessions

### Session Options
- **Create New**: Checkbox - Create new session if needed
- **Verify Status**: Checkbox - Check session is active
- **Reconnect**: Checkbox - Attempt reconnection if disconnected

## Variables Usage
### Output Variables
- `{{selected_session}}`: Active session ID
- `{{session_status}}`: Connection status
- `{{phone_number}}`: Associated phone number

## Common Use Cases
- Switch between multiple WhatsApp accounts
- Verify session before sending messages
- Manage personal and business accounts
- Handle session failures

## Technical Notes
- Manages WhatsApp Web connections
- Handles QR code authentication
- Maintains session persistence
- Supports multiple concurrent sessions

# Personal WhatsApp Trigger

## Overview
The Personal WhatsApp Trigger node initiates workflows from personal WhatsApp accounts using WhatsApp Web. It enables automation of personal WhatsApp conversations and message handling.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Personal WhatsApp
- **Type**: Trigger
- **Element Type**: Trigger

## Configuration Fields

### Trigger Configuration

#### Session Selection
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Description**: WhatsApp Web session to monitor
- **Source**: Connected WhatsApp sessions

#### Trigger Type
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Any message
  - Specific contact
  - Group message
  - Keyword match

#### Contact Filter
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Visible When**: Type = "Specific contact"
- **Description**: Phone number or contact name

#### Keyword Filter
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Visible When**: Type = "Keyword match"
- **Description**: Keywords to trigger on

## Variables Usage

### Output Variables
- `{{message_text}}`: Received message content
- `{{sender_number}}`: Sender's phone number
- `{{sender_name}}`: Sender's contact name
- `{{is_group}}`: Boolean for group message
- `{{group_name}}`: Group name if applicable
- `{{message_time}}`: Message timestamp

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Personal Assistant
Monitor messages for task keywords and create reminders

### 2. Auto-Response
Send automated responses when unavailable

### 3. Message Backup
Archive important messages automatically

### 4. Group Monitoring
Track specific group conversations

## Best Practices

1. **Session Management**: Keep WhatsApp Web connected
2. **Privacy**: Respect message privacy
3. **Rate Limiting**: Avoid spam-like behavior
4. **Error Handling**: Handle disconnections
5. **Testing**: Test with test contacts first

## Technical Notes
- Requires WhatsApp Web session
- Uses browser automation
- Maintains session persistence
- Handles reconnection automatically
- Supports media messages
- Monitors real-time messages

---

**Last Updated:** September 07, 2024 at 02:51:15 PM

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/reply_to_mail.md
# Reply to Email

## Overview
The Reply to Email node sends email replies within a workflow conversation thread. It maintains email context and threading, enabling automated email responses while preserving the conversation history.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Email
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Send automated email responses
- Maintain email conversation threads
- Reply to customer inquiries
- Send follow-up emails
- Acknowledge receipt of emails
- Provide automated support responses
- Continue email-based workflows
- Send status updates via email

## Configuration Fields

### Reply Configuration

#### Original Email
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Message ID or thread reference
- **Source**: From Email Trigger or previous email node

#### Reply Content
- **Field Type**: Rich text editor (required)
- **Description**: Email reply body
- **Supports**: HTML, variables, formatting

#### Subject Prefix
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Default**: "Re: "
- **Description**: Prefix for reply subject

### Recipients

#### Reply To All
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Include all original recipients

#### Additional Recipients
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Description**: Add CC/BCC recipients
- **Format**: Comma-separated emails

### Email Options

#### Include Original
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Quote original message in reply

#### Attachments
- **Field Type**: File selector
- **Description**: Files to attach to reply

#### Email Account
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Description**: Email account to send from
- **Default**: Default email account

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{original_email_id}}`: Thread to reply to
- `{{reply_content}}`: Dynamic reply content
- `{{attachment_paths}}`: File paths to attach

### Output Variables
- `{{reply_message_id}}`: Sent reply message ID
- `{{reply_status}}`: Delivery status
- `{{thread_id}}`: Email thread identifier

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Auto-Acknowledgment
```
Reply Content: "We received your request and will respond within 24 hours."
Include Original: true
Auto-send on email receipt
```

### 2. Support Response
```
Reply Content: "Your ticket #{{ticket_id}} has been created."
Attachments: Support documentation
Reply To All: false
```

### 3. Order Confirmation
```
Reply Content: "Order #{{order_number}} confirmed. Delivery in 3-5 days."
Include invoice attachment
Maintain thread context
```

### 4. Approval Response
```
Reply Content: "Your request has been {{approval_status}}."
Include Original: true
Add manager in CC
```

### 5. Information Request
```
Reply Content: "Please provide the following information: {{required_fields}}"
Include form attachment
Track reply thread
```

## Best Practices

1. **Thread Preservation**: Maintain email threading
2. **Clear Content**: Write clear, concise replies
3. **Context Inclusion**: Include relevant original content
4. **Signature**: Include appropriate email signature
5. **Attachments**: Verify attachment sizes
6. **Testing**: Test with various email clients
7. **Error Handling**: Handle delivery failures

## Related Nodes
- **Email Trigger**: To receive emails
- **On Email Reply**: To wait for responses
- **Send Mail**: For new email threads
- **On Email Seen**: To track email opens

## Technical Notes
- Preserves email headers for threading
- Supports HTML and plain text
- Handles email encoding properly
- Maintains Reply-To headers
- Supports inline images
- Manages email signatures
- Tracks delivery status
- Implements retry on failure

---

**Last Updated:** August 30, 2024 at 08:32:51 PM

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/send_bulk_mail.md
# Send Bulk Email

## Overview
The Send Bulk Email node sends emails to multiple recipients efficiently. It supports email templates, personalization, batch sending, and delivery tracking, making it ideal for newsletters, announcements, and mass communication campaigns.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Email
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Send newsletters to subscribers
- Deliver mass announcements
- Send marketing campaigns
- Distribute reports to multiple users
- Send event invitations
- Deliver system-wide notifications
- Process email lists
- Send personalized bulk messages

## Configuration Fields

### Recipients Configuration

#### Recipient List
- **Field Type**: Textarea/List (required)
- **Description**: List of email addresses
- **Format**: Comma-separated or one per line
- **Supports**: Variables like {{email_list}}

#### Recipient Source
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Direct list
  - Database query
  - CSV file
  - Variable
- **Default**: Direct list

### Email Content

#### Email Template
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Description**: Pre-defined email template
- **Optional**: Can use custom content instead

#### Subject
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Email subject line
- **Supports**: Personalization variables

#### Body
- **Field Type**: Rich text editor (required)
- **Description**: Email body content
- **Format**: HTML or plain text
- **Supports**: Variables and merge tags

### Sending Options

#### Batch Size
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Default**: 50
- **Description**: Emails per batch

#### Delay Between Batches
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Default**: 5 seconds
- **Description**: Pause between batches

#### Personalization
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Enable merge tags for personalization

#### Track Opens
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Track email opens

## Variables Usage

- `{{email_data}}`: Data for personalization
- `{{template_variables}}`: Template merge data

### Output Variables
- `{{sent_count}}`: Number of emails sent
- `{{failed_list}}`: Failed email addresses
- `{{delivery_report}}`: Detailed delivery status
- `{{batch_status}}`: Status per batch

## Common Use Cases

```
Recipients: {{subscriber_list}}
Template: Monthly Newsletter
Personalization: true
Track Opens: true
```

```

Track engagement metrics
```

```
Batch Size: 25
```

## Best Practices

1. **List Hygiene**: Clean email lists regularly

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/send_mail.md
# Send Mail

## Overview
The Send Mail node enables sending emails through funnel workflows with comprehensive configuration options including templates, attachments, and multi-recipient support. It integrates with Frappe's email system and supports both custom content and pre-defined templates.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Email
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Send notification emails to users
- Deliver automated reports or documents
- Send confirmation emails after form submissions
- Distribute documents with print attachments
- Send template-based communications
- Notify multiple recipients about workflow events
- Send personalized emails with dynamic content

## Configuration Fields

### Email Account Settings
- **Select Email Account**: Dropdown (optional)
  - Choose from configured email accounts
  - Leave blank to use default email account
  - Description: "Keep Blank to use default email account"

### Template Options
- **Select From Email Templates**: Checkbox
  - When enabled: Use pre-defined email templates
  - When disabled: Create custom email content

**When using templates:**
- **Select Email Template**: Dropdown (required when template option enabled)
  - Choose from available Email Template records
  - Automatically populates subject and content
- **Create New Email Template**: Button
  - Opens new Email Template form in separate window

**When not using templates:**
- **Email Subject**: Text field (required)
  - Subject line for the email
  - Supports variable interpolation
- **Email Content**: Markdown editor (required)
  - Rich text email content
  - Supports HTML and markdown formatting

### Recipient Configuration
- **Recipients**: Autocomplete field (required)
  - Comma-separated list of email addresses
  - Supports variable interpolation
  - Description: "Comma (,) seperated list of recepients (when this field is provided above field would be ignored)"

- **Sender User Id**: Autocomplete field (optional)
  - Specify sender user ID
  - Uses default if not specified

- **CC**: Autocomplete field (optional)
  - Carbon copy recipients
  - Comma-separated list
  - Description: "Comma (,) seperated list of recepients to keep in CC"

- **BCC**: Autocomplete field (optional)
  - Blind carbon copy recipients
  - Comma-separated list
  - Description: "Comma (,) seperated list of recepients to keep in BCC"

### Additional Options
- **Send to Assigned Users also**: Checkbox
  - When enabled: Also sends to users assigned to linked documents
  - Default: false

### Document Linking
- **Link Doctype**: Checkbox
  - Enable to link email with specific document

**When Link Doctype is enabled:**
- **Doc Type**: Dropdown (required)
  - Select document type to link with
- **Doc Name**: Text field (required)
  - Name of the document to link with

### Attachments
- **Attach Prints**: Checkbox
  - Enable to attach print formats of documents

**When Attach Prints is enabled:**
- **Attachments**: Edit grid for multiple attachments
  - **Doc Type**: Dropdown (required) - Document type for attachment
  - **Doc Name**: Text field (required) - Document name for attachment
  - **Use Variable for Print Format**: Checkbox
    - When enabled: Use variable for print format name
    - When disabled: Select from dropdown
  - **Variable Print Format**: Text field (when variable option enabled)
    - Default: `{{print}}`
  - **Print Format**: Dropdown (when variable option disabled)
    - Choose specific print format
    - Filters based on selected doctype
    - Description: "Keep empty to use default print format"

### Legacy Fields (deprecated)
- **Recipients (Legacy)**: Data grid (disabled)
  - Description: "This is no more supported use the new seperate field for recepient and CC"
  - Contains Recipient and CC fields
  - Automatically hidden when new recipient field is used

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{email_list}}`: Dynamic recipient list
- `{{subject_text}}`: Dynamic subject content
- `{{email_body}}`: Dynamic email content
- `{{document_name}}`: Document reference for linking
- `{{print_format_name}}`: Dynamic print format selection
- `{{sender_email}}`: Sender identification
- `{{cc_list}}`: Carbon copy recipients
- `{{bcc_list}}`: Blind carbon copy recipients

### Example Variable Usage
```
Recipients: {{customer_email}}, {{manager_email}}
Subject: Order Confirmation - {{order_number}}
Content: Dear {{customer_name}}, your order has been {{status}}.
Doc Name: {{order_doc_name}}
```

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Order Confirmation
```
Subject: Order Confirmation - {{order_number}}
Recipients: {{customer_email}}
Template: Order Confirmation Template
Attach Prints: true (Invoice print format)
```

### 2. Approval Notifications
```
Subject: Approval Required - {{document_title}}
Recipients: {{approver_emails}}
CC: {{requestor_email}}
Link Doctype: true
```

### 3. Report Distribution
```
Subject: Weekly Report - {{report_date}}
Recipients: {{management_emails}}
Attachments: Multiple reports with custom print formats
```

### 4. Status Updates
```
Subject: Status Update - {{project_name}}
Recipients: {{stakeholder_emails}}
Template: Status Update Template
```

## Best Practices

### Email Configuration
1. **Use Templates**: Create reusable templates for consistent messaging
2. **Default Account**: Configure a reliable default email account
3. **Error Handling**: Monitor email sending failures
4. **Rate Limits**: Be mindful of email service provider limits

### Recipient Management
1. **Validation**: Validate email addresses before sending
2. **Privacy**: Use BCC for bulk emails to protect recipient privacy
3. **Personalization**: Use variables for personalized content
4. **Opt-out**: Provide unsubscribe options for marketing emails

### Content Creation
1. **Clear Subjects**: Write descriptive, actionable subject lines
2. **Mobile Friendly**: Design content that works on mobile devices
3. **Variable Testing**: Test variable interpolation thoroughly
4. **HTML Formatting**: Use proper HTML structure for rich content

### Attachments
1. **File Size**: Monitor attachment file sizes
2. **Print Formats**: Test print formats before using in production
3. **Variable Formats**: Use variables for dynamic print format selection
4. **Security**: Ensure attached documents don't contain sensitive data

## Related Nodes
- **Send Bulk Mail**: For high-volume email campaigns
- **Reply to Mail**: For responding to incoming emails
- **Email Trigger**: For email-based workflow initiation
- **Create Print File**: For generating attachment documents
- **Data to Doc**: For creating email logs
- **Variable Filter**: For processing email data
- **Assistant Message**: For ChatNext Assistant notifications
- **Send Notification**: For system notifications

## Technical Notes
- Integrates with Frappe's Email Queue for reliable delivery
- Supports HTML and plain text email formats
- Automatically handles email template variable substitution
- Creates email communication records for tracking
- Supports email bounce handling and delivery status
- Respects email account authentication settings
- Handles attachment generation and optimization
- Supports internationalization for multi-language emails
- Implements proper error handling and retry mechanisms
- Logs all email activities for audit purposes

# Send Notification

## Overview
The Send Notification node sends system notifications to users through various channels including in-app notifications, push notifications, SMS, and system alerts. It provides a unified interface for multi-channel user communication.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Notification
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Alert users of important events
- Send system notifications
- Deliver push notifications
- Send SMS alerts
- Create in-app notifications
- Broadcast announcements
- Send reminder notifications
- Alert about workflow status

## Configuration Fields

### Notification Type
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Options**:
  - System (in-app)
  - Push notification
  - SMS
  - All channels
- **Default**: System

### Recipients
- **Field Type**: Multi-select/Text field (required)
- **Description**: User IDs, roles, or phone numbers
- **Format**: Comma-separated list
- **Supports**: Variables like {{user_list}}

### Message Content

#### Title
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Notification title/subject
- **Max Length**: 100 characters
- **Supports**: Variables

#### Message
- **Field Type**: Textarea (required)
- **Description**: Notification body
- **Max Length**: 500 characters
- **Supports**: Variables and formatting

#### Priority
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - High (immediate delivery)
  - Normal (standard delivery)
  - Low (batch delivery)
- **Default**: Normal

### Additional Options

#### Action URL
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Link to open on notification click
- **Format**: Relative or absolute URL

#### Icon
- **Field Type**: Select/Text field
- **Description**: Notification icon
- **Options**: Predefined icons or custom URL

#### Expires After
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Description**: Hours until notification expires
- **Default**: 24 hours

#### Track Delivery
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Track notification delivery status

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{recipient_list}}`: Dynamic recipient list
- `{{notification_data}}`: Data for message formatting
- `{{action_link}}`: Dynamic action URL

### Output Variables
- `{{notification_id}}`: Unique notification ID
- `{{delivery_status}}`: Delivery status per recipient
- `{{delivered_count}}`: Number of successful deliveries

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Order Status Update
```
Type: Push notification
Recipients: {{customer_id}}
Title: Order Status Update
Message: Your order #{{order_id}} is {{status}}

```

### 2. System Maintenance Alert
```

Title: Scheduled Maintenance
Message: System will be unavailable from {{start_time}}
Priority: High
```

### 3. Task Assignment
```
Type: System

Title: New Task Assigned
Message: You've been assigned: {{task_title}}
Action URL: /tasks/{{task_id}}
```

### 4. SMS Alert
```
Type: SMS
Recipients: {{phone_number}}
Message: OTP: {{otp_code}} Valid for 5 minutes
Priority: High
Expires After: 0.083 (5 minutes)
```

### 5. Approval Required
```
Type: Push notification
Recipients: {{approver_list}}
Title: Approval Required
Message: {{document_type}} needs your approval
Action URL: /approvals/pending
Priority: High
```

## Best Practices

1. **Message Clarity**: Keep messages concise and clear
2. **Priority Usage**: Use high priority sparingly
3. **Recipient Validation**: Verify recipient availability
4. **Channel Selection**: Choose appropriate channels
5. **Timing**: Consider timezone and working hours
6. **Personalization**: Use variables for personal touch
7. **Action Links**: Provide relevant action URLs
8. **Tracking**: Monitor delivery for critical notifications

- **ChatNext Assistant Message**: For in-app messages
