# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/send_template_whatsapp.md
# Send WhatsApp Template

## Overview
The Send WhatsApp Template node sends pre-approved WhatsApp template messages with support for file attachments. These templates must be approved by WhatsApp Business API and support variable substitution for personalized messaging while maintaining compliance. The node now supports sending media files (images, videos, audio, documents) alongside template messages.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: WhatsApp Messages
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Send business-initiated conversations
- Send notifications outside 24-hour window
- Send standardized messages with or without attachments
- Deliver transactional updates
- Send appointment reminders
- Provide order confirmations with documents
- Send OTP messages
- Deliver account alerts with media

## Configuration Fields

### WhatsApp Number
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Field Key**: `whatsapp_id`
- **Description**: Variable containing valid WhatsApp number
- **Format**: Phone number with or without country code
- **Validation**: Numeric only, 10-12 digits
- **Example**: `{{contact_phone}}` or `919876543210`

### Select WhatsApp Template
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Field Key**: `whatsapp_template`
- **Description**: Pre-approved WhatsApp template to use
- **Source**: WhatsApp Business API templates

### Template Preview
- **Field Type**: Textarea (optional)
- **Field Key**: `template_text`
- **Description**: Preview of the selected template
- **Purpose**: Display-only field for template content

### File Attachment
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Field Key**: `attachment_file`
- **Description**: Add only if required, LEAVE EMPTY if not required
- **Accepts**: File ID from Frappe or file path
- **Supports**: Variables with {{}} syntax
- **Example**: `{{file_id}}` or `/files/document.pdf`
- **Media Types Supported**:
  - Images: jpg, jpeg, png, gif, webp
  - Videos: mp4, avi, mov, wmv, flv, mkv
  - Audio: mp3, wav, ogg, m4a, aac
  - Documents: All other file types

### Template Parameters
- **Field Type**: Data grid (optional)
- **Field Key**: `whatsapp_template_parameters`
- **Description**: Parameters to fill template placeholders
- **Format**: Array of parameter objects
- **Supports**: Variables with {{}} syntax
- **Example**: `[{"parameter": "{{customer_name}}"}, {"parameter": "{{order_id}}"}]`

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{whatsapp_id}}`: Recipient WhatsApp number (via whatsapp_id field)
- `{{attachment_file}}`: Optional file ID or path for attachment
- Template parameter variables used in `whatsapp_template_parameters`
- Any variables used for dynamic field values

### Output Variables
- `{{sent_templated_message}}`: Complete message object with all details
- `{{contact_name}}`: Frappe contact name for the WhatsApp number

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Order Confirmation with Invoice
```
Template: order_confirmation
Attachment: {{invoice_file_id}}
Parameters:
  - {{customer_name}}
  - {{order_number}}
  - {{delivery_date}}
```

### 2. Appointment Reminder
```
Template: appointment_reminder
Parameters:
  - {{patient_name}}
  - {{appointment_time}}
  - {{doctor_name}}
```

### 3. Document Delivery
```
Template: document_delivery
Attachment: /files/report.pdf
Parameters:
  - {{recipient_name}}
  - {{document_type}}
```

### 4. Product Catalog with Image
```
Template: product_showcase
Attachment: {{product_image_url}}
Parameters:
  - {{product_name}}
  - {{price}}
  - {{availability}}
```

## Best Practices

1. **Template Approval**: Ensure templates are approved by WhatsApp
2. **Variable Validation**: Verify all required parameters are provided
3. **Phone Number Format**: Accepts 10-12 digit numbers (with or without country code)
4. **File Attachments**: Leave attachment field empty if not needed
5. **Media Type Detection**: File type is automatically detected based on extension
6. **Error Handling**: Validates phone numbers before sending
7. **Compliance**: Follow WhatsApp Business policies

## Technical Notes
- Requires WhatsApp Business API setup
- Templates must be pre-approved by Meta
- Supports rich media templates with attachments
- Automatically detects media type from file extension
- Handles both Frappe file IDs and direct file paths
- Phone validation: Numeric only, 10-12 digits
- Auto-adds country code (91) for 10-digit numbers
- Creates or retrieves WhatsApp contact automatically
- Returns complete message object and contact name

---

**Last Updated:** September 18, 2025 at 10:24:21 PM

# Send WhatsApp Video

## Overview

- **Description**: Video caption

### Recipient
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: WhatsApp number

### Compression

- **Default**: Auto

## Common Use Cases

- Tutorial videos

- Video messages
- Promotional content

## Technical Notes

- Auto-compresses large videos

# Split Array

## Overview
The Split Array node splits an array variable into individual items for parallel processing. It creates child tasks for each array element, enabling batch operations and parallel workflow execution.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Utility
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Process array items in parallel
- Perform batch operations
- Send individual messages to multiple recipients
- Process list of records separately
- Create multiple documents from array
- Distribute workload
- Implement map-reduce patterns

## Configuration Fields

### Array Variable
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Name of array variable to split
- **Format**: Variable name without {{}}

### Item Variable
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Description**: Variable name for each item
- **Default**: "item"

### Index Variable
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Variable name for item index
- **Default**: "index"

### Parallel Processing
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Process items in parallel vs sequential

### Batch Size
- **Field Type**: Number
- **Description**: Number of items to process per batch
- **Default**: No limit

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{array_variable}}`: The array to split
- Must be valid array/list

### Output Variables
For each iteration:
- `{{item}}`: Current array element
- `{{index}}`: Current element index (0-based)
- `{{array_length}}`: Total array size

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Send Emails to Multiple Recipients
```
Array Variable: email_list
Item Variable: recipient_email
Use: Send individual personalized emails
```

### 2. Process Order Items
```
Array Variable: order_items
Item Variable: current_item
Use: Calculate pricing for each item
```

### 3. Bulk Document Creation
```
Array Variable: records_to_create
Item Variable: record_data
Use: Create documents from each record
```

## Best Practices

1. **Array Validation**: Ensure variable is an array
2. **Error Handling**: Handle empty arrays
3. **Performance**: Consider batch size for large arrays
4. **Parallel Limits**: Limit parallel execution for resource-intensive operations
5. **Variable Naming**: Use descriptive variable names
6. **Testing**: Test with different array sizes

## Technical Notes
- Creates separate workflow branches for each item
- Supports nested arrays
- Handles various data types as array elements
- Maintains workflow context for each branch
- Error in one branch doesn't affect others
- Supports array modification during iteration

# Stop Current Workflow

## Overview
The Stop Current Workflow node terminates the current workflow execution. It can be used conditionally to exit workflows based on specific criteria, preventing further processing and resource consumption.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Control Flow
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Exit workflow on error conditions
- Stop processing based on validation
- Terminate on specific business rules
- Cancel workflow on user request
- Prevent further execution after success
- Implement circuit breaker patterns
- Stop on quota limits
- Exit after achieving goal

## Configuration Fields

### Stop Reason
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Reason for stopping workflow
- **Logged**: Stored in workflow history

### Stop Status
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Completed (successful termination)
  - Cancelled (user cancellation)
  - Failed (error termination)
- **Default**: Completed

### Conditional Stop
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Description**: Stop based on condition
- **Default**: false

### Stop Condition
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Visible When**: Conditional Stop = true
- **Description**: JavaScript expression to evaluate

### Final Message
- **Field Type**: Textarea
- **Description**: Message to log on stop
- **Supports**: Variables

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- All workflow variables accessible for conditions
- Can reference any workflow state

### Output Variables
- No new variables (workflow ends)
- Final state preserved in logs

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Validation Failure
```
Stop Reason: "Invalid input data"
Stop Status: Failed
Condition: {{validation_result}} === false
```

### 2. Success Termination
```
Stop Reason: "Process completed successfully"
Stop Status: Completed
Final Message: "Order {{order_id}} processed"
```

### 3. Quota Exceeded
```
Stop Reason: "Daily limit reached"
Stop Status: Cancelled
Condition: {{daily_count}} >= {{max_limit}}
```

### 4. User Cancellation
```
Stop Reason: "Cancelled by user request"
Stop Status: Cancelled
Condition: {{user_action}} === "cancel"
```

### 5. Error Circuit Breaker
```
Stop Reason: "Too many errors"
Stop Status: Failed
Condition: {{error_count}} > 5
```

## Best Practices

1. **Clear Reasons**: Provide descriptive stop reasons
2. **Appropriate Status**: Use correct status for termination type
3. **Logging**: Include relevant data in final message
4. **Conditional Logic**: Test conditions thoroughly
5. **Cleanup**: Ensure resources are released
6. **User Feedback**: Inform users of termination
7. **Error Handling**: Distinguish between error and normal stops

## Related Nodes
- **Wait Node**: For pausing execution
- **Variable Filter**: For condition evaluation
- **Trigger Funnel**: For starting alternative workflows
- **Send Notification**: For stop notifications

## Technical Notes
- Immediately terminates workflow execution
- Preserves workflow state up to stop point
- Triggers workflow completion handlers
- Releases all held resources
- Logs termination in workflow history
- Can trigger cleanup operations
- Supports graceful shutdown

---

# Text Intent Detection

## Overview

- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Classify customer inquiries

- **Supports**: Variables like {{user_message}}

- **Field Type**: List/Multi-select
- **Description**: Predefined intent categories
- **Examples**:
  - Sales Inquiry
  - Technical Support
  - Billing Question
  - Complaint
  - Feedback

### NLP Model
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - Default classifier
  - Custom trained model
  - GPT-based classification
- **Default**: Default classifier

### Confidence Threshold
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Range**: 0-1
- **Default**: 0.7
- **Description**: Minimum confidence for intent detection

### Detect Multiple Intents
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Allow multiple intent detection

### Include Entities
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: true
- **Description**: Extract entities from text

## Variables Usage

- `{{text_input}}`: Text to analyze

### Output Variables

- `{{all_intents}}`: All detected intents with scores

```
Return relevant answer based on intent
```

```
Categories: Urgent, Normal, Spam, Newsletter
Route to appropriate folder
```

## Best Practices

2. **Category Definition**: Define clear, distinct categories
3. **Threshold Tuning**: Adjust confidence threshold based on use case
4. **Fallback Handling**: Handle low-confidence detections
5. **Context Usage**: Provide context for better accuracy
6. **Testing**: Test with various text inputs
7. **Monitoring**: Monitor and improve classification over time

## Technical Notes

- Supports multiple languages
- Can be trained on custom data
- Handles various text formats
- Provides confidence scores
- Extracts named entities
- Supports real-time processing
- Integrates with various NLP services

# Update Document


# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/var_filter.md
# Filter Data

## Overview
The Filter Data node is a conditional branching node that evaluates data against specified criteria and routes the workflow to different paths based on the result. It supports both Python expressions and configurable filter conditions to make decisions in your workflow.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Utilities
- **Type**: Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Route workflow based on conditions
- Implement if-then logic in workflows
- Validate data before processing
- Check variable values against criteria
- Create conditional branches
- Implement business rules
- Filter records based on multiple conditions
- Apply conditional logic to variables
- Perform calculations for decision making
- Validate and clean data before branching

## Configuration Fields

### Node Label
- **Field Type**: Text field (optional)
- **Description**: Custom label for the node in the workflow

### Use Python Expression
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Description**: Toggle between Python expression or filter conditions
- **Default**: False

### Python Expression
- **Field Type**: Code editor (required when checkbox is true)
- **Language**: Python
- **Description**: Python code that returns True or False
- **Note**: Variables are accessible via `variables` dictionary
- **Default**: `return True`
- **Visibility**: Only shown when "Use Python Expression" is checked

### Filters
- **Field Type**: Edit grid (required when checkbox is false)
- **Description**: List of filter conditions (evaluated with AND logic)
- **Visibility**: Only shown when "Use Python Expression" is unchecked
- **Components**:
  - **Variable**: Text field with autocomplete for workflow variables
  - **Match**: Select dropdown with options:
    - `equals`: Variable equals value exactly
    - `not_equals`: Variable does not equal value
    - `contains`: Variable contains value
    - `not_contains`: Variable does not contain value
    - `regex`: Variable matches regex pattern
  - **Value**: Text field for comparison value (supports variables)

## Output Handles

This node has **two output handles** for branching:
- **Yes Handle** (bottom): Workflow continues here if conditions pass
- **No Handle** (right): Workflow continues here if conditions fail

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- All workflow variables are accessible
- In Python mode: Access via `variables` dictionary
- In Filter mode: Use `{{variable_name}}` syntax

### Output Variables
- No new variables created
- Returns routing decision via `source_handle_id`

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Python Expression Mode
```python
# Check if amount is above threshold
return variables.get('amount', 0) > 1000

# Check multiple conditions
return variables.get('status') == 'active' and variables.get('verified') == True

# Complex validation
user = variables.get('user', {})
return user.get('age', 0) >= 18 and user.get('country') in ['US', 'CA', 'UK']

# String manipulation check
email = variables.get('email', '')
return email.endswith('@company.com') and len(email) > 5

# Date string validation (no imports allowed)
date_str = variables.get('date_string', '')
# Basic date format check without datetime module
parts = date_str.split('-') if date_str else []
if len(parts) == 3:
    year, month, day = parts
    return len(year) == 4 and 1  0

# Calculations for routing
total = sum([item.get('amount', 0) for item in variables.get('items', [])])
return total > variables.get('threshold', 1000)
```

### 2. Filter Conditions Mode
```
Variable: {{customer_status}}
Match: equals
Value: premium
---
Variable: {{order_amount}}
Match: contains
Value: 100
---
Variable: {{email}}
Match: regex
Value: ^[a-zA-Z0-9._%+-]+@company\.com$
```

### 3. Workflow Routing Examples
- Route premium customers to special handling
- Skip steps if data is incomplete
- Send to error handling if validation fails
- Branch based on user preferences
- Implement A/B testing logic

## Best Practices

1. **Python Expression**:
   - Always return boolean (True/False)
   - Use `.get()` to handle missing variables
   - Keep expressions simple and readable
   - Add defensive checks for None values
   - Use try-catch for robust error handling
   - Validate inputs before processing
   - Avoid complex operations on large datasets

2. **Filter Conditions**:
   - All conditions must pass (AND logic)
   - Use regex for complex pattern matching
   - Test with edge cases and empty values
   - Consider case sensitivity in comparisons
   - Check variable types to prevent errors

3. **Workflow Design**:
   - Label both output paths clearly
   - Handle both Yes and No branches
   - Document decision logic
   - Consider error scenarios
   - Test with various input scenarios

4. **Performance**:
   - Keep Python expressions efficient
   - Avoid heavy computations in filters
   - Consider caching complex calculations

5. **Security**:
   - Never execute user input directly
   - Validate all external data
   - Be cautious with regex patterns

## Related Nodes
- **Execute Python**: For complex data processing and transformations
- **Switch**: For multiple branch conditions
- **Loop**: For iterative processing
- **Merge**: To rejoin branched paths
- **Data to Doc**: For document creation from variables
- **ChatGPT Message**: For AI-based transformations
- **Text Intent Detection**: For NLP-based filtering
- **Split Array**: For array processing

## Technical Notes
- Python expressions run in sandboxed environment using Frappe's `safe_exec`
- **No imports allowed** - Cannot import any modules (datetime, json, etc.)
- Filters are evaluated sequentially (stops at first failure)
- String comparisons are case-sensitive
- Regex uses Python's `re.compile()` and `.match()`
- Empty/None values are converted to strings for comparison
- All filter conditions must pass for "yes" branch (AND logic)
- `frappe` module is disabled in Python expressions for security
- Variables persist across workflow execution
- Error messages included in workflow logs
- Automatic type conversion where applicable
- The node returns `source_handle_id` as either "yes" or "no"
- Supports Python built-in functions and operations only

---

**Last Updated:** January 23, 2025 at 06:22:14 PM

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/wait_for_nodes_to_complete.md
# Wait for Nodes to Complete

## Overview
The Wait for Nodes to Complete node synchronizes parallel workflow branches by waiting for specific nodes or branches to complete before continuing. It enables coordination between parallel executions and ensures proper sequencing of dependent operations.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Control Flow
- **Type**: Awaiting Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Synchronize parallel workflow branches
- Wait for multiple operations to complete
- Aggregate results from parallel tasks
- Ensure prerequisites are met
- Coordinate batch processing
- Implement fork-join patterns
- Wait for async operations
- Control workflow dependencies

## Configuration Fields

### Nodes to Wait For
- **Field Type**: Multi-select/List (required)
- **Description**: Node IDs or branch names to wait for
- **Format**: Comma-separated list

### Wait Mode
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown
- **Options**:
  - All (wait for all nodes)
  - Any (continue when any completes)
  - Minimum count
- **Default**: All

### Minimum Count
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Visible When**: Wait Mode = "Minimum count"
- **Description**: Minimum nodes that must complete

### Timeout
- **Field Type**: Number field
- **Default**: No timeout
- **Description**: Maximum wait time (minutes)

### Include Failed Nodes
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Count failed nodes as completed

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{node_list}}`: Dynamic list of nodes to wait for
- `{{branch_ids}}`: Parallel branch identifiers

### Output Variables
- `{{completed_nodes}}`: List of completed node IDs
- `{{failed_nodes}}`: List of failed node IDs
- `{{completion_count}}`: Number of completed nodes
- `{{aggregated_results}}`: Combined results from all nodes

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Parallel Data Processing
```
Wait for: data_processor_1, data_processor_2, data_processor_3
Wait Mode: All
Aggregate results after all complete
```

### 2. Multi-Channel Notifications
```
Wait for: email_sender, sms_sender, push_sender
Wait Mode: Any
Continue when first notification delivered
```

### 3. Batch Import
```
Wait for: All import_batch_* nodes
Include Failed: false
Process successful imports only
```

### 4. Approval Collection
```
Wait for: manager_approval, finance_approval
Wait Mode: All
Proceed only with all approvals
```

### 5. Resource Preparation
```
Wait for: file_download, database_setup, cache_warm
Timeout: 10 minutes
Ensure resources ready before proceeding
```

## Best Practices

1. **Clear Dependencies**: Define clear node dependencies
2. **Timeout Setting**: Set reasonable timeouts
3. **Error Handling**: Plan for partial completions
4. **Resource Management**: Consider resource usage
5. **Deadlock Prevention**: Avoid circular dependencies
6. **Testing**: Test with various completion scenarios
7. **Documentation**: Document why synchronization is needed

## Related Nodes
- **Split Array**: For creating parallel branches
- **Wait Node**: For time-based waits
- **Stop Current Workflow**: For early termination
- **Trigger Funnel**: For parallel workflow execution

## Technical Notes
- Monitors workflow execution state
- Handles node failure scenarios
- Preserves branch isolation
- Aggregates variables from branches
- Implements deadlock detection
- Supports nested parallelism
- Maintains execution order
- Logs synchronization points

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/wait_node.md
# Wait Node

## Overview
The Wait Node pauses workflow execution for a specified duration. It's useful for implementing delays, rate limiting, scheduling gaps between operations, and giving time for external processes to complete.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Control Flow
- **Type**: Awaiting Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Add delays between API calls (rate limiting)
- Wait for external processes to complete
- Schedule time gaps in workflows
- Implement retry delays
- Create timed sequences
- Pace message sending
- Allow time for data propagation
- Implement cooling-off periods

## Configuration Fields

### Wait Duration
- **Field Type**: Number field (required)
- **Description**: Duration to wait
- **Unit**: Defined by time unit field

### Time Unit
- **Field Type**: Select dropdown (required)
- **Options**:
  - Seconds
  - Minutes
  - Hours
  - Days
- **Default**: Seconds

### Dynamic Wait
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Description**: Use variable for wait time
- **Default**: false

### Wait Variable
- **Field Type**: Text field
- **Visible When**: Dynamic Wait = true
- **Description**: Variable containing wait duration

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{wait_duration}}`: When using dynamic wait
- All variables remain accessible during wait

### Output Variables
- No new variables created
- Workflow context preserved during wait

## Common Use Cases

### 1. API Rate Limiting
```
Wait Duration: 1
Time Unit: Seconds
Purpose: Prevent API rate limit violations
```

### 2. Email Sequence Delay
```
Wait Duration: 24
Time Unit: Hours
Purpose: Space out email campaign messages
```

### 3. Retry Delay
```
Wait Duration: 5
Time Unit: Minutes
Purpose: Wait before retrying failed operation
```

### 4. Business Hours Wait
```
Dynamic Wait: true
Wait Variable: {{hours_until_business_open}}
Purpose: Wait until business hours
```

### 5. Data Processing Gap
```
Wait Duration: 30
Time Unit: Seconds
Purpose: Allow time for database replication
```

## Best Practices

1. **Appropriate Durations**: Use reasonable wait times
2. **Dynamic Waits**: Use variables for flexible delays
3. **Error Handling**: Consider timeout implications
4. **Performance**: Long waits consume resources
5. **User Experience**: Inform users of delays
6. **Testing**: Test with different durations
7. **Documentation**: Document why wait is needed

## Related Nodes
- **Wait Till Date**: For waiting until specific time
- **Wait for Nodes to Complete**: For synchronization
- **Cron Node**: For scheduled execution
- **Stop Current Workflow**: For conditional termination

## Technical Notes
- Workflow remains in "waiting" state
- Resources are released during wait
- Wait is persistent across server restarts
- Maximum wait duration limits apply
- Precision depends on system scheduler
- Can be cancelled during wait
- Maintains exact wait duration

---

**Last Updated:** December 02, 2024 at 01:58:43 PM

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/wait_till_date.md
# Wait Till Date

## Overview
The Wait Till Date node pauses workflow execution until a specific date and time. It enables scheduled workflow resumption, deadline-based processing, and time-sensitive automation.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: Control Flow
- **Type**: Awaiting Action
- **Element Type**: Action

## When to Use It
Use this node when you need to:
- Schedule workflow resumption
- Wait for specific dates/times
- Implement deadline logic
- Create scheduled reminders
- Delay until business hours
- Wait for month/year end
- Schedule batch processing
- Implement time-based triggers

## Configuration Fields

### Target Date
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Key**: `target_date`
- **Description**: Date string must match YYYY-MM-DD format otherwise it will fail
- **Format**: YYYY-MM-DD
- **Supports**: Variables like {{deadline_date}}

### Execute At
- **Field Type**: Text field (required)
- **Key**: `execute_at`
- **Description**: Time to execute on the target date
- **Format**: HH:MM format or variable

### Days
- **Field Type**: Number field (required)
- **Key**: `days`
- **Description**: Number of days to wait (alternative to specific date)

## Variables Usage

### Input Variables
- `{{target_date}}`: Specific date to wait until
- `{{calculated_date}}`: Dynamically calculated date

### Output Variables
- `{{actual_resume_time}}`: When workflow actually resumed
- `{{wait_duration}}`: Total time waited

## Common Use Cases

### 1. Scheduled Report
```
Wait Until: {{first_day_next_month}}
Purpose: Generate monthly report
```

### 2. Reminder System
```
Wait Until: {{appointment_date}} - 1 day
Purpose: Send appointment reminder
```

### 3. Business Hours Processing
```
Wait Until: {{next_business_day}} 09:00:00
Purpose: Process during business hours
```

### 4. Deadline Enforcement
```
Wait Until: {{task_deadline}}
Purpose: Trigger escalation if not complete
```

### 5. Scheduled Campaign
```
Wait Until: {{campaign_launch_date}}
Purpose: Start marketing campaign
```

## Best Practices

1. **Timezone Awareness**: Consider timezone differences
2. **Past Dates**: Handle dates already passed
3. **Long Waits**: Consider resource implications
4. **Cancellation**: Allow workflow cancellation during wait
5. **Precision**: Account for execution time variance
6. **Documentation**: Document wait purpose
7. **Testing**: Test with various date scenarios

## Related Nodes
- **Wait Node**: For duration-based waits
- **Cron Node**: For recurring schedules
- **Stop Current Workflow**: For cancellation

## Technical Notes
- Persists across server restarts
- Handles daylight saving changes
- Supports millisecond precision
- Releases resources during wait
- Can be cancelled while waiting
- Logs wait start and end times

---

**Last Updated:** August 30, 2024 at 08:32:50 PM

```
Max Turns: 15
```

```
Session tracking enabled
```

6. **Data Extraction**: Define what data to extract
7. **Testing**: Test with various scenarios

- Uses OpenAI or custom LLM APIs
- Maintains conversation context
- Supports multi-turn conversations
- Extracts structured data
- Handles multiple languages
- Implements token limits
- Provides conversation summaries
- Logs all interactions

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/nodes/whatsapp_trigger.md
# WhatsApp Trigger

## Overview
The WhatsApp Trigger node initiates workflows when WhatsApp messages are received. It can trigger on any message, first message only, or specific text patterns, enabling automated WhatsApp conversation flows and bot interactions.

## Category and Type
- **Category**: WhatsApp Messages
- **Type**: Trigger
- **Element Type**: Trigger
- **Is Chatbot Node**: Yes

## Configuration Fields

### Trigger On Any Message
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Default**: false
- **Description**: Trigger workflow on any incoming WhatsApp message

### Trigger On First Message Only
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Visible When**: Trigger On Any Message = true
- **Description**: Only trigger on the first message in a conversation

### Trigger On Text Match
- **Field Type**: Checkbox
- **Visible When**: Trigger On Any Message = false
- **Description**: Trigger only when message matches specific text

### Trigger Text
- **Field Type**: Text field (required when text match enabled)
- **Visible When**: Trigger On Text Match = true
- **Description**: Text to match (case insensitive)

## Created Variables
- `last_whatsapp_message`: Contains the message that triggered the workflow

## Best Practices
1. **Clear Configuration**: Set up node parameters clearly
2. **Error Handling**: Implement proper error handling
3. **Testing**: Test all workflow paths
4. **Documentation**: Document complex configurations
5. **Performance**: Consider performance implications

## Related Nodes
See other nodes in the same category for related functionality.

## Technical Notes
- Integrates with system APIs
- Handles asynchronous operations
- Maintains workflow state
- Supports error recovery
- Implements retry mechanisms

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/nextai/nextai/funnel/docs/triggers.md
# Funnel Trigger Nodes Documentation

## Overview

Triggers are the starting points of workflows in the Funnel system. They respond to various events and initiate workflow execution by creating tasks for connected action nodes.

## Trigger Types

### 1. Manual Trigger (`manual_trigger`)

**Purpose**: User-initiated workflow execution

**Location**: `triggers/on_manual_trigger.py`

**Function**: `trigger(trigger_name, doctype, doctype_name, variables=None)`

**Parameters**:
- `trigger_name`: Name of the Funnel Definition
- `doctype`: Target document type
- `doctype_name`: Target document name
- `variables`: Additional variables to pass to workflow

**Process**:
1. Validates user permissions via `can_execute()`
2. Creates Funnel Manual Execution Log
3. Creates new Funnel Workflow with variables
4. Creates tasks for connected action nodes
5. Queues task execution

**Variables Set**:
- `doc`: Document as dict
- `event`: "manual_trigger"
- `trigger_user_id`: User who triggered workflow
- Additional custom variables passed

---

### 2. Document Event Trigger (`document_event_trigger`)

**Purpose**: Responds to Frappe document lifecycle events

**Location**: `triggers/on_doctype_events.py`

**Events Supported**:
- `before_insert`
- `after_insert`
- `before_save`
- `after_save`
- `before_submit`
- `after_submit`
- `before_cancel`
- `after_cancel`
- `before_delete`
- `after_delete`
- `on_update`
- `on_change`
- `on_trash`

**Configuration**:
- Set in Funnel Definition with doctype and event type
- Can filter by document conditions

**Variables Set**:
- `doc`: Document triggering the event
- `event`: Event type (e.g., "after_save")
- `old_doc`: Previous document state (for update events)
- `trigger_user_id`: User who triggered the event

---

### 3. Cron Trigger (`cron_node`)

**Purpose**: Time-based scheduled workflow execution

**Location**: `triggers/on_cron_trigger.py`

**Scheduling Options**:
- Minute-based (every X minutes)
- Hourly
- Daily at specific time
- Weekly on specific days
- Monthly on specific dates
- Custom cron expressions

**Function**: `on_cron_trigger()`

**Process**:
1. Called by Frappe scheduler
2. Checks all active cron-based Funnel Definitions
3. Creates workflows for matching schedules
4. Queues task execution with high priority

**Variables Set**:
- `event`: "cron_trigger"
- `trigger_time`: Timestamp of trigger
- `schedule`: Cron schedule that triggered

---

### 4. Email Trigger (`email_trigger`)

**Purpose**: Triggered by incoming emails

**Location**: `triggers/email_trigger.py`

**Configuration**:
- Email account to monitor
- Sender filters
- Subject filters
- Body content filters

**Variables Set**:
- `email`: Email document as dict
- `sender`: Sender email address
- `subject`: Email subject
- `body`: Email body content
- `attachments`: List of attachments
- `event`: "email_trigger"

**Features**:
- Can process attachments
- Supports regex pattern matching
- Can trigger on specific email accounts

---

### 5. WhatsApp Trigger (`whatsapp_trigger`)

**Purpose**: Responds to WhatsApp Business API messages

**Location**: `triggers/whatsapp_trigger.py`

**Message Types**:
- Text messages
- Media messages (images, videos, documents)
- Location messages
- Contact cards
- Button responses
- List responses

**Variables Set**:
- `message`: Full message object
- `from`: Sender WhatsApp number
- `message_type`: Type of message
- `text`: Text content (if applicable)
- `media_url`: Media URL (if applicable)
- `event`: "whatsapp_trigger"
- `contact_name`: Associated contact

**Features**:
- Automatic contact creation/linking
- Media download and processing
- Message status tracking

---

### 6. Personal WhatsApp Trigger (`personal_whatsapp_trigger`)

**Purpose**: Integration with personal WhatsApp accounts

**Location**: `triggers/personal_whatsapp_trigger.py`

**Subtypes**:
- `personal_whatsapp_message_action_trigger`: Message-based triggers

**Requirements**:
- WhatsApp Web session
- QR code authentication
- Session management

**Variables Set**:
- `message`: Message content
- `from`: Sender number/name
- `chat_id`: Chat/Group ID
- `is_group`: Boolean for group messages
- `event`: "personal_whatsapp_trigger"

**Features**:
- Group message support
- Media handling
- Session persistence

---

### 7. ChatNext Assistant Trigger (`chatnext_assistant_trigger`)

**Purpose**: Triggered by ChatNext AI assistant interactions

**Location**: `triggers/chatnext_assistant_trigger.py`

**Trigger Events**:
- User queries
- Assistant responses
- Conversation milestones
- Error conditions

**Variables Set**:
- `query`: User query
- `context`: Conversation context
- `session_id`: Chat session ID
- `user_id`: User identifier
- `event`: "assistant_trigger"

**Features**:
- Context preservation
- Multi-turn conversation support
- Intent detection integration

---

### 8. ChatNext Assistant Public Trigger (`chatnext_assistant_public_trigger`)

**Purpose**: Public/unauthenticated ChatNext assistant interactions

**Location**: `triggers/chatnext_assistant_public_trigger.py`

**Differences from Regular Assistant Trigger**:
- No authentication required
- Limited context access
- Public data only
- Rate limiting applied

**Variables Set**:
- `query`: User query
- `session_id`: Anonymous session ID
- `event`: "assistant_public_trigger"
- `ip_address`: Client IP for rate limiting

---

### 9. Submit Assistant Trigger (`on_submit_assistant_trigger`)

**Purpose**: Triggered when forms are submitted through ChatNext assistant

**Location**: `triggers/on_submit_assistant_trigger.py`

**Form Types**:
- Custom forms
- Document creation forms
- Approval forms
- Survey forms

**Variables Set**:
- `form_data`: Submitted form data as dict
- `form_id`: Form identifier
- `user_id`: Submitting user
- `event`: "form_submit_trigger"
- `validation_status`: Form validation result

**Features**:
- Form validation
- File upload handling
- Multi-step form support

---

## Trigger Configuration

### Common Configuration Fields

All triggers share common configuration options:

1. **Name**: Unique trigger identifier
2. **Enabled**: Active/inactive status
3. **Priority**: Execution priority (0-2)
4. **Conditions**: Filter conditions for trigger activation
5. **Variables**: Default variables to set
6. **Error Handler**: Error flow configuration

### Trigger Registration

Triggers are registered in the Funnel system through:

1. **Funnel Definition**: Defines trigger configuration
2. **Funnel Published**: Active published version
3. **Funnel Node**: Node definition with FormIO schema

### Trigger Execution Flow

```
Event Occurs
    ↓
Check Active Triggers
    ↓
Evaluate Conditions
    ↓
Create Workflow
    ↓
Set Variables
    ↓
Create Initial Tasks
    ↓
Queue Execution
```

## Best Practices

### 1. Permission Checks
Always validate permissions before trigger execution:
```python
if not can_execute(published_funnel.funnel):
    raise frappe.PermissionError
```

### 2. Variable Initialization
Set all required variables at trigger time:
```python
variables = {
    "doc": doc.as_dict(),
    "event": "trigger_type",
    "trigger_user_id": frappe.session.user,
    **custom_variables
}
```

### 3. Error Handling
Implement robust error handling:
```python
try:
    # Trigger logic
except Exception as e:
    # Log error
    # Create error workflow if configured
    raise
```

### 4. Performance Optimization
- Use appropriate queue priorities
- Batch create tasks when possible
- Minimize database queries in triggers

### 5. Logging
Create execution logs for debugging:
```python
log_doc = frappe.get_doc({
    "doctype": "Funnel Manual Execution Logs",
    "time_and_date": frappe.utils.now_datetime(),
    "funnel_definition": trigger_name,
    "variables": json.dumps(variables),
    "funnel": published_funnel.name
})
log_doc.insert(ignore_permissions=True)
```

## Security Considerations

1. **Authentication**: Validate user permissions
2. **Input Validation**: Sanitize all trigger inputs
3. **Rate Limiting**: Implement for public triggers
4. **Session Management**: Use Funnel User for execution
5. **Data Access**: Respect document permissions

## Debugging Triggers

### 1. Check Trigger Registration
```python
# Verify trigger is active
trigger_doc = frappe.get_doc("Funnel Definition", trigger_name)
published = frappe.get_doc("Funnel Published", trigger_doc.parent)
```

### 2. Monitor Execution Logs
- Check `Funnel Manual Execution Logs`
- Review `Funnel Workflow` creation
- Track `Funnel Task` generation

### 3. Test Conditions
Verify trigger conditions are met:
- Document state matches
- User has permissions
- Time conditions satisfied
- External service available

## Advanced Features

### 1. Conditional Triggers
Use conditions to filter when triggers activate:
```python
if evaluate_conditions(trigger_conditions, variables):
    create_workflow()
```

### 2. Multi-Trigger Workflows
Workflows can have multiple trigger points:
- Different events start same workflow
- Merge points for parallel triggers
- Conditional trigger selection

### 3. Trigger Chaining
Triggers can initiate other triggers:
- Use `trigger_funnel` action
- Pass variables between triggers
- Create trigger cascades

### 4. External Integrations
Triggers can integrate with external services:
- Webhooks
- API callbacks
- Message queues
- Real-time events

## Common Issues and Solutions

### Issue: Trigger Not Firing
**Solutions**:
1. Check trigger is enabled
2. Verify conditions are met
3. Ensure user has permissions
4. Check scheduler is running (for cron)

### Issue: Duplicate Triggers
**Solutions**:
1. Implement idempotency checks
2. Use trigger deduplication
3. Add cooldown periods

### Issue: Performance Problems
**Solutions**:
1. Optimize condition evaluation
2. Use appropriate queue priorities
3. Batch task creation
4. Add caching where appropriate

---
